# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

## [Unreleased]

### Summary

The search for a project's `.moai/state` used to have no upper bound. It walked from the working directory toward the filesystem root and took the first `.moai/state` it found; anywhere below the home directory, that first hit is `~/.moai/state`. So a command run in a directory that was not a MoAI project at all resolved against a state directory the caller never named — and reported success. The same command run under `/tmp` failed with a clear error. The only difference between the two was whether the working directory sat below `$HOME`.

`moai clean` is the sharp end of it, because it deletes: the walk decided which `runs/` directory `os.RemoveAll` was pointed at.

The lookup now stops at the project root. The commands that read or append state share one resolution path with the project-root convention the rest of the codebase already used, which is where the home boundary comes from.

Six quality layers move into `moai-domain-svg-infographic` from a survey of `cathrynlavery/diagram-design` v2.6.1 (MIT), restated rather than copied.

One of the six was not a thinness but an outright defect: the skill had no accessibility contract at all. An SVG carries no accessible name of its own, so every diagram it emitted was announced as an unlabelled graphic — and a screen reader does not read the `<text>` inside a graphic it cannot name. Every label in every diagram the skill has ever produced was inert. That is the item with an executable guard, and it is the one that could regress silently once the prose was written, so it ships with fixtures that exercise the check in both directions rather than only the passing one.

The other five are rules the demo diagrams' look actually comes from — a semantic-role skin, connector geometry with numbers attached, a node budget, four output dials, and a list of the symptoms that mark a generated-looking schematic. The 39-type catalogue that makes up the bulk of the source was refused: it presupposes a skill that owns every diagram type, while this one routes most types to mermaid, so importing it would have brought a different skill's architecture along with the rules.

Absorbed text is restated, never lifted. The source permits external font loading and HTML output variants, both of which this skill forbids; its "never JetBrains Mono as a blanket dev font" rule is a case in point, since this skill uses JetBrains Mono as its mono role — the rule survives as its intent (mono is for ports, paths, and field types; names take the sans) with the font name dropped.

The mermaid routing table is unchanged and the bypass exception list is empty. One sample pair was rendered for `journey` and is committed as evidence; the carve-out it would have justified was declined, which is the outcome the acceptance criteria name as a pass.

A root `AGENTS.md` joins the repository as the standing contract for any agent harness, not only Claude's. The reason it is one file and not several is the failure mode: codex reads project instructions under a byte cap and drops the overflow with no warning, no stderr, and exit 0 — so a contract that does not fit is not reported missing, it is reported complete. The document is therefore self-sufficient, ordered most-critical-first, and held under a ceiling by a guard that fails the build. Making room for it took eleven always-loaded documents down to stubs against eight lazy companions; no obligation moved, only the prose explaining why each one exists.

### Added

- **[SPEC-CODEX-DUAL-AGENTS-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CODEX-DUAL-AGENTS-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The 11 retained MoAI agent definitions are now published in dual form — the existing `.claude/agents/moai/*.md` and a new codex-cli-consumable `.codex/agents/moai/*.toml` — generated deterministically from a single neutral source: the template `.md` frontmatter+body core.** A new `internal/template/agentemit` emitter package (loader → manifest → TOML writer, `agents-codex.yaml` manifest) drives the publication, and the 11 committed `.codex/agents/moai/*.toml` template artifacts ship in `internal/template/templates/` so `moai init`/`moai update` distributes them alongside the markdown set. Golden, embed, and deploy guards pin the emitted TOML against drift from the markdown source.
  - The `mcp_servers` field in the agent-TOML output is a `[mcp_servers.moai]` table (map shape), not an array — codex-cli rejects the array form. This was a run-phase measured correction (M3 `445bfd3b5` + M3b `e6c2239e5`, errata `a0a426d7c`), not an assumption: the array form failed real codex-cli parsing.
  - Run-phase per-milestone commits: M1 `7a7a05384` → M2 `abf08c1f0` → M3 `445bfd3b5` → M3b `e6c2239e5` → MS4 `48032316d` → errata `a0a426d7c` — factory lane, not pushed (the lead integrates). 13 acceptance criteria (verified count against `acceptance.md`), all covered by the run-phase §E.2/§E.3 evidence at HEAD `48032316d`. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition on `spec.md` (`status:` + `updated:` only), the `progress.md §E.4` audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (lightweight sync per lead scoping: no README/docs-site changes, which belong to the M-series design cards). `sync_commit_sha` populated as a `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder and backfilled in a follow-up commit. 🗿 MoAI
- Six mandatory connector rules in `references/authoring.md` section 2.5, each stated with the number a checker can assert rather than as a preference: rounded orthogonal bends at `r = 8`, a 6-10 unit gap between a label's mask and its own stroke, at least 12 units between any two connectors with a bridge at `rHop = 5` on a crossing, attach points fanned at `offset(k) = L * k / (N + 1)`, and no routing behind a non-endpoint box. That last rule carries its exception: where a cross-cutting node sits on the only direct orthogonal path, the stroke is dashed to signal transit, the label sits at the visible end, and no arrowhead lands on the intervening box. Stating it as an absolute would have made the sibling verifier flag every legitimate transit.
- A complexity budget: the node ceiling is the `detail` dial's, at most 12 connectors and at most 2 `accent` elements in every mode, with per-archetype ceilings at `references/archetypes.md`. Exceeding one means splitting into an overview and a detail diagram, never shrinking the boxes.
- Four output dials — format, size, detail, audience — each with a default, because a dial without one turns a four-part contract into four questions on every invocation.
- Fourteen slop symptoms in `SKILL.md` § Red Flags, each phrased as something to look for in the rendered image rather than as a matter of taste.
- `scripts/fixtures/a11y-present.svg` and `a11y-missing.svg`, which the accessibility check must pass and fail respectively. A check that only ever passes proves nothing.

- A root `AGENTS.md` — the standing contract every turn is bound by, regardless of which agent harness reads it. Codex loads project instructions from that filename under a byte cap and truncates the overflow **silently**; a rule cut mid-sentence is worse than an absent one, because it still reads as complete. The file is self-sufficient by construction: it assumes no other instruction document is loaded and no nested `AGENTS.md` exists anywhere in the repository, and its clauses are ordered most-critical-first so that what a truncation drops is what matters least. It ships to every project, and `CLAUDE.md` reaches it through the same `@`-import mechanism it already used for the config sections, so the contract is stated once and loaded once rather than duplicated inline.
- A byte guard on that contract layer. The ceiling is 24,576 B — the untrusted first session's effective cap of 32,768 B, less a reserve for the reader's own global `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` layer and for future growth. It binds the shipped mirror as well as the live file, because a mirror over the ceiling truncates on the reader's machine no matter what the repository's own copy measures. A breach **fails the build** rather than warning: truncation produces no signal of its own, so this guard is the only one there will ever be.
- The always-loaded budget guard now counts `AGENTS.md`. Without that, moving a clause out of a rule file and into the contract layer would have scored as a reduction while the loaded context stayed exactly the same — a diet that never happened, recorded as if it had.

- The deployed skill catalog is now reachable from `.agents/skills/` as well as `.claude/skills/`. Agent runtimes that scan the former — Codex CLI among them — see the skills MoAI-ADK installs with no manual wiring. Each entry is a relative symlink to the canonical directory, so there is still exactly one copy of every skill on disk.

  Claude Code's path is unchanged, and that is a property of the design rather than a claim to be tested for: the mirror is written beside the canonical tree and never into it. The deploy output was diffed against the pre-change commit with the mirror enabled — 262 entries, byte-identical.

  The template source tree carries no symlink and cannot: `//go:embed` drops symlinks, files and directories alike, with no error and no warning, so a link committed to the template would simply be missing from the binary. The link is created at deploy time instead, and a regression test fails if a symlink ever appears in the template tree.

  `.agents/skills/moai*` is added to the deployed `.gitignore`. The mirror is a build product. The `.agents/` root itself stays tracked, so entries you put there are unaffected — and an existing non-symlink entry at a mirror path is never removed or overwritten; the deployer skips it and leaves it alone.

  Two limits are worth knowing up front:

  - **Where symlinks are unavailable the mirror is a copy, and from the second deploy onward that copy is left as it is.** Windows without Developer Mode or elevated privileges is the usual case. The deployer cannot tell a copy it made itself from a directory you created, so it preserves both — the canonical skills refresh on each deploy and the copy does not. It goes stale. Refreshing copies, and removing mirrors of renamed or retired skills, is a deliberate scope split and lands in a follow-up change.
  - **The fallback warning does not currently reach you.** The deployer reports which mode it used, and any warning, through its return value rather than printing it, and the CLI does not yet read that seam. A deploy that falls back to copying is therefore silent today. Wiring the CLI to surface it is follow-up work.

### Changed

- Resolving a project's `.moai/state` no longer climbs past the project root. `moai state dump`, `moai state show-blocker`, `moai chain`, `moai tokens`, and `moai clean` stop at the directory that owns the project and fail there when it has no state directory, instead of continuing upward and succeeding somewhere else.
- `moai clean` deliberately does **not** read `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`; it anchors on the directory it runs in. The read-and-append commands above do read it. The asymmetry is intentional: the variable does not reliably track a worktree-resident session, and a delete should not inherit its target from the environment — that is the same "a directory the caller never named" hazard arriving through a different door. To clean another project, run the command inside it.
- The chain event ledger has one location: `<project>/.moai/state/chain`. It previously landed in `.moai/chain` when `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` was unset and in `.moai/state/chain` when it was set, so a single project could scatter its events across two files. A ledger sitting at the legacy `.moai/chain` is relocated once, automatically. Where both exist the legacy file is kept untouched and a warning naming its path is printed — the two are never merged. When a relocation cannot be completed, the command keeps using the legacy path and says so on stderr rather than losing events quietly.
- Every command that resolves a project's state now prints the project root it resolved, before acting on it; `moai clean` prints it before listing or deleting anything. Because `clean` anchors on the working directory while the readers honor `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`, the two can point at different projects within one session — inspect one project, clean another. The printed line is what makes that visible.
- A directory holding a bare `.moai` with no `state` inside it now stops resolution and fails there. Previously the walk skipped such a directory and succeeded at an ancestor. This is deliberate: silently skipping a bare `.moai` is the same behavior that let the walk skip past `~/.moai`, and repairing one without the other would leave the hazard in place. The failure message names the directory it stopped at.
- Resolved state paths are returned in symlink-resolved form. On macOS this changes the string a command prints for the same directory — `/private/var/...` rather than `/var/...`.
- `references/authoring.md` section 3 is restructured around eleven semantic roles, each carrying a light and a dark value. The dark column is derived rather than separately maintained: the `ink` and `surface` anchors swap, every other role keeps its distance from its anchor rather than its hex, opacities never change, and `accent` alone shifts one step lighter because the light value's contrast against ivory does not survive against near-black. A role added later inherits the rule instead of needing a judgement. The one-accent focal discipline survives the restructure — it was the thing most likely to be lost, so it is guarded by its own acceptance criterion.

- Eleven always-loaded documents were reduced to stubs, with their rationale, procedure, worked examples, incident records, and long cross-reference tables moved into eight new companion files that load only when the work touches them. Every obligation stayed where it was; what moved was the material explaining why it exists. The always-loaded surface fell by 8,555 tokens.
- A personal `~/.codex/AGENTS.md` joins the same merged chain and is consumed **before** a project's own contract, narrowing what that contract can carry. The shipped documentation now says so, because the effect is otherwise invisible until something is already missing.

### Fixed

- A state-reading command run anywhere under the home directory resolved to `~/.moai/state` and reported success instead of failing. Beneath that, `moai clean` deleted `runs/`, `moai chain` created `~/.moai/chain`, and the readers reported on a project nobody had named. The reproducing case now ends in `not in a MoAI project (no .moai directory found in project directories)` where it previously printed `No blockers found`.
- Every SVG the skill emits now carries a resolving accessible name: `role="img"`, `aria-labelledby`, a `<title>` as the first child of `<svg>`, and a `<desc>` describing the content rather than narrating the geometry. IDs are prefixed per diagram, because two diagrams inlined into one page with bare `title`/`desc` ids collide and the second is announced with the first one's name.
- `scripts/check-svg.mjs` enforces the contract as errors `SVG060`-`SVG064` and names which part is missing. A graphic that is genuinely decorative declares `aria-hidden="true"` and is exempt; a diagram never is.

## [3.1.2] - 2026-08-21

### Summary

A statusline change and the documentation sweep it forced.

The slash pair after the repo name used to be git ahead/behind. It now carries open issues over open change requests, and ahead/behind no longer renders. The counts had moved three times in four days — merged onto the repo head as an arrow-linked prefix, removed the same day because `59/0` read as issues/PRs when it was ahead/behind, then restored as a glyph-tagged `🐛N 🔀N` suffix specifically to avoid that misread. The misread recurred anyway. The pair is where a reader looks for issue and PR counts, so that is what it holds.

The pair distinguishes four things a reader needs to tell apart: `1/1` fetched, `0/0` fetched and nothing open, `-/-` unknown but still coming, and an absent pair when no answer was ever going to arrive. That last case has five causes — the segment is off, `statusline.forge` names `none`, the forge value is unrecognised, no forge CLI is installed, or the remote is on no recognised host (a self-hosted GitLab or GitHub Enterprise checkout). Suppression does not latch: the refresh child re-derives it every run, so installing `gh` restores the pair within one TTL with no config change.

Documentation across README and docs-site described a statusline several layouts out of date, in all four locales at once.

### Changed

- The statusline repo segment's slash pair is **open issues / open change requests**, not git ahead/behind. Ahead/behind no longer appears on the statusline; the data is still collected, it simply has no slot on the bar. Anyone reading `↑2` or `0/0` as sync state should know it is gone.
- `statusline.forge: none` (and `off`) now removes the pair entirely instead of leaving `-/-` on the bar permanently. An unrecognised `statusline.forge` value does the same, so a typo surfaces as an absent pair rather than a marker suggesting a broken fetch.
- A checkout with no forge CLI installed, or whose remote is on no recognised host, shows no pair rather than a permanent `-/-`.

### Fixed

- `-/-` is reserved for states that can resolve themselves. It previously appeared for opt-out, missing CLI, and unrecognised-host cases, telling operators an answer was coming when none ever would.
- README and docs-site statusline descriptions corrected in all four locales: the ASCII `|` separator (documented as `│`), the removed branch arrows, the unified `💾` git-status glyph (documented as the retired `📬`/`📫`/`📪`/`📭` quartet), `🔅 v<version>` without the `cc` prefix, the unspaced `TODO: n/m` ratio, and the fourth line's forge counts which had not rendered there for days.
- docs-site advertised four display presets and a `preset: compact` config key, both retired; `settings-json` described Compact/default/full display modes when every mode collapses to one layout. Both predate this release. The Korean pages were already correct — the drift was in the derived locales.
- Stale comments in `internal/statusline`: two claiming the forge counts render nowhere, a format sketch with arrows and a glyph that appears nowhere in the code, two references to the retired mailbox quartet, and a header claiming a 5-line Full layout the same file records as retired. `renderGitBranch`, which had no non-test caller, is deleted — its doc comment was the likely source of the stale arrow prose in the READMEs.
- `moai glm tools enable --force` overwrites a mismatched token instead of re-emitting the error that just advised it ([#1587](https://github.com/modu-ai/moai-adk/issues/1587)). The flag parsed and the command branched on it, but both branches called the same function, whose token check is a deliberate contract. Changing the token needed a hand edit of `~/.claude.json`. The force path is now its own entry point; the checking path still refuses, and a forced overwrite still leaves every other `mcpServers` entry alone.
- The forge counts could render stale for one frame during a refresh. The refresh stamps the cache before fetching so concurrent renders stop spawning children, and it was clearing the suppression flag in that same early write — before it knew whether a forge or its CLI existed. A render landing in that window drew counts for a checkout that had neither. The flag now clears once both are confirmed.
- The statusline docs said a missing forge CLI leaves the cached counts on the bar. The pair is dropped whenever suppression is set, cached or not; the cache survives on disk for the restore after the CLI is installed. The same page already said so two sections earlier, so it contradicted itself. Corrected in all four locales, along with a `segments:` example that labelled `worktree` opt-in when an omitted key resolves to enabled.

## [3.1.1] - 2026-08-20

### Summary

v3.1.1 is a maintenance release on top of v3.1.0, gathered from two integration batches on the `release/v3.1.1` lane. Four themes dominate it.

**`~/.moai` gets a single source of truth, and a way to clean it.** Home-scope path construction — previously three incompatible styles scattered across seven packages — collapses into one stdlib-only `internal/paths` package honoring a new `MOAI_HOME` override, and all 17 home-scope join sites route through it. On top of that foundation, `moai doctor` gains a `Home Disk Usage` advisory check and `moai clean` gains a `--home` flag whose deletion candidates come from an explicit four-category allowlist guarded by a recursive carve-out predicate — unreadable means unverified, and unverified never deletes.

**The always-loaded context surface goes on a measured diet.** 295,044 → 288,975 bytes, guard headroom 1,239 → 2,757 tokens, with the file count and the budget constant both left untouched — the mechanism is `paths:`-scoped companions rather than deletion. Two structural countermeasures land with it: five prompt-cache discipline directives folded into the always-loaded rule set, and a recurrence control that turns silent re-growth into a stated, sized cost.

**Kanban and Factory terminology settles.** The v3.1.0 rename of `manager-lead` to `manager-kanban` is reversed — the agent is `manager-lead` again, now covering both the kanban lead and the factory lead session — and the board drops from six columns to five, with the sync gate absorbing the review verdict rather than a `review` column owning it. Factory Mode gets its own entry flag — `moai cc -f <N>` / `moai glm -f <N>` launches a lead plus N numbered lanes, each lane carrying a whole card through plan → run → sync in-session, while the kanban chain keeps `-k` — companion sessions are named by bare role, and the orchestration mode catalog is renamed onto the spawn-count axis (six modes to four: `direct` / `serial` / `fanout` / `sweep`).

**Correctness fixes across the CLI, hooks, and the test harness.** `moai update` now backs up unmanaged files *before* removing managed roots, so a template redeploy no longer silently drops local-only files. A raw `*exec.ExitError` is rejected at the exit-code seam, where it had been structurally satisfying the `ExitCoder` interface and passing a subprocess's exit code through as the CLI's own. GLM-5.3 is recognized as a genuine 1M-context model on every surface that reports a context window. And the timing test harness pairs its calibration reference with the measured runs so both sides share one load window, removing a class of load-dependent false trips.

### Added

- **[SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-HOME-PATHS-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-HOME-PATHS-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **`~/.moai` path construction now has a single source of truth: a new stdlib-only `internal/paths` package (`Home()` + `MoaiHome()` + 8 typed sub-path accessors) honors a new `MOAI_HOME` environment override, and all 17 home-scope `.moai` join sites across 7 packages route through it — collapsing the three previously incompatible home-resolution styles (inline `os.UserHomeDir()`, the HOME-first `homedir.go` wrapper, and injected seams) into one contract.** Before this, `~/.moai` path construction was scattered across seven packages with no override mechanism, so tests could only redirect home via `t.Setenv("HOME", ...)` and no way existed to relocate the state root. `MOAI_HOME` semantics: a non-empty absolute path is returned verbatim as the root; empty equals unset; relative values are disregarded (XDG semantics). The SPEC supersedes SPEC-V3R6-SESSION-HANDOFF-AUTO-001's recorded home-resolution position ("new code MUST reuse `os.UserHomeDir()` — no abstraction layer exists"; REQ-MHP-012): the abstraction layer now exists and is mandatory for `~/.moai` paths.
  - **M1/M2 — the package + the env registration (`internal/paths/paths.go`, `internal/config/envkeys.go`).** `internal/paths` imports the standard library only (REQ-MHP-003), so the stdlib-only `internal/glmcred` can adopt it without an import cycle. `EnvHome = "MOAI_HOME"` is registered in `envkeys.go`; `internal/paths` references the name through a glmcred-style exported local alias (`paths.EnvHome`) because importing `internal/config` from `internal/paths` would create a cycle — the `"MOAI_HOME"` string literal lives nowhere else (AC-MHP-006 grep: 0 matches outside the two owners). The sub-path accessors (`StateDir`, `CacheDir`, `ReleasesDir`, `WorktreesDir`, `ProfilesDir`, `GlmEnvFile`, `UserSettingsFile`, `UserConfigSectionsDir`) consume segment constants mirroring `internal/defs/dirs.go`, the canonical owner; no accessor re-literals `".moai"`. Every accessor resolves per call — caching is prohibited (REQ-MHP-005) so the 107 `t.Setenv("HOME", ...)` tests keep observing env changes made later in the same process.
  - **M3/M4 — the 17-site migration.** Every home-scope `filepath.Join(<home>, ".moai", ...)` site migrated: statusline (model_cache ×2, usage, task), config resolver ×2, cli (launcher ×2, deps, migrate_profiles), update (local, cache), kanban/status_read, hook (session_start, pre_tool whitelist), glmcred, and the predicate-hidden receiver-prefix site `internal/cli/migrate_agency.go:184` (fed by the `update.go:827` adapter, which also migrates — so an overridden `MOAI_HOME` reaches the `.migrate-tx-*` checkpoint path). The acceptance predicate (the home-scope-anchored grep) returns 0 matches post-migration against a pre-migration baseline of exactly 17. Injected test seams (`HomeDirFn`, `homeDir` parameters) are preserved: seam-carrying sites consume `defs` segment constants while seam callers resolve via `paths`.
  - **M5 — helper collapse + security lockstep.** `internal/cli/homedir.go` is now a thin delegate to `paths.Home()` and the verbatim duplicate in `internal/cli/preference/cmd.go` is deleted — exactly one HOME-first implementation remains (AC-MHP-009 grep: one match). The `internal/hook/pre_tool.go` allowed-external-paths security whitelist and the `internal/core/project/root.go` home special-case resolve through the same accessors in lockstep (REQ-MHP-009), so an overridden `MOAI_HOME` is consistently trusted by the security boundary and the project-root guard, not only by state writes.
  - **Known hazard — the `.env.glm` shell/Go split (spec.md §4).** `status_line.sh` sources `$HOME/.moai/.env.glm` BEFORE the Go binary runs, while Go reads the same file via glmcred — under a non-default `MOAI_HOME` the two would read different locations, splitting one credential file across two roots. Shell-side `MOAI_HOME` support (`status_line.sh` + the 42 `handle-*.sh` files) is deliberately out of scope and recommended as a follow-up backlog card; until it lands, `MOAI_HOME` is effectively Go-process-only.
  - 16 acceptance criteria (AC-MHP-001..016 — the Tier M ceiling), all PASS: 14 verified in the run phase, and the two doc checks discharged by this sync commit (AC-MHP-013 — REQ-MHP-012 supersession note present in spec.md with SPEC-V3R6-SESSION-HANDOFF-AUTO-001 in `related_specs`; AC-MHP-014 — the §4 hazard recorded in this entry). Coverage per run-phase §E.2: `go test -cover ./internal/paths/` → **100.0%**. Run-phase per-milestone commits M1 `6600bfd8e` → M2 `da85d179c` → M3 `51a587bb0` → M4 `2a598a877` → M5 `d50a54ba9` → M6 evidence `d50725c85`; merged into the `release/v3.1.1` integration lane at merge commit `e518fc897` (user-directed lane routing — no per-phase PR; this commit ships with the next lane push). This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition on `spec.md` (allowed scope: `status:` + `updated:`), the `progress.md §E.4` audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (sync is markdown-only). `sync_commit_sha` populated as a `pending-backfill-SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-HOME-PATHS-001` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot know its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-CLEAN-HOME-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-CLEAN-HOME-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **`moai doctor` gains a `Home Disk Usage` advisory check and `moai clean` gains a `--home` flag, giving `~/.moai` both visibility and safe cleanup of its regenerable artifacts (aged release binaries, per-profile `debug/` logs, aged backups).** Before this, the existing `moai clean` was project-scoped (`.moai/state/runs/` only) and `moai doctor`'s check lineup had no disk check at all — `~/.moai` accumulated old release binaries, profile debug logs, and aged backup directories with no surface to see them and no safe command to remove them. The home root resolves through `internal/paths.MoaiHome()` (the dependency SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-HOME-PATHS-001 foundation), so an absolute `MOAI_HOME` override redirects both surfaces uniformly (AC-MCH-012 redirect test: aged entries under the override root are deleted while the default-`HOME` fixture stays untouched).
  - **M1 — the doctor check (`internal/cli/doctor_disk.go`, registered in `internal/cli/doctor.go`).** `checkHomeDisk` reports the `~/.moai` top-level breakdown (top 3 by size), per-profile sizes under `claude-profiles/`, cross-profile duplicate clusters, the `releases/` count vs the current version, and a cleanable-bytes estimate. Duplicate clusters use the resolved D1 heuristic — a category name whose (total size, file count) signature is carried identically by two or more profiles — with no content hashing; false positives are accepted by design because the finding is report-only. `plugins/` directories are reported by the cluster detection but never touched by `clean --home` (REQ-MCH-006 single-home protection). The cleanable estimate is computed by the SAME scanner `clean --home` deletes by, under the home-tier retention, so doctor and clean cannot disagree. Above `DefaultHomeDiskWarnBytes` the check emits WARN with a `moai clean --home` pointer. `~/.claude` appears only as a summary line marked `(report-only — moai clean never touches it)`.
  - **M2/M3 — `moai clean --home` + the carve-out invariant (`internal/cli/clean_home.go`, `internal/cli/clean.go`).** Dry-run by default (lists what would be deleted with sizes); `--force` deletes. Scope is `~/.moai` only — `~/.claude` is never mutated. Deletion candidates come exclusively from an explicit four-category allowlist: per-profile `debug/` entries older than retention, `releases/` binaries beyond the current version plus the `DefaultReleaseKeep` (3) newest (sidecar `.sha256` files follow their binary), root `logs/` files older than retention, and `backups/removed-*` directories older than retention — everything not enumerated is invisible to the scanner. The `isCarvedOut` path-predicate guard enforces the REQ-MCH-005 invariant with recursive any-segment semantics: carved-out names (`projects/`, `config/`, `state/`, `credentials*`, `launch.yaml`, `preferences.yaml`, `worktrees/`, `mcp/`, `bin/`, `search/`, `studio/`, `plugins/`) match at ANY depth — root level and per-profile alike — and the carve-out WINS inside allowlisted containers, so a `credentials*`-named file inside an aged `backups/removed-*` directory is never deleted, even under `--force`; an aged backup containing any carved-out entry skips the WHOLE directory (audit residual R1: whole-dir skip, never a partially-deleted restorable snapshot). Walk errors are treated as carved-out — unreadable means unverified, and unverified never deletes. `os.RemoveAll` fires only on scanner-produced candidates (`@MX:WARN`-tagged deletion sites). The project-scope `clean` path (`stateYAMLWrapper` in `clean.go`) is byte-untouched — its SPEC-V3R2-RT-004 contract is pinned by the AC-MCH-009 regression test.
  - **Retention — home tier only (`internal/config/defaults.go`, `clean_home.go`).** `state.home_retention_days` is read from the HOME tier — `~/.moai/config/sections/state.yaml` resolved via `paths.UserConfigSectionsDir()` — never from a project-tier state.yaml (cwd-dependent reads would let two projects clean one home with different windows). An absent key defaults to `DefaultHomeCleanRetentionDays` (30); an explicit `0` disables cleaning. The three thresholds live as constants in `defaults.go` (`DefaultHomeDiskWarnBytes`, `DefaultHomeCleanRetentionDays`, `DefaultReleaseKeep`) with zero inline literals in the check or clean sources (AC-MCH-011 grep: 0 matches). A `*int` pointer distinguishes an explicit `0` from an absent key.
  - 12 acceptance criteria (AC-MCH-001..012), all PASS. Run-phase per-milestone commits M1 `051a2fa94` → M2 `6ec0ad212` → M3 `a42941195` → M4 `46ed09a66` (evidence + `run_commit_sha` backfill `1b9440595`) on the `release/v3.1.1` integration lane (user-directed lane routing — no per-phase PR). Coverage per run-phase §E.2: **87.6% weighted across the two NEW files** (276/315 statements; `go tool cover -func` per-function `clean_home.go` 75.0–92.1%, `doctor_disk.go` 50.0–100.0%). AC-MCH-010 disclosed gap: the unscoped `go test ./internal/cli/` run exits 1 on `TestHandleCodexReviewGate_LiveCodexBlocksInjectionAndKey`, a pre-existing test that shells out to the real codex backend — non-intersecting with this SPEC (the 17-file run diff contains no codex file; reproduced 2/2 in isolation; identical in cause to the pre-run state) and skipped in CI, which lacks a working codex. Everything else in both packages passes. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition on `spec.md` (allowed scope: `status:` + `updated:` — already 2026-08-17), the `progress.md §E.4` audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (sync is markdown-only; no separate doc-check ACs exist for this SPEC — this entry is the docs surface). `sync_commit_sha` populated as a `pending-backfill-SPEC-V3R6-MOAI-CLEAN-HOME-001` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot know its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-ALWAYS-LOADED-DIET-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-ALWAYS-LOADED-DIET-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The always-loaded context surface was put on a measured diet — 295,044 → 288,975 bytes, guard headroom 1,239 → 2,757 tokens — and two structural countermeasures landed with it: five prompt-cache discipline directives folded into the always-loaded rule set, and a recurrence control that turns silent re-growth into a stated, sized cost.** Before this, the mechanical guard (`TestAlwaysLoadedTokenBudget`, budget 75,000 tokens) had 1.65% headroom left (1,239 tokens ≈ 4,956 bytes), and the surface had a demonstrated recurrence pattern — roughly 4x growth in the three months after the previous reduction, about half of it existing files expanding in place. The diet is deliberately conservative: the always-loaded file count stays 14 (files were split and scoped, not deleted), the budget constant is untouched, and zero Go files changed — the mechanism is `paths:`-scoped companions, the pattern the existing rule set already uses.
  - **M1 — `kanban-dispatch.md` split into an always-loaded stub + a paths-scoped companion.** The six lead-only sections (The board; Entry into the board is an operator act; Card classes; The dispatch cycle including Dispatch language; Review lens selection; The `/clear` handoff) moved verbatim to the new `kanban-dispatch-detail.md`, keyed to load only when a kanban-dispatch file, `manager-kanban.md`, or `workflows/todo.md` is touched. The stub keeps every section that binds non-lead sessions, plus the Class B paragraph relocated whole-line into "Completion is read, never trusted" so the one card-class rule a companion session might rely on stays always-loaded. Measured: stub 13,027 B + companion 8,866 B = 21,893 B against the pre-split file (890 B split overhead); HARD-clause census preserved (6 + 3 = 9); headroom 1,239 → 3,233 tokens after M1.
  - **M2 — G3-G7 cache disciplines folded into `cache-aware-execution.md` as directives 6-10, with a cited-numbers reference companion.** Five one-paragraph `[ZONE:Evolvable] [HARD]` directives in the existing 1-5 shape: pass files by `@`-mention rather than name-citation (`/context` as a one-shot audit, not a routine check); keep command output bounded (quiet flags, targeted queries, redirect-to-file with exit code + bounded tail — `BASH_MAX_OUTPUT_LENGTH` is the runtime backstop, not the target); prefer the quiet form of routine commands; weigh session length as a cost axis (a fresh session re-pays the always-loaded prefix at write price); and a mid-session model or effort switch busts the cache. The new `cache-aware-execution-reference.md` (self-keyed to the parent rule) carries the cited cache numbers — explicitly quoted source values, never restated as measurements of this project — plus per-directive rationale and the axis resolution against Per-Spawn Model Injection (main-session cache vs subagent model selection: different axes, neither SSOT revises the other). Growth +1,907 B, inside the plan's [1,000, 2,000] byte window.
  - **M3 — `rule-authoring.md` recurrence control (new, paths-scoped).** A statement duty binding all four guard slots (unscoped rule files, `CLAUDE.md`, output styles, `MEMORY.md` head): creating a new always-loaded file, or a single edit growing one by more than 1,000 bytes, requires stating the size and a cost justification that addresses what sessions that never need the file pay every turn and after every `/clear` — with scope-first (can this live under a `paths:` companion instead?) asked before either duty fires. Threshold calibration table included; deliberately no cumulative-delta secondary trigger (accepted residual risk, stated as such rather than patched). The rule carries its own top-level `paths:` key, so the recurrence control is excluded from the surface it polices — the control must not itself be recurrence.
  - **M4 — all 5 rule files mirrored to templates.** The two modified and three new rule files are mirrored byte-identical to `internal/template/templates/` (sources authored neutral, so no sanitization was needed — scan evidence in progress.md §E.2 M4), `make build` regenerates the embedded catalog, and the three template-neutrality CI targets pass locally in isolation exactly as CI runs them.
  - 16 acceptance criteria (AC-ALD-001..016), all PASS, verified against the run-phase implementation at PR #1577 squash `67466e997d` on origin/main (per-milestone commits M1 `a203a7c3a` → M2 `6d701d26c` → M3 `fcc07d1bc` → M4 `9269a5e56` preserved inside that squash). Final state measured by both the authoritative guard (`go test ./internal/config/ -run TestAlwaysLoaded` green; budget constant unchanged) and the frontmatter-aware auxiliary: `files=14 bytes=288975 tokens=72243 headroom=2757`. Zero Go files changed across the entire run phase. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` terminal transition on `spec.md` (sole YAML-frontmatter artifact — plan.md / acceptance.md / progress.md use the markdown-header convention), the `progress.md §E.4` audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further changes (sync is markdown-only). README / docs-site unchanged — this is internal rule architecture with no user-facing surface (no CLI, no config key, no documented behavior change; the rules govern agent-side context discipline). `sync_commit_sha` populated as a `pending-backfill-*` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot know its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **Factory Mode.** `moai cc -f <N>` (or `moai glm -f <N>`) launches a lead session plus N numbered lanes (`lane-1`..`lane-N`); each lane carries one whole card through plan, run, and sync in a single session. `moai cc -f lane-<n>` re-enters an individual lane. The kanban chain keeps `-k`.
- **`moai graph build` and `moai graph query`.** Build a call-graph edge index (`edges.jsonl`) over the repository, then query it for callers and blast radius. `graph build` also reports milestone-to-card cross-check edges and can list milestones with no card via `--milestones-no-card`.
- **`moai memory doctor` and `moai memory archive`.** Diagnose the memory store (broken links, orphaned topic files) and archive superseded entries.
- **`moai tokens record`.** Record token usage into the per-pool ledger.
- **`moai todo` gains `drop`, `undrop`, `edit`, `move`, and `unpick`**, plus a bare `moai todo` list form and natural-language `add`.
- **New MCP tools for GLM delegation and cross-model audit.** `glm_task`, `glm_job_status`, `glm_job_result`, and `glm_job_cancel` delegate a task to the z.ai backend as a synchronous or background job; `glm_audit` and `audit_multi` add single-backend and multi-backend audit convergence.
- **Config section `crosssession.yaml`.** `inbound` (`""` / `accept` / `hold` / `refuse`) controls how this session treats messages from your other Claude Code sessions; `isolate_machines` requires explicit approval before a message leaves the machine; `dialog_expiry` bounds how long a held message waits.
- **Config section `cache.yaml`.** `cacheStrategy.enabled`, `cacheStrategy.session_ttl` (`1h` / `5m` / `off`), and `cacheStrategy.spec_ttl` (`5m` / `off`) control Anthropic prompt-cache breakpoint injection, alongside a minimum cacheable segment size. GLM caches implicitly and is unaffected.
- **`statusline.forge`.** Select the forge the statusline reports items from — `github`, `gitlab`, or `none`.
- **A bare `/loop` now runs the kanban foreman.** Each iteration watches the backlog queue, dispatches the next operator-picked card to an isolated worker, and reports on evidence read rather than on claims.
- **`moai-domain-design-dna` skill.** Reverse-engineer a reference design — screenshot, image set, or URL — into a portable Design DNA JSON, then generate a new self-contained artifact from it.
- **Six ast-grep rule files now ship with the templates** under `.moai/config/astgrep-rules/{go,security}/`, so `moai init` and `moai update` deploy a working baseline ruleset instead of a single example rule.
- **`MOAI_SESSION_PID`** is stamped into launched sessions on POSIX platforms (Windows launches do not set it).
- **`ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_MODEL`** is now a recognized environment key.

### Changed

- **`gate.ast_grep_gate.rules_dir` now defaults to `.moai/config/astgrep-rules`** instead of `""`, and the shipped config is the sole source of truth — the code-side fallback path is gone. A project that relied on the empty-value fallback must set the key explicitly.
- **`manager-kanban` is `manager-lead` again**, reversing the v3.1.0 rename. The one agent now covers both the kanban lead and the factory lead session. Any local prompt, script, or config naming `manager-kanban` must be updated; the old name no longer resolves.
- **The kanban board has five columns, not six.** The `review` column is gone — the sync gate absorbs the review verdict.
- **Companion sessions are named by bare role** (`plan`, `run`, `sync`, collision-bumped when duplicated), and factory lanes are named `lane-<N>`.
- **GLM-5.3 is recognized as a 1M-context model** on every surface that reports a context window, rather than being treated as a 200K session.
- **The orchestration mode catalog is renamed onto the spawn-count axis**, going from six modes to four: `direct`, `serial`, `fanout`, `sweep`. Configuration referring to the previous names needs updating.

### Fixed

- **`moai update` backs up unmanaged files before removing managed roots.** A template redeploy previously wiped managed roots first, silently dropping local-only files that had no template counterpart.
- **A raw `*exec.ExitError` is rejected at the exit-code seam.** It had been structurally satisfying the `ExitCoder` interface, so a subprocess's exit code passed through as the CLI's own and its output was silently dropped.
- **Installer scripts resolve the latest version without the rate-limited REST API**, using the `/releases/latest` redirect instead, and `install.bat` now ships with CRLF line endings so `cmd.exe` runs it.
- **`moai mcp` is registered on the root command** and is reachable again.
- **Worktree and hook correctness.** `moai worktree done` now surfaces git's stderr and points at the lock when removal fails instead of exiting silently; anchor guards protect the session's own worktree during remove, clean, and post-PR-merge cleanup; and `isolation: worktree` agent spawns no longer fail with zero diagnostics on a non-repository cwd.
- **`/moai gate` resolves the Node test step to a run-form command**, preferring `test:run` over a watch-mode runner and dropping stray flags.
- **An armed goal now disarms** when a turn dies on an unrecoverable API error, instead of leaving the session looping against a condition nothing is advancing.

## [3.1.0] - 2026-08-15

### Summary

v3.1.0 promotes the `v3.1.0-rc.0` → `-rc.1` → `-rc.2` candidate line to a production release. Three themes dominate it.

**Kanban Mode** is the headline addition: a six-column board (`backlog → plan → run → review → sync → done`) driven by a lead session that dispatches cards to operator-launched companion sessions. The mode arrives with a single-origin board state store that never writes into SPEC frontmatter, a `moai todo` CLI for the backlog queue, a lead session name that survives `/clear`, and an operator-language bootstrap notice. The former `manager-lead` agent is renamed `manager-kanban`, and the launcher flag `-f/--factory` is renamed `-k/--kanban`.

**The `moai web` console** completes its redesign: the transitional settings and board layers are retired into the shell, the console chrome is wired and translated across all four locales, and the schema-driven form renders closed-set fields as closed widgets.

**Model policy and hooks** are tightened: the effort matrix is phase-weighted (plan at the top, run/review at high, sync at low), GLM model tiers collapse to one model per Claude slot, the branch-guard scan is scoped to the command being invoked rather than to text it carries as data, and `moai update` derives a merge base so template additions actually reach existing projects.

### Changed

- **The launcher flag `-f` / `--factory` is renamed `-k` / `--kanban`.** The rename is complete rather than aliased — package, environment variables, functions, and file names all move — so a script invoking `moai cc -f` must switch to `moai cc -k`. "Factory" described an earlier design that the six-column board replaced; keeping the old name would have meant the flag and the feature disagreed.
- **Internal documentation staleness rewrite.** Four stale references retired, three re-anchored, five confirmed live.
- **The effort matrix is phase-weighted, and the GLM session reasoning default is lowered.** Effort is now routed by workflow phase rather than applied uniformly: plan runs at the top level (the phase where a wrong decision is most expensive to unwind), run and review at high, and sync steps down to low — sync is largely mechanical, so paying peak reasoning there bought nothing. The GLM session reasoning default drops in the same direction. Model and effort continue to resolve through `moai model profile`; no agent file hardcodes either.
- **GLM model tiers collapse to one model per Claude slot.** The tier table previously allowed several GLM models to share a slot, which made the resolved model depend on ordering rather than on the slot. Each Claude slot (Opus / Sonnet / Haiku / Fable) now maps to exactly one GLM model.
- **The `moai web` console retires its transitional layers.** Settings fold into the shell and the board view folds into `/specs`, removing the two-layer arrangement that survived the console redesign as scaffolding. The console shell chrome is wired and translated, so the navigation, headers, and status surfaces render in the operator's locale rather than falling back to English.

- **`manager-lead` renamed to `manager-kanban`.** The coordination agent now carries two roles over one skill set rather than one: **Role A** is the existing in-session hierarchical fan-out at Tier L (unchanged — same entry threshold, same depth-2 seal, same context-folding procedure), and **Role B** is the new cross-session kanban-lead role that moves a card across the six-column board by instructing operator-launched companion sessions. The rename is mechanical and complete across live wiring: the agent file, `CLAUDE.md` §4, `.moai/config/sections/workflow.yaml`, the harness delegation map and v4 manifest, the agent-lint surface, the depth-seal CI guard (`manager_lead_depth_test.go` → `manager_kanban_depth_test.go`), README (en/ja/zh), and the docs-site page in all four locales (`advanced/manager-lead` → `advanced/manager-kanban`, with permanent redirects added to `docs-site/vercel.json` so existing links keep resolving). Historical records are deliberately NOT rewritten — prior CHANGELOG entries and closed SPEC bodies keep the name that was actually shipped at the time, because rewriting them would misstate what a given release contained.

### Added

- **`moai todo` — the backlog CLI behind `/moai todo`, with a lock-guarded store.** The slash surface needed a non-interactive counterpart so the queue could be read and written from a script, a hook, or a companion session that has no `AskUserQuestion` channel. `moai todo` supplies it: append, list, and remove operate on the same project-local queue, guarded by a lock so two sessions on one checkout cannot interleave a read-modify-write and lose an entry. A malformed store is reported and left untouched rather than reset — operator intent is the one thing here that cannot be regenerated.
- **The six-column kanban board, with a single-origin state store.** Column assignments live in exactly one file that only the `lead` role may write, so a card's position is never a second datum competing with SPEC frontmatter. The store resolves relative to the git common directory (so every worktree of a checkout sees the same board), writes atomically, treats an absent file as the empty board, and clears a stale lock only when the holder is provably dead — an indeterminate liveness result is treated as live, because guessing "dead" is what unlinks a live holder's lock.
- **The kanban lead session is named, so it survives `/clear`.** The lead's run id is derived from the session name, and an operator-supplied name is adopted rather than overridden. A lead that clears its context can therefore still be addressed by companion sessions under the same name, which is what makes the between-phase `/clear` in the dispatch protocol safe to perform.

- **`/moai todo` — the backlog queue, and the kanban dispatch protocol that consumes it.** The six-column board (`backlog → plan → run → review → sync → done`) leaves `backlog` with no owning session by design: nothing dispatches work *into* the board, so admission has to be an operator act — and until now no surface performed it. `/moai todo` is that surface: `"<description>"` appends, a bare invocation lists, `next` presents the queue through `AskUserQuestion` for the operator to pick, and `done <n>` removes. State is a thin project-local queue at `.moai/state/kanban/backlog.json` (atomic writes; a malformed file is reported and left untouched rather than reset, since operator intent is the one thing here that cannot be regenerated). It holds intent only — no priorities, assignees, or due dates, and it is never auto-populated from TODO comments, open issues, or audit findings.
  - **`kanban-dispatch.md` (new always-loaded rule)** specifies what the lead session does with a picked card: dispatch by session name to the companion owning each column, choose review lenses from what the card actually changed (`--security` / `--deep` for reachable-by-untrusted-input surfaces, `--lean` for suspected over-engineering, no flag for a small local diff), and advance the card **only on evidence it read from `progress.md`** — never on a companion's reply, since inter-session replies are claims rather than observations and are not guaranteed to arrive. Between phases the lead asks the operator to `/clear` the named session (a user-typed command that cannot be sent as an instruction), stating what closed, which session to clear, and what happens next. A column whose owning role has no live session is reported as a fault rather than held silently, because a stalled board with no error is the most expensive failure shape to diagnose.
  - Explicitly out of scope, and stated as such rather than implied: persistent six-column board state, per-card worktree lifecycle, WIP limits, and card/frontmatter consistency reconciliation. The lead spawns nothing — it addresses sessions the operator launched — and being inside a dispatch cycle bypasses no approval gate.

- **[SPEC-WEB-CONSOLE-REDESIGN-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-WEB-CONSOLE-REDESIGN-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The `moai web` settings console was redesigned around a schema-driven form whose widgets tell the truth about the values they accept: dead config was removed from the edit surface, the console was restructured from 6 tabs into 9, closed-set fields now render as closed widgets (select/radio) instead of free text, the GLM tab honestly names the applied source of reasoning effort, the profile UI was de-duplicated, and the incomplete `/autonomy/tiers` stub was removed.** Before this, the form rendered whatever the schema declared but lied in three ways: it offered 7 fields nothing consumed (`workflow.token_budget.{plan,run,sync}`, `workflow.auto_clear.{enabled,after_plan,after_run,token_threshold}` — accessors preserved for backward compat, the edit surface dropped); the `git_strategy` section had FieldDefs registered but no render meta, so `mode` and the three per-profile `merge_method` controls had no UI at all; and every bool was a checkbox / every closed-set field was free text, so a user could type a value the server would silently reject. The redesign closes those gaps and records a structural-coverage ceiling as debt rather than padding toward 90% with tests that assert nothing. Run-phase landed on main as PR #1410 squash `b0d3b61f8` (2026-08-08).
  - **M1 — dead config retired + git_strategy render surface restored (REQ-WCR-001..004).** The 7 dead-config `FieldDef`s are removed from the web edit schema. The yaml keys, `internal/config` struct members, and accessors are preserved verbatim — `TestWorkflowBackwardCompat` pins that an existing `workflow.yaml` carrying both retired blocks still loads and the accessors still return its values (no user's config breaks). `git_strategy.mode` and the three per-profile `merge_method` controls are rendered through a new `git-worktree` panel; the section id is registered in `SchemaSectionIDs()` and now has render meta to match.
  - **M2 — tab restructure 6→9 (REQ-WCR-010..013).** `consoleTabs()` returns 9 entries: `identity` / `language` / `launch` (LLM) / `llm` (3rd Party LLM) / `workflow` / `git-worktree` / `audit` / `agentfm` / `report`. The overloaded Workflow tab is split: `workflow.worktree.*` + `branch_guard.enabled` move to the new Git & Worktree tab; `workflow.audit.*` moves to the new Audit tab. The move is render-placement only — `FieldDef.Section` stays `SectionWorkflow` and the persist target stays `workflow.yaml`, so no write route changes. The tab list is now the single source of panel order (the previous hand-written panel sequence had drifted from the tab order — `report` rendered before `agentfm`).
  - **M3 — widget honesty policy (REQ-WCR-020..023).** Every bool field is now a 2-option radio group (used / not-used) instead of a checkbox, changed in one place (`schemaToggleRow`) so it applies to every tab. The hidden companion `<name>__present` input is preserved so a submitted-but-empty bool still parses as "value retained", not "value cleared". Closed-set fields (`workflow.execution_mode`, `workflow.default_mode`, `workflow.audit.model`, `workflow.audit.gates.{claude,codex,glm}`, `harness.*`) render as select/radio with membership validation — a save request carrying an out-of-set value is rejected at the apply layer. Genuinely open fields (`feedback.repository`, `observability.{report_dir,trace_dir}`, GLM API key) remain free text.
  - **M4 — 3rd Party LLM tab (REQ-WCR-030..034).** `llm.glm.models.{high,medium,low,fable}` render as selects over `{glm-5.2, glm-5.1, glm-4.7, glm-4.5-air}` (derived from `DefaultGLM*` constants — zero literal re-declaration). Each tier carries a reasoning-effort select over `{Max, High, None}` (derived from `template.GLMReasoningStateNames()`). Tier labels read Opus / Sonnet / Haiku / Fable (i18n titles); internal form keys `high` / `medium` / `low` / `fable` stay unchanged. **GLM honesty badge (F4a correction):** the reasoning-effort runtime channel is a single session-level env var, so per-tier effort values are *store-only*. The panel renders a badge naming the **applied source** (the session-level `effort_level` collapsed to z.ai's 3-state) rather than implying a tier's effort is applied at runtime. GLM API key never echoes by default (`value=""` always); a reveal toggle fetches plaintext once server-side and is refused for non-loopback origins.
  - **M5 — profile UI dedup (REQ-WCR-040..042).** The standalone `profileManager` card is removed. Add / rename / delete controls sit adjacent to the profile bar; each is its own POST form placed *outside* the main settings form (nested forms are invalid HTML). A new rename handler rejects default profiles, the currently active profile, name collisions, and path-traversal targets, with the reason surfaced as a banner. `profileSwitch` (the select + "selected: X" header) is preserved.
  - **M6 — autonomy stub removed (REQ-WCR-050).** The bare `<a href="/autonomy/tiers">` link injected before `</body>`, the GET-only `handleAutonomyTiers` handler with no form/action, and the `/autonomy/tiers` route are removed. The init-path `ApplyAutonomyTierBundle` writer and the `MOAI_AUTONOMY_TIER` env seam are preserved (console surface only removed). Removal rationale recorded in plan.md §B D3.
  - **`workflow.execution_mode` closed-set correction (M3 residual, §E.2.1).** M3 had declared `config.ValidExecutionModes()` as `{auto, solo, team}` — an inference with no SSOT basis that omitted `cg` (the fourth documented mode in `harness.yaml` `mode_defaults` and `SPEC-V3R2-HRN-001` REQ-HRN-001-014). Because M3 converted the field to a closed widget, the omission was a regression: a documented mode was unselectable. Fixed by splitting `ValidExecutionModePins()` = `{solo, team, cg}` and `ValidExecutionModes()` = `auto` + pins, with the `harness.mode_defaults.*` FieldDef list derived from the same accessor so the two sets cannot drift.
  - **AC-WCR-063 — PASS-WITH-DEBT (user-approved 2026-08-13).** Coverage measured **73.5%** against a 90% bar. 83% of the package's statements (4323/5208) are `templ generate` output; 927 of the uncovered statements (17.8% of the package) are unreachable `if templ_err != nil { return }` / `ctx.Err()` / `NopComponent` boilerplate that templ emits after every write — unreachable when rendering into `strings.Builder` / `httptest` buffers. **Structural ceiling: 80.7%** even with every real branch covered. Hand-written code alone is **91.1%** (already meets a 90% hand-written-code bar). The honest fix is to redefine AC-WCR-063's denominator (exclude `*_templ.go` generated output, or redefine as hand-written-code ≥ 90%) — **that SPEC-body change is manager-spec's domain and is recorded as the DEBT**, to be resolved in a follow-up. Full rationale at `progress.md §E.2.3`.

- **[SPEC-CHAIN-CORE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CHAIN-CORE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **An append-only JSONL lineage tree at `.moai/state/chain/events.jsonl` now records worktree spawn boundaries, session_id backfills, and completion edges as named fields on a `WorktreeNode` record — structurally solving the depth-2–3 context-loss pain where the maintainer loses track of origin, what just completed, and where to resume after `/clear`.** Before this, every persistence primitive was FLAT: the session registry `Entry` carried `CWD` as its only worktree link (no parent, no depth, no origin chain); the handoff `pending.json` was singular per-project and overwritten on each save (a depth-3 save clobbered the depth-1 record); the worktree `Snapshot` captured only divergence state. The three pain questions become named fields on a structured record — **Origin** → `origin_chain` + `depth` + `parent_node_id`; **What just completed** → `last_completed_milestone` + completion edges; **Where to resume** → `resume_target` + `resume_command`. This is Phase 1 of the Origin-Trail Chain Epic; it is independently shippable with no dependency on kanban mode (`-k`), factory mode (`-f`), or the manager-lead agent (those are later phases P3–P7).
  - **M0 — WorktreeNode struct + append-only JSONL store (`internal/chain/`).** `WorktreeNode` (13 named fields: NodeID, ParentNodeID, Depth, OriginChain, WorktreePath, SessionID, SpecID, Milestone, EnteredAt, ExitedAt, LastCompletedMilestone, ResumeTarget, ResumeCommand — REQ-CHAIN-001) is derived at read time from a flat `ChainEvent` stream (three event types: `node-enter`, `node-update`, `completion-edge`). `Store.Append` opens with `O_APPEND|O_CREATE|O_WRONLY` + `0o600` and NEVER overwrites, truncates, or seeks-replace (REQ-CHAIN-002). `Store.ReadAll` skips corrupt JSONL lines with a diagnostic warning identifying the line number, and a missing file returns an empty slice, not an error (REQ-CHAIN-003). `Store.ResolveByCWD` resolves the current node via the `(worktree_path, session_id)` pair (REQ-CHAIN-004).
  - **M1 — spawn-boundary population + env constant + session_id backfill.** `Populator.CreateNodeAtSpawn` reads `MOAI_CHAIN_NODE_ID` from the spawning context's environment, derives `depth = parent_depth + 1`, builds `origin_chain = parent_chain + [nodeID]`, and appends a skeleton `node-enter` event with `session_id` empty (REQ-CHAIN-005). `EnvChainNodeID = "MOAI_CHAIN_NODE_ID"` is exposed as a constant in `internal/config/envkeys.go` (REQ-CHAIN-006) — every code site that reads the variable references the constant, never the inline string. The REQ-CHAIN-021 two-phase backfill: a skeleton node created at spawn is backfilled with the child's runtime-assigned `session_id` via a `node-update` event when the child's SessionStart fires — the precondition for REQ-CHAIN-013's `(worktree_path, session_id)` re-resolution after `/clear`.
  - **M2 — `moai chain` CLI subcommands (`internal/cli/chain.go`).** Five subcommands registered under a new `chain` parent cobra command: `moai chain status` (current node summary: depth, parent, spec, milestone, resume_target — REQ-CHAIN-007), `moai chain lineage` (root-to-leaf origin chain with each ancestor's path/spec/milestone/entered_at — REQ-CHAIN-008), `moai chain back` (parent node's resume_target + resume_command — REQ-CHAIN-009), `moai chain list` (all nodes with depth, session_id, staleness status — REQ-CHAIN-010), `moai chain prune [--dry-run]` (fold old exited nodes into archive — REQ-CHAIN-011). Staleness classification (`classifyStaleness`) overlays on the session registry's `LastHeartbeat` via a read-only join (threshold 15 min, REQ-CHAIN-015); the registry `Entry` struct is NEVER mutated (REQ-CHAIN-014 overlay-only). Single-host v1 limitation surfaced when a remote CWD is detected (REQ-CHAIN-016).
  - **M3 — SessionStart lineage banner (`internal/hook/chain_banner.go`).** `chainLineageBanner` is wired into the existing `sessionStartHandler.Handle` flow (`session_start.go:361`). When `MOAI_CHAIN_NODE_ID` is absent from the environment (post-`/clear` env loss), the handler resolves the node from the ledger via `(worktree_path, session_id)`, re-injects the node ID, and emits a lineage system-reminder containing depth, an abbreviated parent chain (N0→N1→N2), spec, milestone, last-completed, and resume_target (REQ-CHAIN-013). The handler is time-boxed via `context.WithTimeout` (2 s) and degrades to a no-op banner on timeout — fail-open (REQ-CHAIN-013 time-box + AC-CHAIN-014).
  - **M4 — mechanical completion hook (`internal/hook/chain_event.go` + `.claude/hooks/moai/chain-event.sh`).** `RunChainEvent` reads a SubagentStop hook payload from stdin, resolves the child node, and appends a `completion-edge` event `{parent_node, child_node, completed_milestone, completed_at, next_resume_target}` to the ledger without requiring any leaf-agent cooperation — the ledger stays current even if the spawning session has crashed or been cleared (REQ-CHAIN-012). The hook is fail-open: any error (missing store, unresolvable node, malformed payload) is a silent no-op (exit 0). The shell wrapper `chain-event.sh` resolves the `moai` binary across 3 tiers and always exits 0.
  - **M5 — compaction / prune (`internal/chain/prune.go`).** `Store.Prune` folds exited nodes older than the threshold (default: 30 days OR 10 MB, whichever fires first) into a `node-summary.json` snapshot under `.moai/state/chain/archived/`, backs up the original `events.jsonl` to `archived/events-{timestamp}.jsonl`, and rewrites the active file with only kept events (REQ-CHAIN-011). This is the ONE exception to the append-only invariant — a maintenance compaction invoked only by the explicit user-facing `moai chain prune` command. `--dry-run` is the default (no files modified).
  - **M6 — template-mirror + `make build` + count fixes.** The `chain-event.sh` hook wrapper is mirrored to `internal/template/templates/.claude/hooks/moai/chain-event.sh`, and `make build` regenerates the embedded catalog. The template source passes the neutrality CI guard (no SPEC IDs, no commit SHAs, no macOS-bias paths — REQ-CHAIN-019 / AC-CHAIN-020).
  - **Overlay join, Entry frozen (REQ-CHAIN-014 / AC-CHAIN-024 MUST-PASS).** The chain ledger NEVER mutates the session registry `Entry` struct. Lineage data is overlaid by joining on `SessionID` at read time only, respecting the REQ-COORD-024 schema freeze. `Entry` retains exactly its 8 frozen fields — zero chain-specific fields (ChainNodeID, OriginChain, Depth, ParentNodeID, ResumeTarget) are present.
  - **Flag-agnostic + subagent boundary (REQ-CHAIN-017 / REQ-CHAIN-018).** The chain record, store, CLI, and hook function independently of `-k`, `-f`, and the `manager-lead` agent — `grep` for these tokens in chain scope returns 0 matches (AC-CHAIN-018). The chain CLI and hook do NOT invoke `AskUserQuestion` or `mcp__askuser__*` — the orchestrator-only interaction boundary is preserved (AC-CHAIN-019).
  - 24 acceptance criteria (AC-CHAIN-001..024: 14 MUST + 6 SHOULD + 4 NICE-TO-HAVE), all PASS, verified against the run-phase implementation at HEAD `10988db68` of the worktree (per-milestone commits M0 `b7641cc7f` → M1 `ecb41536c` → M2 `3a4b2ca34` → M3 `2781048e3` → M4 `cd70d72b5` → M5 `82b9b87a6` → M6 `bd0a34f37`; progress evidence at `10988db68`). Coverage: `go test -cover ./internal/chain/` → **87.4%** (≥85% threshold). Orchestrator trust-but-verify: `go build ./...` exit 0; cross-platform build (`GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...`) exit 0; `go vet ./internal/chain/... ./internal/cli/... ./internal/hook/...` clean. MX tags (sync sub-step): `@MX:ANCHOR [AUTO]` on `WorktreeNode` (REQ-CHAIN-001 13-field invariant contract), `NewStore` (3 cross-package callers), and `Populator.ResolveCurrentNode` (5 cross-package callers) — all carrying `@MX:REASON`. - **[SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-DISCRIM-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-DISCRIM-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **Corrects the Main-Checkout Branch-State Guard discriminant so a Claude-native worktree is no longer misclassified as the primary checkout.** Before this, the guard compared the project root (resolved via the primary-checkout path) against the git-common-dir, so a PreToolUse Bash event fired inside a worktree still resolved to the primary checkout dir and tripped BRANCH_GUARD_VIOLATION on legitimate worktree `git` operations (a rebase or force-push was refused inside the worktree that owns it). The parent SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-001 landed the v1.1.0 mechanical enforcer and SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-OPTIN-001 added the opt-in gate + pattern refinement; this SPEC owns the directory-correction seam.
  - **M1 — Seam A discriminant queries `input.CWD`, not the project root (REQ-WBG-D-001 / AC-WBG-D-001..008).** `internal/hook/branch_guard.go` `isPrimaryCheckout` now receives the per-event CWD (`input.CWD`, reliably populated by Claude Code for PreToolUse:Bash) and compares the absolute `git rev-parse --git-dir` resolved AT THAT CWD against the absolute `git rev-parse --git-common-dir`; equal paths classify as the primary checkout, differing paths as a worktree. The audit-log dir stays pinned to the primary checkout (REQ-WBG-D-004) so worktree events still record against the shared audit log. New tests `TestBranchGuard_Worktree_Allow` / `_Primary_Still_Denies` / `_AuditLog_Placement` / `_FailOpen_NonGitCwd` / `_Exempt` / `TestIsPrimaryCheckout_Worktree` prove the discriminant admits worktree operations while the primary checkout still refuses branch-state mutations (`git` + `switch`, `checkout`, `reset --hard`, `rebase`). Existing branch-guard tests (Latency, CheckBranchStateOrigin, DeniesGitSwitchInPrimary, FailOpenOnGitError, ConfigGate_FlagFlips) PASS after the existing-test CWD-contract was updated to match Seam A.
  - **E7 blocker: NONE.** The suspected third seam (a path-resolver preferring `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` over `input.CWD`) was ruled out: `resolveProjectRootFromInputOrEnv` (path_resolve.go:88) prefers `input.CWD` exactly as plan.md §C claims. `input.CWD` IS reliably populated for PreToolUse:Bash (proven by the passing worktree-allow test).
  - **E8 RED gap (documented).** The verbatim pre-GREEN failing-test output was NOT captured: a transient 429 rate limit terminated manager-develop after it authored `branch_guard_worktree_test.go` but before it ran the test against the pre-fix code; the orchestrator then implemented Seam A directly (GREEN-first). The RED driver is documented in the test doc comment; residual-risk: without a captured RED, test-first falsifiability rests on the test's stated logic rather than an observed pre-fix failure output.
  - 8 acceptance criteria (AC-WBG-D-001..008, all MUST), all PASS, verified against the run-phase implementation at PR #1488 squash `61e2c3bc0` on origin/main. - **[SPEC-MCP-CONSOLE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-MCP-CONSOLE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The `moai web` console now exposes the full MCP surface — per-tool enablement for all 17 tools, codex auth state, and GLM key state — in the same schema-driven console the rest of the project settings already use, in the user's own language, with no credential written to any git-tracked file.** Before this, SPEC-A provisioned the `moai` MCP server by default and SPEC-B wired agents to its tools, but the server's `registerMoaiMCPTools` had NO per-tool gating seam (a console toggle written today would be read by nothing), the console had no MCP section at all, and the only way to see which tools were live or whether codex was authenticated was to hand-edit JSON or shell out to a CLI. SPEC-C closes the Epic's user-facing loop: the console is now the single place to see and control the entire MCP surface, and every control writes through seams the runtime actually reads. This is the third and final SPEC of the moai-MCP integration Epic (A: server default-on → B: agent wiring → C: console surface).
  - **M1 gating seam — a control that actually controls (REQ-C-2 / AC-C-004).** A new shared declaration `internal/mcp/catalog.go` (`ToolDef{Name, WriteCapable}` + `MoaiMCPTools()`, @MX:ANCHOR-tagged) is the single source of truth for the 17-tool surface, consumed by BOTH the server (registration) and the settings schema (per-tool fields). `registerMoaiMCPTools(s, projectDir)` now reads per-tool enablement once at construction via `readMCPToolEnablement`, and each `add(name, tool, handler)` is gated on `enabled[name]` — a disabled tool is structurally ABSENT from `tools/list`, not merely marked inactive. The reader is fail-OPEN (all-enabled default per owner decision D2), the inverse posture of the codex gates' fail-CLOSED readers, so a missing or malformed `mcp.yaml` never silently disables the server. `TestMoaiMCPServer_RegistrationMatchesCatalog` asserts set-equality between tools/list and the catalog (REQ-C-1 single-declaration guard — a tool added to registration without a catalog entry fails the build).
  - **M2 console surface — all 17 tools, write-capable distinguished in text (REQ-C-1 / REQ-C-3 / AC-C-001..003 + AC-C-005).** A new `SectionMCP` section ID (`internal/settings/schema.go`) routes through the SAME schema-driven form + `yamlpatch` seam `workflow.branch_guard.enabled` and the audit-selection fields already use (`internal/settings/sectionroute.go` / `sectionwrite.go`); `mcpFields()` generates one `mcp.tools.<name>.enabled` bool per catalog tool — there is no second tool list. The `fieldsetMCP` templ component (`internal/web/fieldsets.templ`) renders one control per tool, and the four write-capable tools (`goal_arm`, `verify_snapshot`, `codex_task`, `codex_job_cancel`) carry a marker carried in TEXT (not color alone), so the blast-radius difference is legible at the point of decision and survives a monochrome or screen-reader rendering. `TestMCPConsoleRendersAllTools` / `_ToolCountMatchesCatalog` / `_WriteCapableTextDistinctinction` PASS.
  - **M3 codex auth — probe state surfaced, NOT recomputed (REQ-C-4 / REQ-C-5 / REQ-C-6 / AC-C-006..009).** `internal/cli/mcp_codex.go` exports `ProbeCodexSetup(ctx)` returning a typed `CodexSetupResult`; `handleCodexSetup` delegates to it. The classifier (`classifyCodexAuth`) is untouched — consumed, not forked. `internal/web/codex_state.go` carries the `CodexStateView` view model + a fail-open default probe for bare test apps; `internal/web/handlers.go` / `app.go` / `server.go` wire it via DI; `internal/cli/web.go` adapts the probe for web consumption without importing `internal/cli`. The `codexAuthBlock` templ component renders installed/binary/version/auth_provider, surfaces the `codex login` command as remediation when unauthenticated (NO in-console OAuth flow, NO browser launch — REQ-C-5), degrades gracefully when codex is absent, and offers the review-gate + write-mode opt-in toggles through the same seam the fail-closed readers (`readCodexReviewGateEnabled` / `readCodexTaskAllowWrite`) consume — one source of truth, no parallel key. Grep guard: `grep -rn 'classifyCodexAuth\|codex login status' internal/web/ --include=*.go | grep -v _test.go` → 0 matches (no second auth-classification implementation in web).
  - **M4 GLM key state — glmkey.go reused byte-identical (REQ-C-7 / AC-C-010 / AC-C-011).** The `glmKeyStateBlock` templ component renders the GLM key STATE only inside the MCP section: a configured/not-configured boolean + the bounded trailing-four hint. It consumes `view.GLMKeyConfigured` / `view.GLMKeyHint` already populated by the pre-existing `populateGLMKeyHint` → `computeGLMKeyHint` path authored by SPEC-GLM-KEY-INPUT-001. No second credential path, no submit, no reveal (those stay in the 3rd Party LLM section). `git diff ed70e4354 -- internal/web/glmkey.go` → 0 lines changed (byte-identical); `TestGLMKeyField_AbsentFromSchema` still PASSes (field absent from `settings.AllFields()`, preserving the C-C-2 structural anti-leak guarantee); `TestGLMKeyHint_TrailingFourOnly` + `_ShortKeyDisclosesNothing` still PASS — disclosure stays bounded on both branches (configured-boolean + final four only; for a key of four characters or fewer, no characters are shown).
  - **M5 secret-hygiene + i18n + no-fork sweep — test-only (REQ-C-8 / REQ-C-9 / REQ-C-10 / AC-C-012..014).** M5 introduces ZERO production-code changes — it is a pure characterization sweep (DDD PRESERVE) of invariants M1-M4 already established. NEW `internal/web/mcp_secret_hygiene_test.go` (4 tests) reflect-walks the view models to prove no credential field hides in `CodexStateView` or `pageView`'s GLM pair, asserts `glm_api_key` stays absent from `settings.AllFields()`, and that no `.templ` source references the `glmcred` package (closing the template-surface bypass). i18n coverage was already complete at M5 entry — M2/M3/M4 each added all 4 locales (en/ko/ja/zh) in the same change (17 + 10 + 5 keys); the full 14-test governance suite (`TestI18nKeySetParity`, `TestI18nKeyCoverageForward`/`Reverse`, `TestI18nUntranslatedValues`, `TestI18nAllowlistNoOrphans`, `TestI18nEndonymInvariants`, …) PASS — no M5 key fills needed. The no-fork guards (`mcp_audit_surface_test.go:47-95`) remain PASS and untouched.
