- **[SPEC-CONFIG-MODE-MIGRATE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CONFIG-MODE-MIGRATE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **A new `moai config mode-migrate [--apply]` CLI widens permission bits of `.moai/config/**` files back toward `defs.FilePerm` (`0o644`) — a dry-run-first, operator-approval-gated one-time migration that repairs the already-narrowed population the sibling `SPEC-CONFIG-ATOMIC-WRITE-001` (CLOSED) intentionally left alone.** `SPEC-CONFIG-ATOMIC-WRITE-001` shipped a `Stat`-based mode-*preservation* helper, which is correct for every future write but perpetuates the narrowed mode of any file already narrowed (a `Stat`-based fix faithfully preserves whatever mode it finds). This SPEC owns the one-time *widening* migration that the sibling explicitly deferred (parent `SPEC-CONFIG-TIER-PERSIST-001` REQ-CTP-025/026).
  - **Dry-run-first + `--apply` gate (candidate C design lock).** Default invocation is a no-op dry-run: it lists widening candidates (path + current mode → target mode) and modifies nothing. `--apply` performs the widening. The migration is never blind-widen and never announce-then-auto-widen — the operator reviews the dry-run list and explicitly re-runs with `--apply`.
  - **Only-widen predicate (spec.md §D.2).** `IsWideningCandidate(mode)` returns true only when `(cur|target)==target && cur!=target` — every bit set in the file's mode is also set in `defs.FilePerm`, AND the mode is not already equal. A file carrying a bit NOT in `defs.FilePerm` (exec bits, group-write, set-uid/gid, sticky — e.g. `0700`, `0660`, `0666`) is excluded, because setting it to `defs.FilePerm` would DROP that bit (a narrow, forbidden by REQ-MIG-002). The migration only adds bits; it never removes them.
  - **Scoped to `.moai/config/` only + symlink-safe (AC-MIG-004 / AC-MIG-008).** `ScanConfigDir` walks `.moai/config/` recursively; files outside that tree (e.g. `.claude/settings.json` at 0600) are untouched. Symlinks are `Lstat`-detected (NOT `Stat` — the symlink entry itself is inspected, never followed) and skipped, so `os.Chmod` can never land on an out-of-scope target. The symlink-skip AC (AC-MIG-008) was added in the plan-audit iter1 fix batch (D3 edge case).
  - **Idempotent (AC-MIG-005).** A tree already at `defs.FilePerm` yields an empty candidate list; `--apply` on an empty list is a no-op (exit 0, no marker file, no output noise). Re-running on an already-migrated tree changes nothing.
  - **Routes through the shared atomic-write helper (AC-MIG-006).** `ApplyWidening` re-reads each candidate, writes the content back unchanged via `atomicfile.Write` (the sibling SPEC's atomic + mode-preserving helper), then performs the single permitted `os.Chmod(path, defs.FilePerm)` — the AC-MIG-006 scoped exemption, because the atomic helper preserves the pre-existing destination mode and widening REQUIRES this post-write chmod. The chmod references the named constant `defs.FilePerm` (never a numeric literal — CLAUDE.local.md §14); bare `os.WriteFile` is never used. Verified: source contains `atomicfile.Write`; zero bare `os.WriteFile(`; single `os.Chmod(` site.
  - **New `config` parent command.** No prior `moai config` existed (diagnostics live under `moai doctor config`, which is read-only); `mode-migrate` is the first mutating subcommand under a new `config` parent, registered as a sibling of `doctor config`. The `configCmd` parent owns mutating config operations; future config-surface subcommands slot under it.
  - **Implementation path note.** Plan-phase §E matrix estimated `internal/cli/config/mode_migrate.go`; manager-develop found no prior `moai config` parent existed and placed the file at `internal/cli/mode_migrate.go` (the `config` parent is created inside `mode_migrate.go`, not in a separate `cmd.go`). Real deliverable: `internal/cli/mode_migrate.go` + `internal/cli/mode_migrate_test.go`.
  - 8 acceptance criteria (AC-MIG-001..008, all MUST), all PASS, verified against the run-phase implementation at HEAD `5817e9173` of the worktree (M1 single commit — Tier S, API surface + apply path + wiring collapsed into one cohesive unit). - **[SPEC-HOOK-PRETOOL-PERF-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-HOOK-PRETOOL-PERF-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **Collapsed the per-invocation fork+exec+full-config cost that produced 33 PreToolUse timeouts across 30 days, by adding a mtime+size-keyed config disk cache and a concurrent-stress profiling harness — and by landing the security regression test that binds any future fast-path to the destructive-primitive scan.** Tier M, P1, 10 ACs (9 MUST + 1 SHOULD deferred, all MUST PASS, AC-PERF-005 SHOULD DEFERRED — see below). Root cause was structural rather than a deterministic hot spot: every `PreToolUse:Bash` invocation forks a fresh `moai` process via `handle-pre-tool.sh` → `moai hook pre-tool`, and that fresh process loads the **full** merged config (~20 per-section YAML reads + parse + merge) even though the PreToolUse handler only consumes a thin slice (security policy, branch-guard flag, gate config). Under system load and/or concurrent hook execution the fork+exec+full-config cost amplified intermittently — rare enough to be invisible to a smoke test, frequent enough to stall real sessions. The 5→10s timeout widening (separate PR #1473) absorbed the spike as immediate mitigation; this SPEC is the ROOT fix that attacks the structure so the timeout can return toward 5s once per-invocation cost drops (REQ-PERF-010 gates the narrowing on production telemetry — it is NOT performed in this SPEC).
  - **M1 — Config disk cache, production-wired via `LoadWithCache` (`internal/config/cache.go` + `cache_test.go`, REQ-PERF-001..004 / REQ-PERF-008 / REQ-PERF-009 / AC-PERF-001..004 / AC-PERF-008 / AC-PERF-009).** On a cache hit (`.moai/state/config-cache.json` present + schema-version match + per-section mtime+size fingerprint matches every section file), the loader returns the cached config WITHOUT opening any `.moai/config/sections/*.yaml` file — verified by `TestCache_HitSkipsSectionParse`. On a cache miss (absent, corrupt, schema-mismatched, or fingerprint-invalidated), the loader falls through to the existing full `Load` path and writes a fresh cache atomically via `atomicfile.Write` (write-temp + rename in the same directory — REQ-PERF-008; concurrent-writer race guarded by `TestCache_AtomicConcurrentWrite`). Deletion of a section file present at cache-write time is an invalidation signal equal in force to an mtime change (REQ-PERF-003 / `TestCache_SectionDeletionInvalidates`). Corrupt or schema-mismatched cache fails open silently — no user-facing error, no non-zero exit — and is overwritten on successful re-merge (REQ-PERF-004 / `TestCache_CorruptFailsOpen` + `TestCache_SchemaVersionMismatchFailsOpen`). The cache lives at `.moai/state/config-cache.json` (fixed name, predictable for gitignore + cleanup — REQ-PERF-009 / `TestCache_LocationUnderStateDir`). Wired into `ConfigManager.Load` so every consumer benefits, not only the PreToolUse handler.
  - **M2 — Lazy config slice, implemented + unit-tested but DEFERRED from production wiring (`internal/config/slice.go` + `slice_test.go`, REQ-PERF-005 / AC-PERF-005, SHOULD — DEFERRED).** `LoadSlice` / `LoadWithCacheSlice` / `PreToolUseSliceSections` are implemented and `TestLoadSlice_StrictSubset` proves the slice reads only a strict subset of the section set. It is **NOT wired into the production `ConfigManager.Load` hot path**, and this is recorded transparently rather than as a silent PASS. Rationale (progress.md §E.2 M2-WIRING): on a cache miss, `LoadWithCacheSlice` would write a PARTIAL-section cache (only the PreToolUse slice's sections), which subsequent cache-HIT reads would then serve to ALL handlers — breaking non-PreToolUse handlers that need the unloaded sections. M1 (the full-load cache via `LoadWithCache`) IS wired and delivers the measured 93% max-tail reduction on its own; the lazy slice is a capability available for a future per-handler config-needs analysis, not a production optimization today.
  - **M3 — Concurrent-stress profiling harness + post-change measurement (`.moai/specs/SPEC-HOOK-PRETOOL-PERF-001/baseline.md` + `postchange.md`, REQ-PERF-006 / AC-PERF-006).** A new lightweight, env-gated timing collector (`internal/hook/perf/timing.go`) records per-phase wall-time (fork-exec / config-load / dispatch / total) and emits a JSON line to stderr when `MOAI_HOOK_PERF_TIMING` is set; when unset, every method is a no-op so the hot path incurs zero production overhead. The harness (`internal/hook/perf/harness_test.go`) spawns 8 parallel `moai hook pre-tool` invocations across 5 batches (40 total) against a fixture project. **Measured outcome (warm cache, post-change, committed in run `3b6ea0677`)**: config-load p50 dropped 1.26ms → 0.81ms, and the external max-tail collapsed from **894.21ms → 83.39ms — approximately a 91% reduction** under concurrent stress, far exceeding the plan's ≥30% p99-tail target (progress.md §E.2 records the campaign headline as ~93%). The residual tail risk (fork+exec amplification under OS scheduling contention, not config-load) is addressed by REQ-PERF-010: a daemon (C-2, explicitly out of scope per §E) would be the follow-up to eliminate the fork+exec cost entirely.
  - **AC-PERF-007 security regression test (`internal/hook/perf/security_regression_test.go`, REQ-PERF-007, make-or-break MUST).** A FORWARD GUARD that lands now even though NO fast-path is implemented in this SPEC: `TestAC_PERF_007_DestructivePrimitivesNotFastPath` enumerates each of the 13 destructive primitives in the Bash Risk-Amplifier Doctrine set (`rm -rf /`, `rm -rf ~`, `rm -rf .git`, `rm -rf node_modules`, `git push --force`, `git push -f`, `git push --no-verify`, `git commit --no-verify`, `git reset --hard`, `DROP TABLE`, `TRUNCATE` ×2, `chmod -R 777` ×2) as a separate case and asserts a hypothetical fast-path does NOT fire for any of them. `TestAC_PERF_007_CompoundSubcommandOverflowNotFastPath` asserts the same for any Bash command whose compound-subcommand count (pipe `|`, `&&`, `||`, `;`, backtick, `$(...)`) exceeds `BASH_SUBCOMMAND_SOFT_CAP` (5). `FastPathEligible` currently returns `false` unconditionally (no fast-path exists); when a future SPEC introduces one, it MUST implement `FastPathEligible` to return `true` ONLY for commands where `IsDestructiveOrComplex` returns `false`, or this test fails.
  - **Plan correction B5-CORR**: `plan.md` B-5 and C-PRE-4 referenced `internal/hook/observer.go` for the `$CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR`-first path-resolution helper. This file does NOT exist; the actual helper is `internal/hook/path_resolve.go` (`resolveProjectRootFromEnv` / `resolveProjectRootFromInputOrEnv`). M1 reused `path_resolve.go` verbatim; `observer.go` was never searched. Recorded here so an operator reading the SPEC body alongside the implementation is not misled.
  - 10 acceptance criteria (AC-PERF-001..010: 9 MUST + 1 SHOULD deferred), all MUST PASS with attributable evidence. Verified against the run-phase implementation at HEAD `3b6ea0677` of the branch (run commit: `3b6ea0677` M0-M3 single commit; `go build ./...` exit 0; `go test ./internal/config/... ./internal/hook/...` exit 0 across 14 packages; `golangci-lint run` 0 issues; coverage `internal/hook/perf` 96.4%, `internal/config` 80.9% package aggregate — new files `cache.go`/`slice.go` at ~85% function-level). This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on `spec.md` (the `draft → in-progress` intermediate was consolidated into the terminal close — run-phase began from `draft`), the `progress.md §E.4` audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (sync is markdown-only). §25 template neutrality N/A (`internal/config` + `internal/hook` Go sources + SPEC artifacts only — `git diff origin/main..HEAD --name-only -- internal/template/templates/` shows no template mirror touched by this SPEC). README / docs-site unchanged — internal `internal/config` + `internal/hook` performance fix with no user-facing command or surface change (the 10s PreToolUse timeout context is already documented in `internal/hook/CLAUDE.md`). `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync-SPEC-HOOK-PRETOOL-PERF-001` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot know its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit after the sync PR merges. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). **Out of scope (not in this SPEC)**: narrowing the 10s PreToolUse timeout back toward 5s — gated on REQ-PERF-010 production telemetry confirming p99 < 5s, and performed in a separate follow-up PR; a long-lived hook daemon (C-2) — a follow-up ONLY if the profiling milestone shows the cache+lazy approach insufficient. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-MCP-AGENT-WIRING-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-MCP-AGENT-WIRING-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The moai MCP server's 17 typed tools are now phase-scoped-granted to the 7 retained agents whose phase scope calls for them — each agent's `tools:` frontmatter CSV carries exactly the `mcp__moai__*` entries matching its phase, and nothing outside it.** Before this, only two agents (`plan-auditor.md:7`, `sync-auditor.md:9`) carried a single `mcp__moai__audit_multi` grant; the other ten retained agents had no declared access to the state tools the harness ships, so even with SPEC-A making the server present by default, the tools were unreachable from most agent contexts. This SPEC extends the established two-agent pattern to the full catalog and records the per-agent grant matrix as a deliberate decision (the 5 agents that receive zero tools is a documented outcome, not a gap).
  - **Phase-scoped grant matrix (REQ-B-1, M1).** `manager-spec` → `spec_progress, spec_audit, spec_drift`; `manager-develop` → `verify_snapshot, verify_trend, goal_status`; `manager-docs` → `spec_progress, spec_audit`; `plan-auditor` → `audit_multi, spec_audit, spec_drift, codex_audit` (retains its pre-existing `audit_multi`); `sync-auditor` → `audit_multi, verify_trend, audit_cache, glm_audit` (retains its pre-existing `audit_multi`); `manager-lead` → `session_list, goal_status`; `super-advisor` → `spec_audit, verify_trend`. The five non-granted agents (`manager-git`, `manager-design`, `e2e-tester`, `builder-harness`, plus the Anthropic built-in `Explore`) receive no MCP tool — each omission justified in `plan.md` §B.3.
  - **Exactly one write-capable grant (REQ-B-2 / AC-B-003).** Of the four write-capable tools (`goal_arm`, `verify_snapshot`, `codex_task`, `codex_job_cancel`), only `verify_snapshot` is granted — and exactly once, on `manager-develop` (whose run-phase work writes the verification baseline that later attributes its own claims — a deliberate pairing). `goal_arm`, `codex_task`, `codex_job_cancel` appear zero times across the catalog. `goal_arm` is deliberately omitted from `manager-lead`: arming a goal changes the session termination condition and belongs to the orchestrator downstream of Implementation Kickoff Approval — `plan.md` §B.2 states the reason and names the one-line change if the owner overrides (AC-B-004).
  - **Frontmatter-only, no Go change (REQ-B-5).** This SPEC edits only the `tools:` line of the seven granted agent files. No agent body prose, no tool handler, no schema, no Go code, and no `.mcp.json` is modified. The model/effort SSOT (`ResolveAgentModelEffort`) was measured complete across all four model-invoking tools at base — all four already resolve through it — and the existing structural guard `TestMCPAudit_NoDirectFrontmatterRead` (`internal/cli/mcp_audit_test.go:148`) already scans the entire codex session path (`openCodexSession` / `openCodexSessionOn` / the `threadParams["model"] = me.Model` assignment at `mcp_codex.go:566`), so REQ-B-6 is verification-shaped, not change-shaped: no Go test is added because the path is already structurally guarded (AC-B-008 / AC-B-009).

The mirrored copies carry no SPEC ID, REQ token, commit SHA, internal date, `/Users/` path, or `CLAUDE.local.md` reference — `TestTemplateNeutrality` + `TestInternalContentLeak` PASS (AC-B-011). Existing `tools:` entries are preserved in their original order; the `mcp__moai__*` additions are appended; `tools:` remains a CSV string, never a YAML array (REQ-B-4 / REQ-B-5 / AC-B-006).
  - **`manager-lead` depth-2 seal untouched (AC-B-007).** `manager-lead` is the sole `Agent`-carrying retained agent; adding MCP tools to it does not add `Agent` to its `tools:`. The depth-2 seal guard (`internal/template/manager_lead_depth_test.go`) continues to PASS — no agent other than `manager-lead` carries `Agent` in `tools:`.

- **[SPEC-MCP-DEFAULT-ON-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-MCP-DEFAULT-ON-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The self-hosted `moai` MCP server (the 17 typed tools registered by `registerMoaiMCPTools` — `spec_audit`, `verify_trend`, `session_list`, etc.) is now provisioned into every new project by default, the distributed template `.mcp.json` is reduced to a single neutral `moai` entry, and `moai update` no longer clobbers a user's own MCP entries.** Before this, the harness's own MCP server was gated behind an opt-in confirm that defaulted to "no" (so most users never received the tools the harness ships), and `moai update` silently overwrote any MCP entry a user had added because `.mcp.json` was omitted from the 3-way merge set on the false grounds that "MoAI no longer ships an MCP template". Both are fixed, and two completed SPECs whose requirements forbade the flip are amended in place.
  - **Provisioning gate flipped to default-on with an honored decline (REQ-A-3, M3).** The wizard question at `internal/cli/wizard/questions.go` is renamed `mcp_tools_opt_in` → `mcp_provision` and its `Default` flipped `"false"` → `"true"`, keeping positive polarity ("Provision the moai MCP server?"). `provisionMCPEntryIfOptedIn` → `provisionMCPEntryUnlessDeclined` (`internal/cli/init.go`); an explicit decline is honored silently (no `.mcp.json`, no stdout/stderr noise), and the write stays best-effort non-fatal (C-A-4).
  - **Template `.mcp.json` reduced to the single `moai` entry; repo-root carries four (REQ-A-1 / REQ-A-2, M2).** `internal/template/templates/.mcp.json` now carries exactly one active entry — `moai` → `{"command":"moai","args":["mcp-server"]}` with no `env` block — while the repo-root `.mcp.json` carries four (the three third-party entries `context7` / `chrome-devtools` / `playwright` plus `moai`). The two files **diverge on purpose**: the repo root is a maintainer working tree using the third-party tooling directly; the template is a neutral distribution artifact. The neutrality guard `internal/template/mcp_template_neutrality_test.go` is inverted (`mcpAllowedActiveKeys = {moai}`, count = 1), not weakened — the forbidden-token scan and the `${VAR}`-literal secret check are preserved byte-unchanged (REQ-A-9).
  - **`.mcp.json` added to the `moai update` 3-way merge set (REQ-A-4, M4).** `collectMergeableFiles` (`internal/cli/update_template_sync.go`) now returns `.mcp.json` alongside `.claude/settings.json` and `.moai/status_line.sh`, so a user's own MCP entries survive `moai update` instead of being clobbered. The false comment ("MoAI no longer ships an MCP template") is corrected. `TestMergeUserFiles_PreservesUserMCPEntry` proves both the shipped `moai` entry and a user-added `my-tool` entry survive the merge.
  - **Two completed SPECs amended in place to match the flipped contract (REQ-A-6 / REQ-A-7 / REQ-A-8, M1).** `SPEC-MOAI-MCP-SERVER-001` (REQ-MCP-002 gate inversion, REQ-MCP-015 opt-out flag, AC-MCP-002 / AC-MCP-006) and `SPEC-TREND-MCP-001` (REQ-TMC-003 default-on moai, REQ-TMC-001 active count 3 → 1, AC-TMC-001 / AC-TMC-004) are amended per owner direction: `status: completed` preserved, `amendment_of` omitted (the canonical transition pairs it with `in-progress`, which the owner held at `completed`), HISTORY `### Amendments` sub-section carries the record instead. Each amendment states its reversal rationale in prose adjacent to the amended criterion. The load-bearing invariants — REQ-TMC-002 secret hygiene, REQ-TMC-004 `$comment`-free clause, §25 neutrality — are preserved verbatim.
  - **Documentation surfaces reconciled (REQ-A-5, M5).** The three wizard locale strings (ko / ja / zh, `internal/cli/wizard/translations.go`) no longer carry "Opt-in default-off" or the `REQ-MCP-002` token; `.claude/rules/moai/core/settings-management.md` + its template mirror and the template `CLAUDE.md` §12 MCP inventory are restated for the single-entry default-on contract. The `internal/template/mcp_doctrine_parity_test.go` CI guard is inverted to pin the new "default-on" contract (REQ-A-5 cascade — a test asserting the old default is itself such a surface).

- **[SPEC-CONFIG-KEY-HONESTY-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CONFIG-KEY-HONESTY-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **Every config key shipped to a user in `internal/template/templates/.moai/config/sections/*.yaml` now either does what its name says or is explicitly classified as not — and a guard keeps it that way.** Before this, 287 distinct `yaml:`-tagged field names existed in `internal/config/types.go`, of which 174 had zero production readers (path-resolved measurement), and 161 of those unread keys still shipped to users as YAML — each one a silent promise that editing it would change behavior, when in fact nothing consumed it. The gap was invisible because no measurement bridged the Go struct surface to the shipped-template surface, and no guard prevented drift back after a fix.
  - **M1 triage rule + shipped-key inventory (REQ-CKH-001/002/003).** A new four-class classification rule (`.moai/docs/config-key-triage-rule.md`, dev-internal, NOT shipped to users) sorts each shipped key into **W** (Wire — has/blocks a real reader, route it), **P** (Prose-consumed — referenced by a shipped doc via its fully-qualified dotted path), **R** (Reserved — names a real intended capability, ship with the reserved marker), or **D** (Dead — no reader, no prose, no planned capability, remove from the shipped template). The rule is operationalized by a 952-entry path-resolved inventory at `internal/config/testdata/shipped_key_inventory.yaml` that maps every shipped YAML key to its classification + reader path (or absence).
  - **M2 path-resolved anti-rot guard (REQ-CKH-008 — `TestShippedConfigKeysHaveReaders`, `internal/config/shipped_key_reader_test.go`).** A CI-bound test reads the inventory, resolves every `X.F` selector to its receiver type, and asserts each non-P/R/D key has a production reader on the resolved path. The guard is path-resolved (a read counts only when the receiver is a `types.go`-declared struct), closing the false-positive seam the bare-field-name measurement left open. The P/R/D allowlists are explicit and individually justified — a future key that loses its reader is caught at PR time, not after release.
  - **M4 system.yaml resolution — unblind parity guard + `loadSystemSection` (REQ-CKH-004).** `internal/template/templates/.moai/config/sections/system.yaml` is now loaded by a real reader (`internal/config/loader_system.go::loadSystemSection`), and a parity guard (`internal/config/loader_system_test.go`) asserts every key in the shipped system.yaml has a corresponding `types.go` field — the same honesty contract the M2 guard enforces for the rest of the section set, extended to the one section the loader previously didn't read.
  - **M5 documented-but-unenforced reconciliation (REQ-CKH-009/010).** The template's `workflow.yaml` worktree toggles (`auto_create` / `auto_merge` / `auto_cleanup`) previously shipped as `true` while `internal/config/defaults.go` set the same knobs to `false` — a contradiction between "shipped default" and "compiled default" that silently inverted user intent. The template values are aligned to `defaults.go` (all three `false`), and each toggle's actual reader status is now stated accurately in `.moai/docs/local-dev-settings-intent.md §22.8` (auto_create has an advisory-wording reader; auto_merge / auto_cleanup have no reader; session_name_pattern is a declared-only placeholder).
  - **M6 neutrality-leak removal + E5 handoff (REQ-CKH-011/012/013).** Three internal-citation leaks in template comments (`SPEC-AGENT-ARCH-V2-001` × 2 in `workflow.yaml`, `issue #653` in `llm.yaml`) were removed while preserving the mechanism description verbatim — the comments still explain *what* the surrounding config does, just without naming the internal SPEC/issue that produced it. The pattern-coverage gaps the leaks exposed (no class covers `plan.md §D D6`-shaped artifact citations; `C6` matches `PR #N` but not `issue #N`) are recorded for sibling SPEC-UPDATE-CI-GUARD-001 at `.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-CI-GUARD-001/research-ckh-f6-handoff.md`; the existing `internal/template/internal_content_leak_test.go` guard was NOT edited (per ownership boundary).
  - **Report-once / delete-never posture over the merge engine (REQ-CKH-011 / AC-CKH-014 — PASS-WITH-DEBT).** `TestTemplateRemovedKeySurvivesUserConfig` (`internal/config/template_removed_key_test.go`) proves a template-removed D-class key + its user-set value survive a `backup.MergeYAML3Way` call (delete-never) and that the retained-key advisory names the key (reported-at-least-once). The cross-call report-once sub-clause (a second merge over the same tree emits no further advisory) is deferred to SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001, which owns the merge engine — recorded as a deferred gap rather than silently weakening the assertion.
  - **M3 intentionally skipped.** M3 (tier precedence over the config layer) depends on `SPEC-CONFIG-TIER-PERSIST-001` (E3) reaching `status: completed`; E3 is still in-flight, so M3 is held for a follow-up and NOT executed in this SPEC. The active milestone set is M1, M2, M4, M5, M6.

Verification: `go build ./...` exit 0; full `go test ./...` deferred to the sync-auditor phase per the close contract. - **[SPEC-NAVIGATOR-SYNC-005](.moai/specs/SPEC-NAVIGATOR-SYNC-005/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The BAS Epic M3 Falconer Fix layer now incrementally regenerates ONLY the stale doc subtrees an edit produced, via a split architecture: a deterministic Go CLI emits a draft-request, the orchestrator mediates an AI draft + a 4-option approval gate, and the Go CLI applies the approved draft behind an approval-token hard guard.** Before this, regenerating the Navigator graph after a code edit meant re-running the full `/moai project` chain (M0 graph-join + M4 tiers overlay + audit) — slow, and it touched every doc surface even when only one capability row had drifted. The Fix layer closes the BAS detect→route→fix loop (the CodeWiki `--compare-to <commit>` pattern adapted to a doc-graph): it takes the M2 work-items + M1 detect subtrees + the M0 graph context, computes a diff-scope, and drafts only the stale subtrees — a human approves, and the apply atomic-renames the approved draft into the live doc surfaces. The doc map stays a living document instead of degrading into a stale snapshot.
  - **Split architecture (Go + orchestrator-mediated AI, NOT Go-calls-LLM).** Layer 1 (`Run` in `internal/navigator/fix/request.go`) is the deterministic producer: loads the four read-only inputs (M2 work-items.json + M1 detect JSONL + git-diff + M0 nav-graph.json), computes the diff-scope, emits `.moai/project/navigator/fix-drafts/<draft-id>/request.json` + a stdout JSON signal — the `design.md §A.4` handoff contract the orchestrator consumes to spawn the AI draft (layer 2). The orchestrator then runs the AskUserQuestion 4-option approval gate. Layer 3 (`Apply` in `internal/navigator/fix/apply.go`) is the deterministic post-approval consumer: validates the `approval.json` token and atomic-renames the approved + scope-conformant subtrees into the live doc surfaces. The Go CLI never calls an LLM — `grep -rn 'AskUserQuestion\|mcp__askuser' internal/navigator/fix/*.go internal/cli/navigator_fix.go | grep -v _test.go | grep -v "// "` → 0 matches (AC-NS5-007a).
  - **On-demand Hidden CLI, not a PostToolUse hook.** `moai navigator-fix` is a Hidden cobra subcommand (sibling of `navigator-sync` / `navigator-route` / `navigator-tiers`), invoked from the `/moai project` surface. There is NO PostToolUse hook and NO `handle-navigator-fix.sh` wrapper — M1 already owns the real-time advisory surface; M3 is invoked on-demand after the audit + route chain has run. `--apply <draft-id>` is the post-approval mode (validates the token, then applies).
  - **Diff-scope = UNION(M1 detect subtrees, git-diff stale subtrees) ∩ graph-bound nodes (REQ-NS5-003).** `ComputeScope` in `internal/navigator/fix/scope.go` computes the set of doc subtrees whose graph node is touched by either the M1 detect JSONL or the post-baseline git-diff, filtered against the M0 nav-graph. The UNION semantics unify three earlier-artifact scopes into one diff-scope; the graph-bound filter excludes paths the graph doesn't cover (no spec orphan rows in the diff-scope).
  - **Fail-open across 8 error modes, ALL exit 0 (REQ-NS5-009 / AC-NS5-009).** work-items absent, detect absent, nav-graph absent, baseline unresolvable (non-git + no compareTo + HEAD~1 fails), unparseable JSON, schema-invalid input, empty diff-scope, no-LLM-runtime — each degrades silently to exit 0 with an empty or partial draft-request and one log line. The layer-1 producer NEVER aborts the calling `/moai project` step. (Distinct from the layer-3 token-refusal hard guard, which exits non-zero — AC-NS5-008d is NOT fail-open.)
  - **Approval-token hard guard (REQ-NS5-008 c4 / AC-NS5-008d).** `ComputeApprovalToken` in `internal/navigator/fix/apply.go` derives a deterministic 64-char hex SHA-256 over (draft-id + option + provenance). `Apply` validates the `approval.json` token BEFORE any live-doc mutation: no approval.json → `ErrApprovalTokenMissing`; tampered token → `ErrApprovalTokenInvalid`; live doc SHAs unchanged on both refusal paths. The token is the load-binding contract that makes the approval a real artifact, not "someone typed a command".
  - **Scope-conformance partition (REQ-NS5-013 / AC-NS5-013).** `ConformDraftToScope` in `internal/navigator/fix/scope.go` partitions the draft's subtree IDs against the diff-scope — in-scope subtrees apply, out-of-scope subtrees are excluded and logged to `.moai/logs/navigator-sync.log` with the `scope-conformance` marker. The draft OUTPUT must conform to diff-scope[], not just the Go engine source — the approval gate + apply both depend on this partition.
  - **Atomic-rename + idempotence + applied.json ledger (AC-NS5-008c).** Apply writes via `.tmp` + `os.Rename` (no stale `.tmp` left on failure) and records an `applied.json` ledger with Approver + git-committer-date ApprovalTimestamp + AppliedSubtreeIDs + ResultingLiveDocSHA. A second Apply on the same draft is a no-op (live doc SHA unchanged, ledger does not grow) — DBT-2 idempotence.
  - **≥50% automation rate, mechanically measured (REQ-NS5-010 / AC-NS5-010).** A deterministic in-tree 10-scenario fixture corpus (3 audit-missing × 3 audit-orphan × 4 detect; 3 action strategies; fixed per-scenario approvals) yields `AC-NS5-010 automation rate: 60.0% (6/10 drafts approved unmodified)` — 60.0% ≥ 50.0% floor PASS. No wall-clock, no math/rand (deterministic).
  - **Non-overlap with all predecessors.** M3 consumes M0/M1/M2/M4 + the audit chain READ-ONLY; `internal/navigator/{sync,detect,route,tiers}/`, `internal/hook/navigator_detect*.go`, `internal/mx/`, and the three predecessor Navigator chains are byte-unchanged by the M3 diff (AC-NS5-005a / AC-NS5-006 grep-verified).
  - **Template-first (AC-NS5-012).** M3 ships pure CLI + runtime Go — `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep '^internal/template/templates/'` → no matches (no distributed `.claude/` surface, no catalog regen required).
  - 13 REQ (REQ-NS5-001..013) + 20 AC (AC-NS5-001a through AC-NS5-013, including sub-variants a/b/c/d per acceptance.md §B traceability matrix), all PASS, verified against the implementation at run-phase completion (HEAD `066fc7b79` of `worktree-bas-m2-route`). Run-phase landed in six per-milestone Conventional Commits: `0e24dde06`-lineage (M3.1 scope + draft-request schema) + `c960c8f1e` (§F Phase 4 Mode Selection) + `09ae7f5d6` (M3.2 Hidden CLI + draft-request emission) + `ef215bd7a` (M3.3 LLM-client guard test, AC-NS5-007a) + M3.4 scope-conformance + preview (AC-NS5-008b/013) + M3.5 apply + non-overlap (AC-NS5-008a/c/d, AC-NS5-005, AC-NS5-006) + `ff765318d` (M3.6 fail-open 8 modes + ≥50% automation rate). Coverage: `go test -cover ./internal/navigator/fix/` → **87.5%** (≥85% target). Quality gate: full `go test ./...` green (post-#1456 merge resolved pre-existing baseline FAIL); `go vet ./internal/navigator/fix/... ./internal/cli/...` exit 0; `golangci-lint run --timeout=2m ./internal/navigator/fix/... ./internal/cli/...` → 0 issues; `go build ./...` + `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0; `go test -race ./internal/navigator/fix/` ok. **Coverage decision (accepted-as-is per run-phase residual-risk note):** the automation-rate corpus is an in-tree deterministic fixture (10 scenarios), not live manager-develop output — the 60.0% figure is attributable to the exact `go test … -v` invocation + its stdout; a live measurement after orchestrator integration MAY differ. MX tags (sync sub-step): `@MX:ANCHOR [AUTO]` on `ConformDraftToScope` (REQ-NS5-013 load-bearing partition), `ComputeApprovalToken` (REQ-NS5-008 c4 deterministic derivation), and `Apply` (AC-NS5-008c/008d sole live-doc write surface) — all carrying `@MX:REASON`; `@MX:NOTE [AUTO]` on `Run` (layer-1 entry point / public API boundary) and `ComputeScope` (REQ-NS5-003 UNION-semantics diff-scope) added in this sync commit. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on `spec.md` (sole YAML-frontmatter artifact — `acceptance.md` / `plan.md` use the markdown-header convention), the `progress.md` §E.4 audit-ready signal, the two `@MX:NOTE` additions in `internal/navigator/fix/`, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot know its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. - **[SPEC-CONFIG-ATOMIC-WRITE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CONFIG-ATOMIC-WRITE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **Every config and settings write is now atomic and mode-preserving through a single shared helper.** Before this, `atomicWrite` in `internal/config/manager.go` used `os.CreateTemp` (which creates the temp at mode 0600) + `os.Rename` with no `os.Chmod`, so every section save silently narrowed a 0644 config file to 0600; and the CLI writers (`harness.go`, `toolpolicy/{tier_render,codegen}.go`, `update*.go`) called bare `os.WriteFile` with a hardcoded `0o644` that ignored the destination's existing mode and left a partial-write window on crash. Both invariants — crash-atomicity and mode preservation — are now fixed at one choke point.
  - **One helper, every writer.** `internal/config/atomicfile.Write(path, data, defaultMode)` (new package) writes via temp-in-destination-dir + `os.Chmod(tmp, existingMode)` + `os.Rename`; existing destinations keep their pre-write mode (read via `os.Stat`), new destinations take the caller-supplied `defaultMode` (`defs.FilePerm` for regular config, `0o600` for a caller-declared secret). Routed through it: `manager.go saveSection` (Defect 1 — mode-narrowing), `harness.go:390` (Defect 2 — `0o644` hardcode + non-atomic), `toolpolicy/{tier_render,codegen}.go` (Defect 3 — `0o644`), and the `update{,_cleanup,_preserve_inventory,_wizard}.go` production writers (literal → `defs.FilePerm` constant, REQ-CAW-005).
  - **AC-CAW-002 is the load-bearing fix.** A table-driven mode round-trip over `{0o600, 0o644, 0o750}` asserts `os.Stat(target).Mode().Perm() == M` after each write — the 0o750 row is the explicit non-default mode that previously got narrowed to 0600 and now survives. RED was reproduced at both the helper and the `saveSection` level before the GREEN rewrite.
  - **Mutation-verified regression guard (AC-CAW-007).** `internal/config/atomicfile/guard_test.go` walks `internal/config/**` + `internal/cli/**` non-test source for bare `os.WriteFile` to config/settings paths and fails naming the offending site. A throwaway bare write inserted into `manager.go` made the guard FAIL with the exact location; reverting it restored PASS — the guard is not decorative. Six legitimate bare-write sites (backup-dir artifacts, an `O_EXCL` lockfile, a probe, a `mode`-var prior-art site, an injectable seam) pass the predicate naturally and are documented as exempt.
  - **Mode-widening migration deferred.** The parent `SPEC-CONFIG-TIER-PERSIST-001` REQ-CTP-025/026 (one-shot widening of already-narrowed `.moai/config/sections/*.yaml` back to `defs.FilePerm`) is out of scope here and deferred to follow-up sibling `SPEC-CONFIG-MODE-MIGRATE-001` — this SPEC ships the write-path invariant only (forward-looking); repairing historical damage is independently shippable on top. Split lineage recorded in the parent's `§L Split Branches` section.
  - 7 REQ (REQ-CAW-001..007) + REQ-CAW-004a, all MUST ACs PASS. Coverage: `internal/config/atomicfile` 81.8% (gap: the 3 error-wrapping branches need filesystem fault injection to reach; reported honestly rather than padded). Quality gate: `go test ./...` green except the pre-existing `internal/template` 2 baseline FAILs (zone-registry.md SPEC-ID/date leak — unrelated to this SPEC, present on baseline HEAD `ed70e4354`). `go vet` exit 0, `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0. plan-audit FAIL 0.74 → iteration-2 fixes (D1–D5) → PASS. Run-phase landed as `78acfb714`; this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `draft → completed` transition + this CHANGELOG entry. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-HARNESS-EVO-RUN-REPORT-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-HARNESS-EVO-RUN-REPORT-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The harness run is no longer mute about what it learned: a manifest can declare an improvement-signal slot, a Runner returns structured findings, the doctor surfaces a learning readout, and a post-run push step routes those findings to the orchestrator instead of waiting for a pull.** Four wiring points close the execution→learning gap that Epic Harness-Evolution 1/4 (PIPE-REPAIR) left passive — that predecessor repaired the observation→classify→propose pipe but never bridged the harness's own run into it. This SPEC is Epic Harness-Evolution 2/4.
  - **manifest `learning` block (REQ-HRR-001/002, M1).** `internal/harness/v4manifest/{schema,types,validate}.go` gained an optional `Learning` struct (`enabled` / `tier` / `confidence_floor` / `max_findings_per_run`). A manifest declaring it parses to a populated `Manifest.Learning`; a legacy 8-field manifest parses with `Learning == nil` and is NOT rejected (backward-compat preserved — AC-HRR-010). The block is a declaration slot, not a behavior toggle: it tells the harness "I emit improvement signals", and downstream consumers read it to decide whether to look for them.
  - **Runner findings contract + harness_run producer (REQ-HRR-003/004/007, M2).** `internal/harness/harnessrun/{types,proposal}.go` defines the standardized findings array a Runner returns, and `BuildHarnessRunCandidates` is the `harness_run:` producer — proposalgen's reserved-namespace third producer (a delegation-map sibling of `classify:` and `observe:`), mapping findings → `ProposalCandidate` rows for the Tier-4 application gate. The producer reuses the existing `ProposalCandidate` shape; it does not invent a parallel proposal path.
  - **doctor learning axis (REQ-HRR-005, M3).** `internal/cli/harness/doctor.go` carries a new learning readout that reads the manifest's `Learning` block + a Runner file's declared `findings` and reports whether the run→learning seam is wired. Three doctor tests (`doctor_learning_test.go` + the pre-existing doctor test quartet) pin the readout; the doctor is observation-only (no AskUserQuestion in subagent-domain code — C-HRA-008 grep clean).
  - **specialist findings emission + post-run push doctrine (REQ-HRR-006/007/008, M4 — doc-only).** The release-update specialist (`.claude/agents/harness/hns-release-update-specialist.md`) carries a mandatory `Phase 7.5` emission step — a 5-field findings payload (`{surface, kind, summary, confidence, suggested_tier}`) emitted before completion; `kind` reuses `harnessrun/types.go`'s vocabulary (`drift|gap|friction|defect`); `confidence` is grounded in the manifest's `confidence_floor` (0.70) and MUST NOT borrow `learner.go`'s `defaultConfidence` (1.0). The workflow doctrine (`.claude/skills/moai/workflows/harness.md` + template mirror) adds a `### 2.5 post-run push` step: non-empty findings → orchestrator collects → `harness_run:` producer → `ProposalCandidate` → Tier-4 AskUserQuestion gate; empty findings → silent proceed. The asymmetric boundary holds across both new surfaces — specialists and Runners return findings or blocker reports, they never call AskUserQuestion directly.
  - **§25 template neutrality (AC-HRR-009).** `grep -rn "HARNESS-EVO-RUN-REPORT\|REQ-HRR" internal/template/templates/` → 0 matches. The REQ-HRR-008 tag carried by the live `harness.md` is stripped from the template mirror; the dev-only specialist files and `hns-*-run.js` workflows are NOT leaked to the distributed tree. `internal/template/catalog.yaml` hash unchanged (`make build` not required — `harness.md` is not a catalog artifact; M4 verified empty catalog diff).
  - 10 acceptance criteria (AC-HRR-001..010: 8 MUST + AC-HRR-009 §25-neutrality + AC-HRR-010 backward-compat), all PASS, verified against the implementation at run-phase completion (HEAD `e7a6bd496` of `worktree-herr-plan-refresh`). Run-phase landed in four per-milestone Conventional Commits: `870cdd72a` (M1 manifest learning schema) + `cd88275fe` (M2 Runner findings + harness_run producer) + `4f22d97a6` (M3 doctor learning axis) + `e7a6bd496` (M4 specialist emission + post-run push doctrine). M5 was verification-only (no separate commit). **Coverage decision (accepted-as-is per GOOS approval):** `internal/cli/harness` at **80.9%** is below the §D.3 DoD 85% threshold — `doctor.go` (the code M3/M4 touched) is at high coverage; the gap is in non-doctor cmd code tracked as separate debt, NOT a doc-only M4 regression (M4 was 3 markdown files, zero Go delta). This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition on `spec.md` (the sole YAML-frontmatter artifact — `acceptance.md` + `plan.md` use the markdown-header convention), the `progress.md` §E.3 run_commit_sha backfill + M5 verification block, the `progress.md` §E.4 audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3) and backfilled by orchestrator in a follow-up commit.
