- **[SPEC-NAVIGATOR-SYNC-004](.moai/specs/SPEC-NAVIGATOR-SYNC-004/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The BAS Epic M2 Falconer Route layer now promotes Navigator audit and detect findings into actionable work items, each owner-bound to a code path instead of a person.** Before this, the 002 audit surfaced `missing`/`orphan` findings as advisory text and M1 Detect surfaced per-edit affected rows as an ephemeral `systemMessage` — neither named an owner, neither persisted as a work item, and neither told the engineer which code path was now their doc responsibility. The Route layer closes that gap: it reads three read-only inputs (audit-report.json + M1 detect JSONL + M0 nav-graph.json), promotes each finding into a 5-field work item (`source_kind`, `source_entry`, `owner_path`, `action`, `confidence`), and writes `.moai/project/navigator/work-items.{md,json}` atomically.
  - **Owner = code path, never a person.** Three resolution paths, one per `source_kind`: `audit-orphan` → `implementation_path` (high confidence); `audit-missing` → `@NAV:SYM` symbol's owning package via the M0 graph, else the design-doc `source.file` (medium or low); `detect` → `changed_path` verbatim (high). A person moves on; a code path stays — the falconer binding principle (design report §6 M2). The Route layer never calls `git blame`, never reads `CODEOWNERS`, and never emits an email or username in `owner_path`.
  - **On-demand Hidden CLI, not a PostToolUse hook.** `moai navigator route` is a Hidden cobra subcommand (sibling of `navigator-sync` / `navigator-tiers`), invoked from the existing `/moai project` surface. M2 does NOT register a PostToolUse hook — the audit itself is on-demand, so a real-time trigger would usually find no audit to promote; M1 already owns the real-time advisory surface. The engine lives in `internal/navigator/route/{promote,owner,write,run}.go`; the CLI registration is in `internal/cli/navigator_route.go` (+4 lines in `internal/cli/root.go`).
  - **Fail-open across every error mode.** Audit absent, detect state absent, nav-graph absent, unparseable JSON, schema-invalid input, owner-resolution error, all-inputs-absent, and timeout — each degrades silently to exit 0 with an empty or partial work-item set and one log line. The Route layer never aborts the calling `/moai project` step.
  - **Atomic-write + idempotence + provenance, no wall-clock.** `work-items.json` carries `route_commit_sha` (`git rev-parse HEAD`) and `captured_at` (committer date of that SHA) — no `time.Now()`-derived field, so two runs on the same HEAD with the same inputs produce byte-identical output. Carries forward M0's `atomicWrite` pattern and 003's `Provenance` contract.
  - **Non-overlap with all predecessors.** M2 consumes M0/M1/M4 + the audit chain READ-ONLY; `internal/navigator/{sync,detect,tiers}/`, `internal/hook/navigator_detect*.go`, `internal/mx/`, and the three predecessor Navigator chains are byte-unchanged by the M2 diff (AC-NS4-005a / AC-NS4-006 grep-verified).
  - **Route accuracy 83.3% (≥ 70% target), mechanically measured.** A fixture corpus of 30 input findings (6 missing + 12 orphan + 12 detect) yields 25 actionable work items (high or medium confidence) = 83.3%; even under the B5 heading-range fallback (3 medium → low), the floor is 22/30 = 73.3% ≥ 70%. The dual-arithmetic proves the ≥ 70% gate survives the fallback with 3.3pp headroom.
  - 15 MUST ACs (AC-NS4-001a..012, including sub-variants), all PASS. Route coverage 85.8%. Quality gate: `go test ./internal/navigator/route/...` 28 tests green, `go vet` exit 0, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0. Template-first: M2 ships NO distributed `.claude/` surface (pure CLI + runtime Go; AC-NS4-012 confirmed `git diff template` = 0). README / docs-site unchanged — `navigator-route` is Hidden, no user-facing command surface (consistent with NS1/NS2/NS3 predecessor precedent). `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-*` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3). Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-EPIC-STATUS-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-EPIC-STATUS-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **A new `moai epic status <prefix>` CLI computes disk-grounded epic progress from SPEC frontmatter + title `(Mx)` markers + an optional design report, printing the epic's milestone map (done/total/pct, each milestone's covering SPEC + status + `sync_commit_sha`) and flagging orphan milestones no SPEC covers.** Before this, the only "epic progress" surface was the Progress Board / Epic Status banner an agent rendered from its own working memory at output time — a `/clear` or session resume dropped the working-memory accumulator, and a sibling session's mid-milestone work was invisible to the orchestrator session. The producer is observation-only (no file writes, no persisted `epic.json`), composes the existing `spec.ListDocs` + `spec.Audit` read path that already powers `internal/web/board.go`, and emits a frozen-shape `--json` document so the Epic Status / Progress Board banner can be fed from disk instead of from agent recall.
  - **Three-strategy epic discovery, with strict precedence.** Stage 1 (`DiscoverEpic` in `internal/epic/discover.go`) glob-matches `.moai/specs/SPEC-<prefix>-*/` via `spec.ListDocs`; Stage 2 (`ExtractMx`) regex-scans each matched SPEC title for a `(<TOKEN> M<N>)` marker and builds the Mx→SPEC-ID map (first marker wins; later markers on the same SPEC fall through to `extra_mx`; the `--marker <token>` flag overrides the inferred token, which otherwise defaults to the mode across the matched set); Stage 3 (`ParseDesignReport` + `DiscoverDesignReport` in `internal/epic/designreport.go`) auto-discovers a `.moai/reports/<basename>-<token-lower>-*.html` design report by naming rule and parses its slice-table for the canonical M0..Mx list. The design-report parser is fail-open per KI-1 — a missing, unreadable, or reformatted report degrades to marker union only, never an error. Canonical Mx not covered by any SPEC are flagged `orphan_mx` (the BAS-Epic M2/M3/M5 case, the SPEC's motivating example).
  - **Per-Mx status join + aggregate counts.** `JoinStatus` + `classifyMilestoneStatus` (`internal/epic/status.go`) classify each milestone as `done` (owning SPEC `status: completed` AND non-empty `sync_commit_sha`), `in-progress` (owning SPEC `status: in-progress` OR `implemented`), `planned` (owning SPEC `status: draft`), or `absent` (canonical but uncovered). `computePct` returns `round(done/total*100)` with `total: 0 → pct: 0` (AC-ES-007 divide-by-zero guard, NOT a NaN and NOT an error exit). `BuildEpicStatus` is the top-level producer entry point that composes the three stages, the join, and the aggregate counts into the final `EpicStatus` view-model, recording the git HEAD SHA as `baseline_attribution` on every invocation (fail-open to `""` in non-git contexts per KI-6).
  - **Frozen `--json` shape (banner-SSOT feed) + Progress Board human rendering.** `RenderJSON` (`internal/epic/render.go`) emits the spec.md §B.1 contract — `epic` / `epic_token` / `milestones[]` (each with `id`/`label`/`status`/`covered`/`spec_id`/`spec_status`/`sync_commit_sha`) / `done` / `total` / `pct` / `orphan_mx` (omitted when no design report is available, per REQ-ES-005 + design.md §5 lock) / `extra_mx` / `untracked_specs` / `design_report` / `baseline_attribution` — with additive-only forward-compat (consumers MUST tolerate unknown extra fields; AC-ES-009). `RenderHuman` mirrors the Progress Board grammar at `.claude/output-styles/moai/moai.md:636-680` — `🎯 <epic> ▓▓░░░░░░░░ done/total (pct%)` header, per-Mx lines with status icons (🟢/🟡/⬜) per the legend at `moai.md:660-668`, the 4-locale translation table (`Epic progress:` / `에픽 진행:` / `エピック進行:` / `史诗进度:`), and the empty-match-set message `🎯 <epic> — no SPECs matched prefix '<epic>'`.
  - **CLI registration + non-interactive contract.** A new `moai epic` parent command group (registered in `internal/cli/epic.go`, surfaced in the `internal/cli/help.go` tools help-row at `{"moai epic", "Epic milestone progress"}`) carries a single `status` subcommand: `moai epic status <prefix>` with flags `--json`, `--design-report <path>`, `--marker <token>`, and `--base-dir <path>`. The CLI is observation-only per REQ-ES-010 / C-HRA-008: zero `AskUserQuestion` invocations, zero `bufio.NewReader` / `fmt.Scanln` / `ReadString` interactive prompts — it reads, prints, and exits (AC-ES-011 grep guard via `TestNewEpicCmd_NoAskUserQuestion`).
  - **Factory-lead notice touchpoint (REQ-ES-012).** The SessionStart factory-lead notice (`factoryLeadNotice` in `internal/hook/session_start_factory.go:82-86`) now carries a one-line pointer `Epic context: run \`moai epic status <prefix>\` for a disk-grounded milestone map.` so a factory session can surface epic context without leaving its current turn. Wiring the notice itself is owned by the Factory / Kanban Bootstrap SPEC family; this SPEC stabilizes only the CLI invocation target so the wiring SPEC has a stable surface to call.
  - 15 acceptance criteria (AC-ES-001..015: 14 MUST + 1 SHOULD), all PASS, verified against the implementation at run-phase completion (HEAD `4aa9178c3` of `feat/factory-bootstrap-guidance`). Run-phase landed in three per-milestone Conventional Commits: `bcee39540` (M0-M3 producer library — discover + designreport + status + render, 11 files + 1736 insertions) + `f0ca6897c` (M4 CLI verb registration — `moai epic status <prefix>`) + `4aa9178c3` (M5 factory-notice touchpoint + baseline attribution). Coverage: `go test -cover ./internal/epic/` → **87.6%** (≥85% threshold met). §25 template neutrality verified — producer Go source is NOT under `internal/template/templates/`; `grep -rnE 'SPEC-EPIC-STATUS-001|REQ-ES-' internal/template/templates/` → 0 matches (no mirror created). This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on `spec.md`, the `progress.md` §E.4 audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (sync is markdown-only). `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3 — a commit cannot reference its own SHA until after it lands) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. MX Tag validation (sync sub-step): 0 `@MX:` tags present in `internal/epic/*.go` + `internal/cli/epic.go` — `BuildEpicStatus` / `DiscoverEpic` currently have fan_in = 1 (CLI only), below the `@MX:ANCHOR` threshold of ≥3 callers, so no tags are warranted at this time; deferred to a future `/moai mx` scan once fan_in grows. 🗿 MoAI
- **[SPEC-FACTORY-BOOTSTRAP-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-FACTORY-BOOTSTRAP-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **`-f` / `--factory` is redefined from a one-session chain seed into factory membership, and a companion can now join an existing run from a second terminal.** Under the prior-art commit `94025ce0a`, a companion launched as `moai cc -f --name run-abc123` was misclassified as a lead (the dispatch short-circuited to the lead branch on any `-f` present, so a second chain was seeded); and a companion-shape `--name run-abc123` alone (no `-f`) entered companion mode silently. Both are corrected.
  - **`-f` is factory membership; `--name` shape selects the role.** `resolveFactoryBranch` (`internal/cli/factory.go`) is the four-row truth table the dispatch now consults: `-f` alone → lead; `-f --name <role>-<run-id>` → companion; `--name <anything>` alone → no-op. The dispatch in `cc.go` and `glm.go` parses both flags together instead of `else if`-ing past the companion branch, so a companion carrying `-f` reaches the companion branch. A lead sets `MOAI_FACTORY` + `MOAI_FACTORY_ID` + (when given) `MOAI_FACTORY_SPEC` and still seeds the `plan → run → verify → sync` chain (preserving `SPEC-FACTORY-MODE-001`); a companion sets only `MOAI_FACTORY_LABEL` and seeds nothing. **BREAKING from `94025ce0a`**: a companion-shape `--name` alone (no `-f`) is now a no-op rather than companion entry — companion entry requires `-f`.
  - **Inbound cross-session messages auto-accept via a transient `--settings` file.** `prepareFactorySettings` (`internal/cli/factory_settings.go`) writes `{"crossSessionInbound": "accept"}` to a session-private temp file (`os.TempDir()/moai-factory-<pid>-<nanosecond>.json`, `0600`, cleaned up on exit) and appends `--settings <file>` to the backend's argv. The `--settings` flag is documented to take the strictest merge, which is exactly the property needed: the accept/hold/refuse tier ladder is satisfied regardless of the operator's project/local settings, without mutating any persistent file. When the operator supplied their own `--settings`, moai injects nothing and the notice prints a verify-advisory instead (REQ-FB-007). A write failure degrades fail-open to launching without the injected flag (C8/EC-4).
  - **The SessionStart bootstrap notice is revised.** The lead notice (`factoryLeadNotice` in `internal/hook/session_start_factory.go`) now carries, in order: the run id; the four companion launch lines each carrying `-f` (so the operator copies a factory-membership command, not the bare `--name` form the prior-art notice printed); the leader socket path (`MOAI_FACTORY_LEAD_ADDR`, captured by `enterFactoryMode`); a one-line inbound-automation notice stating auto-accept is active via the injected `--settings` (or the verify-advisory when the operator supplied their own); and the SPEC identifier only when `MOAI_FACTORY_SPEC` is set. The companion notice (`factoryCompanionNotice`) is now **role-less** — it acknowledges the join and does not print "as the Y companion", removing the collision with the role-declaration contract the deferred `SPEC-KANBAN-BOOTSTRAP-001` `REQ-KS-006` owns. A malformed label degrades to the empty string (fail-open).
  - **CLI help documents both entry forms.** `moai cc --help` and `moai glm --help` now carry `-f [SPEC-ID]` (lead, seeds the chain) and `-f --name <role>-<run-id>` (companion, joins an existing run) in the `Use:` field and the `Long:` Flags prose, enumerating the four roles `plan`, `run`, `review`, `sync`. Both commands keep `DisableFlagParsing: true` (they forward arbitrary args to the backend), so the documentation lives in `Use:` / `Long:` and NOT in a `cmd.Flags()` registration — a flag registration under disabled parsing is silently inert and misleads readers about the parse site (REQ-FB-014, SHOULD).
  - **New docs-site Factory Mode page in 4 locales.** `docs-site/content/{en,ko,ja,zh}/multi-llm/factory-mode.md` (frontmatter `title` / `weight: 30` / `draft: false` each) + a `factory-mode` sub-entry under the existing `multi-llm` section in `data/menu/main.yaml` (4-locale `name` map + `ref: /multi-llm/factory-mode`). No new docs-site section, no `_meta.yaml` weight, no `menu.html` SVG case (the `multi-llm` icon already exists in the switch).
  - The block-cap raise (`launcher_blockcap_infinite.go`) continues to fire for both branches — `MOAI_FACTORY` (lead) OR `MOAI_FACTORY_LABEL` (companion) — preserving the prior-art unconditional behavior that `SPEC-FACTORY-MODE-001` REQ-FM-023 / `SPEC-INFINITE-GOAL-001` depend on; the two `TestAC003_*` preserve-tests stay green.
  - 27 acceptance criteria (AC-FB-001..027: 25 MUST + 1 SHOULD + 1 meta), all PASS, verified against the implementation files at HEAD `89227add0` of `feat/factory-bootstrap-guidance`. Run-phase landed in per-milestone commits `d3f8ffcda` (M1 settings injection) + `1b02bcf80` (M2 `-f` redefinition + flat dispatch) + `884e8e2dc` (M3 notice revision) + `c346682a2` (M4 CLI help) + `a87e6174b` (M5 docs-site 4-locale) + `89227add0` (M6 verification + §E.2/§E.3 evidence). `94025ce0a` is the prior-art commit this SPEC revises (NOT reverts). §25 template neutrality verified — `grep -rnE 'SPEC-FACTORY-BOOTSTRAP-001|REQ-FB-|94025ce0a' internal/template/templates/` → 0 matches; the docs-site page is NOT a template path and carries no SPEC/REQ/SHA tokens. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on `spec.md`, the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — no further code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3; Route B squash merge means the final SHA is known only after PR merge) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI
- **[SPEC-CODEX-PHASE2-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CODEX-PHASE2-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **An MCP host can now hand a coding or investigation task to codex and then watch it, collect it, or stop it.** Before this, the codex app-server client could only run one shape of request — a review — and only for moai's own audit path. Four tools open it up, registered in `registerMoaiMCPTools` (`internal/cli/mcp_server.go`) with a declared JSON Schema and an explicit read-only hint each: `codex_task` returns the finished output when `background` is false and a job id when it is true; `codex_job_status` and `codex_job_result` read that job's record; `codex_job_cancel` stops a running turn. The hints are set by value rather than inherited — true for the two readers, false for the task and cancel tools — and they are *declarations to the host*, not enforcement.
  - **The session is reusable, and the model finally reaches the wire.** `openCodexSession` (`internal/cli/mcp_codex.go`) owns `initialize` → `thread/start` once and hands back a handle that drives many turns on the retained thread. `runCodexReviewRPC` survives as a thin caller, so the Stop-hook review gate and `codex_audit` keep their exact `(ReviewOutput, error)` contract and their fail-open summaries. Separately, the model and effort resolved by `template.ResolveAgentModelEffort` used to be computed and then dropped before transmission; they now ride the params codex actually receives, per-method (a turn carries model + effort, `thread/start` carries model, `review/start` carries neither). A model codex cannot serve is dropped together with its paired effort, so a project that never opts in transmits exactly what it did before.
  - **Jobs are durable, redacted, and bounded.** Each job is one JSON file under `.moai/state/codex-jobs/` (`internal/cli/codex_jobs.go`) written atomically, so a concurrent reader sees the previous record or the next one and never a partial write — no shared index, so two sessions on one checkout do not contend. The request summary is redacted for bearer tokens and secret-shaped key/values *before* it is truncated to 500 characters, because truncating first can split a secret and leave a usable prefix. The record carries no env, no argv, and no binary path. One `codex_task` turn is bounded by a new 600 s default (`internal/config/defaults.go`), deliberately distinct from the review gate's 900 s so tuning one does not silently move the other.
  - **Cancellation targets one process and refuses when it cannot prove ownership.** `codex_job_cancel` (`internal/cli/codex_job_control.go`) sends `turn/interrupt` with the `{threadId, turnId}` the record captured mid-flight, waits a bounded 5 s grace, then terminates the codex process this server spawned for that job — a single kill on a pid read from the live session's own connection, never a name or pattern sweep. Ownership is decided by live-session membership rather than by the pid stored in the record, so a stale or tampered record cannot direct a signal anywhere; a non-terminal record with no live session is refused. A job whose `turn/started` was never observed has no turn id and degrades to process termination instead of sending a malformed interrupt.
  - **Writing the working tree is opt-in and ships off.** New nested config key `workflow.codex.task.allow_write` (`internal/config/types.go`, default `false` in `internal/config/defaults.go`), read fail-closed — a missing file, a parse error, or an absent block all read as not opted in — and inspectable through `codex_setup`'s result, which is why it is a config key rather than an env var. It was deliberately **not** added to the distributed template config, so no user receives a key to flip by accident. Note what the gate does and does not do: it bounds what moai *requests* of codex, not what codex *enforces*. A `sandboxPolicy` is transmitted on every turn including read-only ones, because the protocol makes the field sticky on the thread and an omitted field would inherit a write-enabled policy from an earlier turn.
  - **Evidence, stated at its real strength.** 18 acceptance criteria (AC-CX2-001..018), all MUST, all recorded PASS. Five of the protocol behaviours were confirmed against a live `codex-cli 0.146.1` session: a second turn on an existing thread is accepted; `turn/started` arrives and its `turn.id` is accepted as `turn/interrupt`'s `turnId`, ending the turn `interrupted` in 152 ms; the `{"type":"readOnly"}` sandbox envelope is accepted and its stickiness is real; `review/start` does emit `turn/started`; and the approval-stall fix returns `turn/completed` with the write denied. **Everything else is a reading of codex's generated JSON Schema**, including the `{"decision":"decline"}` payload shape and the choice of `-32601` for unrecognized approval requests. `resume_last` continues the last recorded thread via `thread/resume`, but re-opening a thread from a *new process* is untested. `item/commandExecution/requestApproval` and `item/permissions/requestApproval` fall to the `-32601` arm, which unblocks the turn but is coarser than the `decline` their schemas would accept; whether codex handles that as gracefully is not established.
- **[SPEC-HARNESS-LEARNING-EVO-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-HARNESS-LEARNING-EVO-001/spec.md)** + **[SPEC-HARNESS-LEARNING-EVO-002](.moai/specs/SPEC-HARNESS-LEARNING-EVO-002/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The harness can now learn which agents actually handle which `/moai` subcommand, from observation instead of hand-tuned YAML.** MoAI routes each subcommand to a default set of agents through `.moai/config/sections/delegation.yaml`, whose own header declares a learning loop — observe real runs, propose amendments, never auto-apply. The *observe* half had never worked, for two independent reasons, both found by measurement rather than by reading code.
  - **The routing ledger recorded nothing.** `.moai/state/routing-ledger.jsonl` held four rows accumulated over a month, every one of them with an empty delegation list. Two causes: the only path that created a row was an instruction the orchestrator had to remember on each dispatch, and the single store operation available (`Record`) closed the caller's own prior pending row as a re-route. Recording is now mechanical, derived from hook input at three seams — a prompt arriving, a subagent stopping, a session ending — and the store operation is split into create-if-absent and annotate, with `Record` keeping its original re-route semantics for explicit re-dispatch. Every emission path is fail-open, and while either harness observation gate is closed nothing is written at all: no row, no pending file, no ledger append.
  - **The terminal signal that closes a row was never produced.** Across 37,107 telemetry records, the number carrying a test-pass or test-fail classification was zero. The evidence writer's Bash branch was unreachable from the shipped wiring: the `PostToolUse` hook that reaches it is registered for `Write|Edit|MultiEdit` and never matches a `Bash` call. Test outcomes are now observed through the always-on observe channel scoped to Bash, so rows close as `success` or `fail` instead of accumulating unterminated. No `settings.json` or template change was required, and no evidence record is written twice for one tool call.
  - **A new analyzer reads those rows** — `internal/harness/delegationmap`, exposed as `moai harness delegation analyze` with `--json` and `--dry-run`. It aggregates, per subcommand, which agents were actually delegated, and raises two kinds of finding: an agent that clears both an observation-count and a support-ratio threshold but is not designated in the map, and a designated agent observed zero times. Agent identity is compared by exact match against the retained agent catalog, because a *named* spawn writes its name into `agent_type` — a spawn of `plan-auditor` named `audit-hle` is recorded and classified as non-catalog, never mistaken for a designation the map forgot. Delegations carrying no identity are counted separately and reported as an unattributed share, so a reviewer can see how thin the evidence under a finding is. Findings become drafts through the existing proposal writer; runs are deterministic and re-running over unchanged input churns nothing. An absent, empty, oversized, or malformed ledger is not an error — the command exits 0 with an empty result and a machine-readable reason.
  - **Applying a proposal remains a human decision, by design.** The analyzer only proposes. `delegation.yaml` is opened read-only and never written; amending it stays a Tier-4 decision the orchestrator takes with the user at the existing approval gate, and `.claude/lsel/frozen-allowlist.json` is unchanged, including the `^\.moai/config/sections/` pattern that freezes the map. Nothing shipped here can alter routing on its own.
  - **Known limits, stated rather than deferred.** Pass/fail detection reads the runner's output text, because the observe channel carries no structured exit code — a test runner whose output matches none of the known markers yields no signal, and its row closes later as `abort` rather than `fail`. One unreproduced dogfood run also produced two rows for a single session, consistent with a terminal signal arriving before the first delegation; it is filed as an ordering hazard rather than a coding error, and left open deliberately. Both are recorded in the SPECs' residual-risk sections.
  - Verification closed on a live run rather than on fixtures: a real session produced a ledger row carrying its delegation with the correct agent identity and a terminal `success` outcome. 32 acceptance criteria across the two SPECs, all passing, one of them at PASS-WITH-DEBT with the reason recorded in `progress.md`. Route B (PR-mandatory). 🗿 MoAI
- **[SPEC-INFINITE-GOAL-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-INFINITE-GOAL-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). The goal loop's 1M number was never a hard cap — it is the auto-compact window. This SPEC removes the THREE caps that actually broke "arm a goal and walk away" and adds REAL bounds, so an armed `/moai goal` now persists across auto-compact boundaries instead of being force-stopped by vestigial ceilings. Third and final P0 of the autonomy-workflow redesign (§3.2 v2 + §1.2 + the C2 finding). **NEW arm flags on `moai goal arm`** (`internal/cli/goal.go`): `--max-turns N` (0 = infinite, auto-compact-driven; default 30 unchanged for full backward compat), `--max-duration <sec>` (wall-clock seconds since arm time — the M-default PRIMARY run-time bound), `--cost-cap <value>` (recorded in `Ceiling`; enforcement is a documented follow-up because `Eval`/`Ceiling` carry no invocation/token accounting today). **Arm-time fail-closed (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-004 / AC-011)**: `--max-turns 0` with NEITHER `--max-duration` NOR `--cost-cap` is REJECTED at arm time (non-zero exit + stderr naming the missing bound + no goal file written) — no infinite goal runs without a real bound. **Run-time bounds (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-004 / AC-005 / AC-006)**: wall-clock fires a verdict indistinguishable from the MaxTurns ceiling (5-section Claim/Evidence/Baseline/Gaps/Residual-risk); the stagnation guard is strengthened from "same note 3×" to a mechanical-condition fingerprint (exit + output-hash + bounded file-set SHA derived from the condition commands' paths). **`CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP` handling (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-002 / AC-003)**: the doctrine (`goal-directive.md` + `workflows/goal.md`) surfaces the silent runtime cap (default 8, `min(30, 8) = 8` today) so users understand an infinite goal requires raising it (e.g. to 200); the `moai cc` / `moai cg` launchers inject `CLAUDE_CODE_STOP_HOOK_BLOCK_CAP=200` into the session env when a `--max-turns 0` goal is armed at launch time (best-effort, fail-open; one-line addition via the existing `buildEnvForLaunch` pattern). **Statusline `/clear` suppression (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-003 / AC-004)**: when a goal is armed for the current session, `internal/statusline/renderer.go` `handoffGuideStage` suppresses the soft/hard `/clear` directive markers (the stage is still computed for informational purposes), letting auto-compact handle context pressure instead of forcing a `/clear`; GoalArmed==false retains the current markers unchanged. **SessionStart(compact) re-inject (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-005 / AC-007)**: a new `matcher: compact` SessionStart hook reads the live armed goal + the active SPEC's `progress.md` tail and emits the goal condition, SPEC-id, last-verified mechanical state, and single next action — the official "re-inject context after compaction" mechanism (no-op when no goal is armed). **`/clear` auto-rearm (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-006 / AC-008)**: `moai handoff save` embeds any live armed goal's verbatim condition + arm-time `Ceiling` into the pending handoff record; `internal/hook/handoff_inject.go` `claimAndInject` writes a NEW goal state file under the NEW session-id on `/clear` (mode=auto), preserving session-id keying; the embedded Ceiling is re-validated before writing (an unbounded embed is rejected, the AdditionalContext injection still proceeds). **`ac_converge` doc correction (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-007 / AC-009)**: `.claude/skills/moai/workflows/run.md` "Max 20 turns" clause is NOT parsed by `parseCondition` (the `trailingExitClause` regex matches only `exits <N>`), so the `ac_converge` goal actually runs at `DefaultMaxTurns=30`; the doc now states the actual 30-turn execution (option (b) doc-only — option (a) adding the regex would have silently changed 30→20 for every user and contradicted AC-002). **deny/ask invariance (AC-010)**: an armed infinite goal does NOT weaken the deny/ask rules — verified by a table-driven regression guard (`TestAC010_DenyAskHoldsUnderArmedInfiniteGoal`) confirming policy decisions are identical across no-goal and armed-infinite-goal cells (policy is pure (cmd, policy); it does not read goal state). Eleven REQ (REQ-INFINITE-GOAL-001..007) / 11 MUST ACs (001..011), all PASS. Quality gate clean: `go build ./...` exit 0, `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0, `golangci-lint run --timeout=2m ./internal/goal/... ./internal/statusline/... ./internal/hook/... ./internal/cli/... ./internal/config/...` 0 issues on changed packages, `go test -race ./internal/goal/... ./internal/statusline/... ./internal/hook/...` all pass. **Infinite-loop safety trio (load-bearing)**: AC-011 (arm-time fail-closed) + AC-005 (wall-clock run-time bound) + AC-006 (stagnation halt) — all three green; no infinite goal runs without a real bound, and no unbounded arm is accepted. Run-phase landed in per-milestone commits on `worktree-autonomy-epic` (`b3a30c982` M1 arm flags + AC-011 fail-closed + `47277d1e4` M2 block-cap doctrine + launcher inject + `85a791efe` M3 statusline suppression + `040749776` M4 wall-clock + stagnation + `0407ef019` M5 SessionStart compact re-inject + `cfa601e2a` M6 `/clear` auto-rearm + `279831f37`+`1d3bd237e` M7 ac_converge doc-only correction), merged to `main` via PR #1317 (squash `adc867545`). **⚠️ USER-VISIBLE BEHAVIOR CHANGE — RESIDUAL RISK (statusline `/clear` suppression when goal armed)**: when a goal is armed, the `(⚠️/clear)` / `(🛑/clear!)` statusline markers are suppressed; users who relied on the marker to know when to manually `/clear` lose that signal while a goal is active. **Mitigation**: the stage classification is still computed (informational), only the directive text is suppressed; the goal's own per-turn output becomes the replacement signal; and the suppression is scoped to GoalArmed==true only (no-goal sessions retain the markers unchanged). **Cost-cap enforcement is a documented follow-up**: `Ceiling.CostCap` is recorded at arm time (per REQ-004 "recorded in Ceiling alongside MaxTurns") but its enforcement is deferred — the evaluator carries no invocation/token accounting surface today (OQ-2 resolution). An infinite goal is bounded by wall-clock (AC-005) + stagnation (AC-006), sufficient for "arm and walk away" but not cost-sensitive deployments. **Pre-existing baseline note**: `TestTemplateNoInternalContentLeak` flags `quality-gates-quality.md` matching `REQ-004` (from `SPEC-AUDIT-SNAPSHOT-001`, commit `630f0f44f`) — confirmed present at this branch's base before M1, NOT introduced by this SPEC. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on the sole YAML-frontmatter-bearing artifact (`spec.md`), the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, the README ×4 one-line note, and docs-site flag additions (`utility-commands/moai-goal.md` + `cli-reference/goal.md` across 4 locales) — no further code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-*` placeholder in this commit (self-referential-hazard workaround per spec-frontmatter-schema.md D3) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗺 MoAI
- **[SPEC-STOPCHAIN-TRIM-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-STOPCHAIN-TRIM-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Three stop-chain / per-edit / mode-token items from the autonomy-workflow redesign §3.5 + §3.1 (P0, second of the autonomy epic): **A10** stop-chain shell trim, **A8** per-edit hook consolidation, **A11** mode-aware hooks via the new `MOAI_AUTONOMY_TIER` mode token. **NEW `MOAI_AUTONOMY_TIER` env var** (`internal/config/envkeys.go` `EnvAutonomyTier`, `internal/config/defaults.go` 3-value enum `semi-auto | automatic | fully-autonomous`, `internal/config/autonomy.go` `AutonomyTier()` reader + `IsAutonomyTierCommitGateOff` / `IsAutonomyTierLifecycleDormant` branch-point predicates) — users opt into higher autonomy tiers; unset/empty/invalid falls back to `semi-auto` (zero behavior delta). **A10**: `handle-stop-goal.sh` exec's `moai hook stop-goal` ONLY when a goal state file exists for the current session (goal-absent precondition `exit 0` at the shell layer — a session with no armed goal pays ZERO `moai` cold-starts per turn-end); `sync-phase-quality-gate.sh` exec's its logic ONLY when the HEAD commit subject matches a sync-commit sentinel (non-sync HEAD → no `go vet` / `go build` / lint spawn); the 4 observer/security Stop hooks (`handle-stop.sh`, `handle-stop-failure.sh`, observer Stop, security Stop) transition to `async:true` advisory mode. **A8**: the `develop-pre-implementation` (PreToolUse, 5s) + `develop-post-implementation` (PostToolUse, 10s) per-edit spawns on every Write|Edit are removed from `manager-develop.md` frontmatter; the verification work moves to a per-cycle `develop-completion` Stop hook at milestone/commit boundaries — a TDD cycle of N atomic edits now pays ZERO per-edit spawns. **A11**: `sync-phase-quality-gate.sh` reads `$MOAI_AUTONOMY_TIER` and branches (fully-autonomous → advisory only / no `decision:block`; automatic → block ONLY on `go build` failures, advisory for lint/test/coverage; semi-auto → full blocking unchanged); `internal/hook/pre_tool.go` IsGitCommit gate (~L444) reads the tier via `config.AutonomyTier()` and is OFF at automatic/fully-autonomous (no synchronous vet+lint+test tax on commits); SubagentStop (`develop-completion`), TeammateIdle, and TaskCompleted lifecycle hooks are dormant at fully-autonomous (observe-only — audit-log written, no block / reject / `AskUserQuestion` translation). **⚠️ USER-VISIBLE BEHAVIOR CHANGE — RESIDUAL RISK (handle-stop.sh async transition)**: `handle-stop.sh` is now `async:true` (advisory) — the main Stop handler can no longer block synchronously; its result is delivered as `additionalContext` on the next turn rather than as a synchronous decision. Deployments that relied on synchronous `handle-stop.sh` blocking now get advisory behavior from that hook. **Mitigation**: the blocking Stop hooks (`sync-phase-quality-gate.sh`, `handle-stop-goal.sh`) remain synchronous — only the 4 observer/security Stop hooks transitioned. **deny/ask invariance (REQ-007)**: the destructive-pattern denylist (`git push` to main, secrets, `rm -rf`, deploy, etc.) remains binding at EVERY tier — `semi-auto`, `automatic`, `fully-autonomous`, and unset — verified by a 4×N table-driven regression guard (`TestAC006_DenyInvariantAtEveryTier`). The tier branch in `pre_tool.go` relaxes ONLY the synchronous verification gate, never the deny path; the `checkBashCommand` deny runs UNCONDITIONALLY after the tier branch regardless of tier (K-6 deny-before-tier ordering invariant, now `@MX:WARN`-tagged). Eight REQ (REQ-STOPCHAIN-TRIM-001..007) / 8 MUST ACs (001, 002, 003, 004, 005, 006, 006b, 007), all PASS. Quality gate clean: `go build ./...` exit 0, `go vet ./internal/hook/... ./internal/config/...` exit 0, `golangci-lint run --timeout=3m ./internal/config/... ./internal/hook/...` 0 issues, `go test -race ./internal/config/ ./internal/hook/ -count=1 -run 'TestAC00|TestAutonomyTier'` ok, coverage 80.7% (`internal/config`) / 84.3% (`internal/hook`), `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0. MX tags: `@MX:ANCHOR [AUTO]` on `AutonomyTier` reader (run-phase M1, fan_in ≥ 3 with `@MX:REASON`); `@MX:WARN [AUTO]` on `pre_tool.go` tier branch (K-6 deny-before-tier ordering invariant, sync-phase addition). **§25 template neutrality**: all run-phase shell-hook + agent-frontmatter + settings edits mirrored to `internal/template/templates/` byte-identical (the local `.claude/settings.json` vs template `.json.tmpl` difference is the pre-existing `command:`-vs-`args:` renderer convention, NOT SPEC drift); `catalog.yaml` regenerated (M3 cascade); no SPEC-ID leakage into the template tree (the 5 mirrored hooks carry `SPEC-STOPCHAIN-TRIM-001` references in comments per the `SPEC-OBSERVE-HYGIENE-001` precedent — the existing sync-phase-quality-gate.sh already carries such references — and the neutrality CI guard did NOT flag them). - **[SPEC-UPDATE-VERSION-FLAG-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-VERSION-FLAG-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). `moai update` gained a `--version <tag>` flag that installs a **specific** GitHub release tag (stable / rc / previous version) through the existing checksum-verified download path, replacing the previous "forward-to-latest-only" limitation. Sixteen REQ / 16 AC, all 16 PASS. **Implementation (M1–M6, `internal/cli/update.go` + new `internal/cli/update_version.go`)**: the flag is registered on the existing `moai update` cobra command (REQ-UVF-001); the new code path `runVersionBranch` runs BEFORE the default binary-update + template-sync flow so a non-project cwd does not block a binary-only install (parity with `--binary`). Tag values are validated by `tagCharsetRegexp` (`^v?[0-9A-Za-z.\-]+$`) BEFORE URL construction — a path-traversal defense that rejects `/`, `?`, `#`, `+`, and whitespace, so a malicious tag value cannot inject path or query fragments into the GitHub API URL (`@MX:ANCHOR` [AUTO] per plan §D#4). `normalizeVersionTag` adds a canonical `v` prefix (so `3.0.0` and `v3.0.0` hit the same URL) and rejects dev-branch `go-v*` tags outright (they belong to the dev-branch self-update path, not `--version`). Tag resolution hits `GET /repos/modu-ai/moai-adk/releases/tags/<tag>` on the compiled `githubReleasesURL` api.github.com allowlist — the URL is constructed from compiled constants, never from environment input, and a poisoned `MOAI_UPDATE_URL` is still rejected fail-closed by the existing `validateUpdateURL` allowlist gate BEFORE any `--version` HTTP call. **Reuse, do NOT fork (AP-UVF-001)**: `installVersionTag` reuses `update.NewChecker`, `update.NewUpdater`, and `update.NewRollback` — the same objects `EnsureUpdate` constructs for the default path. The only new logic is tag validation/normalization, the tag-resolution URL shape, and the backup→download→replace sequence that mirrors `orchestratorImpl.Update` steps 2-4 without the `IsUpdateAvailable` semver gate (a `--version` install is explicit, regardless of newer/older). **Checksum is MANDATORY and has no bypass (REQ-UVF-006)**: there is no `--skip-checksum` / `--insecure` flag, and a checksum mismatch discards the downloaded bytes (the updater cleans its temp file on error) and never reaches `Replace`, so the running binary path is byte-identical pre/post — the mismatch error references CWE-345. **Mutual-exclusion matrix (REQ-UVF-007)**: `--version` is mutually exclusive with `--check`, `--templates-only`, `--restore`, and `--dry-run` (each combination exits non-zero with a usage error naming the conflicting pair, before any network call); `--version` + `--binary` / `--force` / `--yes` are permitted. **Defect paths (REQ-UVF-008 / -009 / -010 / -011)**: HTTP 404 (tag not found), no matching binary asset for the running `GOOS/GOARCH`, checksum mismatch, and network failure each exit non-zero with a structured error naming the tag, the phase (`resolution` vs `download`), and the underlying cause; every defect path leaves the filesystem untouched. **Downgrade confirmation (REQ-UVF-013)**: on an interactive TTY, a requested tag older than the running version prompts for confirmation; `--yes` OR non-TTY stdin skips the prompt. **Re-exec (REQ-UVF-014)**: a successful install re-execs into the newly installed binary (setting `MOAI_SKIP_BINARY_UPDATE=1`) unless `--binary`, so subsequent template sync runs against the new binary's embedded templates. **AC-UVF-002 host-confinement**: the captured outbound request satisfies `req.Host == "api.github.com"` AND `req.URL.Path == "/repos/modu-ai/moai-adk/releases/tags/v3.0.0"` on the constructed tag-resolution URL (not only on the env-var rejection path). **Run-phase verification (16/16 ACs PASS)**: full `go test ./...` exit 0, `golangci-lint run` 0 issues, `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0, and `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD` shows no `internal/template/templates/` path (AC-UVF-015 — scope hygiene; `deps.go` was NOT modified by this SPEC). **4-locale docs-site update (M7 / REQ-UVF-016)**: `cli-reference/update.md` and `getting-started/installation.md` updated in all four locales (ko / en / ja / zh); `hns-oss-docs-verify` exit-gate recipe PASS (warning-free `hugo --minify --gc`, sitemap present, URL-blacklist 0 matches, Mermaid LR/RL 0 matches, 4-locale file-existence + section-count parity at 974 `## ` headings each, README 4-file H2 parity at 13 each, no body-emoji in the M7-modified files). The run-phase implementation + tests + docs-site update landed in commits `843be53d9` (M1–M6) and `c7bae3a77` (M7); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on the sole YAML-frontmatter-bearing artifact (`spec.md`), the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code, test, or docs changes. §25 template neutrality N/A for this sync commit — `git diff --name-only origin/main...HEAD | grep '^internal/template/templates/'` → NONE for this commit range. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-PHASE-FRONTMATTER-OWNER-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-PHASE-FRONTMATTER-OWNER-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Established the `phase:` frontmatter value contract — who owns post-authoring corrections, the authoring-time guard preventing workflow-stage tokens (`plan` / `run` / `sync` / `mx`) from being written into the release-target field, and the schema-SSOT prose that binds the field to the era-classification H-5 tie-breaker. Three surviving milestones shipped. **M1** (정정 소유자 / AC-PFO-006·007): the canonical owner of a `phase:` correction is `manager-spec`, reached via orchestrator re-delegation — documented as a separate section in `spec.md` §1.6 outside the Status Transition Ownership Matrix (which binds `status:`, not `phase:`). **M2** (값 계약 / AC-PFO-001·002·003): `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` now normatively defines the field as a release-target label, lists the four prohibited lifecycle-stage tokens (`plan` / `run` / `sync` / `mx`) case-insensitively on whole-value match, and records that the field is load-bearing for `internal/spec/era.go` H-5 era tie-breaker (`matchesModernPhase` returns true only for a value carrying a modern release prefix — a lifecycle token there silently degrades a two-signal predicate to one). **M3** (저작 시점 방지 / AC-PFO-004·005): the authoring-time guard lives on two surfaces — the `manager-spec` body's pre-write checklist and the `spec-assembly` skill — so a workflow-stage token cannot be written into `phase:` at authoring time. **M6** (회귀 검증 / AC-PFO-010·014·015): full `moai spec lint` baseline confirmed steady at 62 warnings / 0 errors post-edit, and the local↔template parity was preserved (`0` SPEC-ID / REQ / AC tokens leaked into the distributed template mirror). The run-phase doctrine edits landed in PR #1286 (squash merge `850de684c`); this sync commit carries only the `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on the sole YAML-frontmatter-bearing artifact (`spec.md`), the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code or doctrine changes. **Scope-reduction honesty (recorded, not buried)**: this SPEC was Tier M at plan-phase with 16 working ACs across 5 milestones; the parallel SPEC-PHASE-FIELD-VALIDATION-001 (PR #1285, squash merge `998744216`) landed the same lint guard + 9-file correction first, so M4 (the `PhaseValueRule` lint code) and M5 (the 9-artifact correction) were **excluded** from this SPEC — the SPEC was reduced Tier M → **Tier S** as a result, and the 16-AC working set was tightened to 8 surviving ACs (AC-PFO-001/002/003/004/006/007/014/016, inline in `spec.md` §3). The Tier-S artifact contract also means this SPEC carries **no `acceptance.md`** (ACs are inline in `spec.md §3`), so the 4-artifact frontmatter transition reduces in practice to 1 YAML-frontmatter artifact (`spec.md`); `plan.md` and `progress.md` use the markdown-header convention without frontmatter. **§25 template neutrality N/A for this sync commit** — this commit touches `.moai/specs/` markdown + `CHANGELOG.md` only; the doctrine edits that landed in PR #1286 were mirrored to `internal/template/templates/` in that run-phase commit, not here. - **[SPEC-PHASE-FIELD-VALIDATION-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-PHASE-FIELD-VALIDATION-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). A mis-authored `phase:` frontmatter value is now a **hard lint error** instead of passing silently. The field is schema-defined as a release target (`"vX.Y.Z"`), but nothing validated its value shape, no authoring instruction described it, and 31 artifact files across 20 SPECs carried workflow-stage tokens (`plan` / `run` / `sync`) in it instead. Four milestones landed. **M3** corrected the 14 in-scope files to `"v3.0.2"` (9 `spec.md` + 5 siblings), derived from git rather than assumed — every one of them first landed after the `v3.0.1` tag. It landed *before* M1 by necessity: the new code is non-demotable, so the reverse order would have broken repo lint at 9 errors. **M1** added `FrontmatterPhaseInvalid` at `SeverityError` in `internal/spec/lint.go` — a NEW finding code deliberately absent from `eraDemotableCodes`. `applyEraDemotion` switches on the code name, so an unregistered code survives on grandfathered SPECs; reusing the existing `FrontmatterInvalid` would have demoted the guard on ~96% of non-terminal SPECs, namely every SPEC lacking a `sync_commit_sha`, which is every SPEC at authoring time. The predicate is a **denylist on exact equality**, not a version allowlist: measured against 563 files, a strict-semver allowlist yields 301 false positives and substring containment yields 8 (`Run` matching inside `Runtime`), while exact match yields 0. **M2** documented the field for authoring agents in `.claude/agents/moai/manager-spec.md` and its template mirror (`internal/template/templates/.claude/agents/moai/manager-spec.md`), cross-referencing the frontmatter-schema SSOT rather than restating it; the mirror text carries no internal tokens and `internal/template/catalog.yaml` was regenerated. **M4** proved the guard is load-bearing via two falsifiability round-trips in `internal/spec/lint_phase_test.go`, both of which fail as required: removing the call site, and gutting the predicate while leaving the call site intact. The second is the one that matters — reachability alone proves nothing. 16/16 acceptance criteria judged. Verified: `go test ./...` exit 0 across 105 packages, `golangci-lint` 0 issues, repo spec lint steady at 62 findings / 0 errors. Known debt: `AC-PFV-006` cannot fail while the repo carries an empty error set, so it is a weaker check than its siblings. This sync commit carries the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition across all four artifacts, the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-PROFILE-MEMORY-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-PROFILE-MEMORY-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Fixed three defects in the Claude profile subsystem — the `~/.moai/claude-profiles/<name>` directories that `moai cc | glm | cg -p <name>` hand to Claude Code as an isolated `CLAUDE_CONFIG_DIR`. Tier L, six milestones, 21 acceptance criteria: 19 PASS, 2 PASS-WITH-DEBT, 0 FAIL. **(1) Per-project profile memory.** The launch ledger `launch.yaml` held a single global `last_profile`, so launching a named profile in project B silently overwrote what project A would come back to. It now also carries a `projects:` map keyed by normalized absolute project path (`projectsKey = "projects"` in `internal/profile/profile.go`), written alongside — never in place of — `last_profile`, so an older binary that knows only the legacy key still reads the ledger. Bare-launch resolution walks this project's entry → the global `last_profile` → `default`; an explicit `-p` still wins outright, a recorded profile whose directory has since disappeared is skipped rather than resolved, and `MOAI_NO_PROFILE_FALLBACK=1` disables both lookups. The new project-scoped surface is `ResolveLaunchProfileForProject`, `RecordLaunchProfileForProject`, and `GetCurrentNameForProject`; four of the five pre-existing call sites were left unchanged. **(2) The ledger no longer records profiles that do not exist.** The record call sat before directory creation, so a mistyped name could be written into the ledger and then resolved on the next bare launch, sending the user into a directory that was never created. The call moved to after creation and gained an existence guard, and the ledger write stays atomic (`os.CreateTemp` + `os.Rename`) so a failure leaves no partial state. **(3) A fresh profile now warns before it drops the user on the login screen.** A newly created profile directory has no `.claude.json` — the per-config-dir account and onboarding state — so Claude Code starts at the login prompt, which reads as the profile switch having failed. The launcher now prints a stderr notice first, emitted exactly once per launch. Nothing is copied or seeded into the new directory: the credential carrier differs per platform, and quietly cloning account state across an isolation boundary is the wrong default. Touched `internal/profile/`, `internal/cli/launcher.go`, `internal/cli/web.go`, one closure in `internal/web/app.go`, plus tests; `internal/profile` and `internal/web` tests were additionally pinned so they can no longer reach the real home directory. Verified on the final tree: `go build ./...` exit 0, `go vet ./...` exit 0, `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` exit 0, `golangci-lint run ./internal/profile/... ./internal/cli/... ./internal/web/...` → `0 issues.`; `internal/profile` coverage 81.2% → 82.9%. Carried debt is recorded in `progress.md` §E.2/§E.3 rather than buried: AC-PM-014(3) and AC-PM-021(3) each asked for a falsification round-trip (remove the sandbox guard, observe a FAIL, restore) that was **not executed**, so those two guards are asserted but not proven falsifiable; the `os.Rename` failure recipe behind AC-PM-020 is confirmed on macOS only; and `internal/cli` coverage was not measured (a full package run takes 271s). §25 template neutrality N/A — `internal/template/templates/` was untouched by every commit in this SPEC (`git diff --name-only 237fe01a4^..b4ae291e6 | grep -c 'internal/template/templates/'` → `0`). 4-locale documentation (`docs-site/content/{ko,en,ja,zh}/cli-reference/profile.md` and `.../web.md`, plus three screenshots under `docs-site/static/images/profile/`) landed in `ff2f728a9` and states in each locale that this behavior is on `main` but **not yet in a release** — nothing here ships to users until the next tag. Implementation commits `237fe01a4`, `6d5b3696d`, `dab895cfd`, `90dca4f4c`, `525b3c723`, `fa0d5898a`, `d6d2eac40`, `b4ae291e6`, `903fb4a67`; this sync commit carries the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition, the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this entry — no additional code changes. **Amendment (in-place):** closed the AC-PM-014(3)/AC-PM-021(3) sandbox-guard falsification-roundtrip verification debt recorded above — both guards now proven falsifiable (disable → FAIL observed → restore → PASS observed), promoting the two PASS-WITH-DEBT rows to PASS (21/21 PASS). No user-visible change; production code byte-identical to the prior close. Not pushed. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-REF-SEO-ABSORB-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-REF-SEO-ABSORB-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Added `moai-ref-seo`, a search-visibility and crawlability reference skill covering canonical-URL discipline, per-page unique title and meta description, robots.txt / sitemap.xml as host-derived artifacts, JSON-LD structured data with entity consistency, a single response-header chokepoint, and the four SEO-causal document-semantics items (single H1 with unskipped levels, image `alt`, descriptive anchor text, live in-page fragment targets). Shipped as a single 207-line `SKILL.md` in both the local tree (`.claude/skills/moai-ref-seo/SKILL.md`) and the distributed template (`internal/template/templates/.claude/skills/moai-ref-seo/SKILL.md`), byte-identical. Keyboard operability, visible focus, and form-control labeling are delegated to the accessibility surface, and generative-engine optimization is deliberately excluded — both stated in the `NOT for:` clause rather than in a trailing body line, so the invariant triad (`Common Rationalizations` / `Red Flags` / `Verification`) still terminates the body. The second deliverable is a **reusable clean-room re-authoring protocol**: the source third-party document ships with no license, NOTICE, or COPYING file, so structure and concept inventory were studied while every sentence was newly written, the source was pinned by `sha256` digest, and an 8-gram overlap check plus a longest-common-substring bound were run against the delivered artifact. Because both checks are lexical-contiguity measures that a synonym-substituted copy would pass, the protocol additionally requires a recorded structural-divergence verdict, and the overlap check carries a self-trip demonstration (source compared against itself, observed to FAIL) proving the check is not vacuous. Registration landed on the same 15-item surface set `moai-ref-secops` actually occupies, measured rather than assumed: `internal/template/catalog.yaml` (`tier: optional-pack:frontend`, `make build`-generated hash), the three Go count constants (`expectedSkillCount` 32 in `catalog_tier_audit_test.go`, `expectedTotal` 42 in `catalog_loader_test.go`, `wantTotal` 42 in `embed_catalog_test.go`) incremented in the same change as the markdown, `delegation.yaml` under the `frontend` domain key in both local and template copies, the two workflow bodies `review.md` and `sync/quality-gates-quality.md` with their two template mirrors, `internal/template/skills_manifest_test.go`, and the docs-site skill-guide table in all four locales (en / ko / ja / zh). The skill-routing rule is deliberately **not** part of the set — `moai-ref-secops` does not appear there either. 24/24 acceptance criteria PASS. The run-phase implementation landed in PR #1274 (squash merge `1594cf60e`); this sync commit carries the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition, the `progress.md` §E.4 signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. - **[SPEC-UPDATE-DATA-SURVIVAL-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-DATA-SURVIVAL-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). `moai update` now takes an on-disk backup before every destructive step, ships a failure contract with an escape from the marker-gate lockout, and replaces two vacuous safety guards with falsifiable ones. Six milestones, 20/20 ACs PASS. **M1** added the failure contract: a stage runner (`internal/cli/update_recovery_manifest.go`) that marks the destructive region on entry and, on a failure inside it, writes a `recovery-manifest.txt` naming the failed stage, the error, the backup directory, and the exact `moai update --restore <dir>` command — deliberately reporting rather than auto-rolling-back (the rollback path would be the least-tested code running in the worst state). The new `--restore` flag (`internal/cli/update_restore.go` + `internal/cli/update/backup/restore_entry.go`) is handled **before** the project-marker gate, because a destroyed `.moai/config/sections/system.yaml` is exactly the damage restore exists to repair; the exemption is scoped to that one branch, a directory lacking `backup_metadata.json` is refused rather than copied in, and a second merge pass makes repeated application byte-idempotent. **M2** added `internal/cli/update_destructive_registry.go` — 11 (file, function) rows covering 18 `os.RemoveAll`/`os.Rename` sites, each carrying either a protection or an exemption — validated by a guard that enumerates sites by statically parsing Go source rather than reading the table, so the comparison cannot degenerate into `count == count`; rows are keyed on (File, Function, Sites), never line numbers. `.moai/memory/` is now copied into a run-scoped backup before the both-exist branch removes it, and a failed copy aborts the removal. **M3** added `internal/cli/update_disk_backup.go`: `.claude/settings.json`, `.moai/status_line.sh`, and `.gitignore` previously existed nowhere but the process heap between removal and merge-back, so a crash, ENOSPC, or Ctrl+C in that window lost them permanently — both execution paths now route their first destructive step through one shared guard that writes on-disk copies first and **aborts before** the destructive call when the write fails. **M4** routed `ensureGlobalSettingsEnv` through the injectable `userHomeDirFn` seam and pinned the HOME deletion radius to a single named target. **M5** replaced a fake-`simulateMoaiUpdate` user-area guard with one that drives the real entry point (registry-function coverage moved off `0.0%`). **M6** made `mergeBackPreserveInventory` report `restored N/M before failure` on every error path (coverage 64.3% → 94.1%). ### Removed

- **The GLM team-mode settings writers and `enableTeamMode`.** Agent Teams static orchestration is retired (`--team` resolves to `MODE_TEAM_UNAVAILABLE` and falls back to sub-agents), which left the team-mode settings writers with no caller and `enableTeamMode` with no reachable path. Both are deleted along with their test-only surface. Native `moai cg` and `moai worktree --team` are unaffected — they never routed through this code.
- **The `research.yaml` config section and `state.state_dir`, re-removed.** Both had been removed once and reintroduced by a template resync. `research.yaml` had zero read sites, and `state.state_dir` was write-only — every runtime `.moai/state` path is resolved elsewhere. Orphaned files in existing projects are swept on the next `moai update`.

- **`moai init --harness-profile` flag** — removed in #1322 along with its dead writer (`writeHarnessProfileYAML`) and the wizard `harness_profile` question. The flag had been accepted-but-inert since the wizard restructure: it parsed a value, wrote nothing any runtime consumer read, and its `HarnessProfile` field had zero readers. **Deployed-user impact:** the flag shipped in `v3.0.1`, so a script pinned to `moai init <project> --non-interactive --harness-profile default` now fails with `Error: unknown flag: --harness-profile` (cobra's default for an unregistered flag — no deprecation cycle was staged). Drop the flag from any such invocation; there is no replacement, because it never had an effect to replace. Harness depth is configured through `.moai/config/sections/harness.yaml`. **Security note:** the deleted writer interpolated `opts.HarnessProfile` raw into YAML and wholesale-overwrote the deployed `harness.yaml`; removing it permanently forecloses the injection hole that `validateInitFlags` had already closed for `--project-mode`. 🗿 MoAI
- **[SPEC-CONFIG-DEAD-SWEEP-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CONFIG-DEAD-SWEEP-001/spec.md)** — removed three dead configuration surfaces after verifying each had zero runtime consumers. **`.moai/config/sections/cache.yaml`**: `LoadCacheConfig` / `CacheConfig` had no caller anywhere in the tree, and no `cache_control` injection exists in Go — the file configured a code path that was never built. The closed set of `session_ttl` values survives as the `config.ValidSessionTTLs()` accessor, which the `internal/settings` schema seam still consumes. **`.moai/config/sections/research.yaml`**: `ResearchConfig` had definitions and defaults but zero read sites; the loader chain, wrapper, defaults, and strict-unmarshal case are removed together. **`state.state_dir`**: a write-only field whose value nothing read — every runtime `.moai/state` path is a hardcoded literal elsewhere (`internal/cli/state.go`, `internal/worktree/state_guard.go`); `StateConfig.RetentionDays` is retained. **Deployed-user impact:** orphaned `cache.yaml` / `research.yaml` in existing projects are swept on the next `moai update`, which cleans `.moai/config/` wholesale — no manual action needed. The four docs-site locales that documented `cache.yaml` as a live user-editable surface are corrected in the same change. 🗿 MoAI

### Fixed

- **`moai update` now derives a merge base, so template additions reach existing projects.** Without a merge base the update compared against the wrong reference and concluded that a newly added template file was already accounted for, so files added to the template since a project's last update never landed. Existing projects that missed template additions pick them up on the next `moai update`.
- **The branch-guard scan is scoped to the command being invoked, not to text it carries as data.** Quoted spans are collapsed before matching, so `moai todo add "… git switch …"` is no longer denied for containing a branch-state command inside a string, while `git switch main` and `git checkout -b "feat/x"` still deny. The exemption doctrine is corrected in the same change: neither the `manager-git` identity axis nor the `MOAI_BRANCH_GUARD_EXEMPT` sentinel is reachable from inside a tool-spawned subagent — the hook runs as a separate process before the guarded command executes, so exporting the sentinel inside that command never reaches it. A subagent that must mutate branch state works in a worktree instead.
- **The permission decision now survives the hook handler merge.** A decision produced by one handler could be dropped when results were merged, so a deny computed upstream did not always reach the runtime. The decision is carried through the merge.
- **The kanban bootstrap notice is delivered to the operator, in their language, and only on a genuinely new session.** Three defects in one surface: the notice was emitted as internal text the operator never saw, it rendered in English regardless of `conversation_language`, and it re-fired on session resumption — so a resumed session announced a bootstrap that had already happened.
- **Kanban board lock release errors are joined in both directions.** A failure releasing the lock could mask a failure from the operation it guarded, or the reverse, depending on which path returned first. Both errors are now joined, so neither is silently discarded.
- **Statusline usage fetch is bounded against an unbounded `Retry-After`.** A server-supplied `Retry-After` was honored verbatim, so a large value stalled the usage fetch phase for as long as the server asked. The phase is now bounded independently of what the header requests (issue #1467).
- **Tests no longer write to the real home directory.** Several tests resolved paths against the actual `$HOME` rather than a temp directory, mutating the developer's own configuration as a side effect of running the suite.

- **[SPEC-V3R6-LIFECYCLE-CLOSE-THREEPHASE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-V3R6-LIFECYCLE-CLOSE-THREEPHASE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **`moai spec close` no longer blocks 3-phase SPECs from closing.** The schema (`spec-frontmatter-schema.md`) retired the `§E.5 Mx-phase` section (folded into `§E.4`) as part of SPEC-V3R6-LIFECYCLE-REDESIGN-001, but `internal/spec/closer.go` was missed by that migration: Precondition 2 (`closer.go:696-699`) still hard-required `state.HasMxSection` (a `§E.5` section) to be present. The result was a structural contradiction — the schema forbids authoring new `§E.5` sections, but the closer required `§E.5` present — so any 3-phase SPEC honoring the schema by omitting `§E.5` was blocked from closing. Real-world impact: 4 SPECs in the mo.ai.kr checkout were stuck at `status: implemented`, blocked from close by this defect (SPEC-DB-BACKLOG-001, SPEC-DB-CLEANUP-001, SPEC-DESIGN-HANDOFF-NEWS-001, SPEC-ID-SEC-MEDIUM-001); `moai spec close SPEC-DB-CLEANUP-001 --dry-run` reported `"missing §E.5 Mx-phase audit-ready signal"`. **Fix**: Precondition 2 is relaxed to an additive OR modeled on the dual-acceptance pattern already shipped in `transitions.go:79-92` (`closeInfixMatch` accepts both the new `3-phase close` and legacy `4-phase close` infixes). The legacy `§E.5`-present path stays accepted (backward-compat preserved for grandfather-era SPECs — AC-LSG-002) AND a new 3-phase path accepts `§E.4` sync marker + non-empty `sync_commit_sha` (AC-LSG-001). The OR is additive, not a replacement. Three stale `4-phase` references in `closer.go` (L109 doc comment, L352 commit-subject template, L685 doc comment) plus the L91 sentinel-error comment are canonicalized to the 3-phase convention (REQ-LSG-004/005, AC-LSG-004). Scope discipline: `era.go` (`§E.5` literal-heading recognition intentionally preserved for legacy era classification — 9 matches unchanged), `audit.go`, `transitions.go`, and `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` are NOT touched (AC-LSG-003 scope guard). TDD: `TestClose_PreconditionMissingMx` (asserted the `§E.5`-absent case FAILS) is converted to `TestClose_ThreePhasePreconditionPass` (3-phase PASS) + `TestClose_LegacyMxSectionStillAccepted` (legacy PASS); RED captured before GREEN (the 3-phase test failed against unmodified `closer.go` with `missing §E.5 Mx-phase audit-ready signal`). 7 acceptance criteria (AC-LSG-001..007: 6 MUST-PASS + 1 DEFERRED; AC-LSG-007 is the post-merge mo.ai.kr `moai spec close SPEC-DB-CLEANUP-001 --dry-run` validation, recorded as command+expected-output only — requires `make build` reinstall + checkout switch). [The canonical grep `grep -oE 'AC-([A-Z0-9]+-)*[0-9]+' acceptance.md | sort -u | wc -l` returns 9, not 7, because AC-LSG-005's verification text names AC-LSG-014 (atomic-rollback) and AC-LSG-018 (no-op) as parent-SPEC (LIFECYCLE-REDESIGN-001) regression references — they are NOT this SPEC's own ACs; this SPEC's AC matrix is AC-LSG-001..007.] Run-phase landed as M1 `1199e3b58` (Precondition 2 relaxation + TDD pivot) + M2 `617a8418c` (4-phase→3-phase prose canonicalization). Orchestrator trust-but-verify (this tree, this run, HEAD `617a8418c`): `go test ./internal/spec/ -run 'TestClose_ThreePhasePreconditionPass|TestClose_LegacyMxSectionStillAccepted' -v -count=1` → 2/2 PASS; `go test -cover ./internal/spec/...` → **89.1%** (≥85%); `golangci-lint run --timeout=2m ./internal/spec/...` → 0 issues; `grep -c '4-phase' internal/spec/closer.go` → 0; scope guards intact. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `draft → in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on `spec.md` (sole YAML-frontmatter artifact — `acceptance.md` / `plan.md` use the markdown-header convention), the `progress.md` §E.4 audit-ready signal, and this CHANGELOG entry — zero further code changes (sync is markdown-only). **Gap (recorded, not back-dated)**: the `draft → in-progress` frontmatter step was missed on M1 (spec.md stayed `draft` through the run phase); the M1 commit subject `fix(SPEC-V3R6-LIFECYCLE-CLOSE-THREEPHASE-001): M1 ...` DOES match the canonical `draft → in-progress` owner pattern, so the OwnershipTransitionRule subject-prefix check is satisfied — the gap is the missing frontmatter status advance, not a wrong owner.
