- **[SPEC-GATE-ASTGREP-REPAIR-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-GATE-ASTGREP-REPAIR-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Three combined defects behind `moai gate`'s 22,293-finding false-positive firehose (Tier L, P1, 12/13 ACs PASS + AC-GAR-007 SHOULD deferred, 0 uncovered REQ). **D1 — ast-grep rule `go-error-not-wrapped` over-matched `return $ERR`**: the unconstrained metavariable bound any single AST node, so `return nil`, `return true`, `return 0`, and `return f()` were all flagged as unwrapped errors (16,124 of 21,990 findings = 73% of total noise), and the autofix would have rewritten them into `fmt.Errorf("TODO: operation: %w", nil)` — catastrophic if applied. Refined with a name constraint (`constraints.ERR.regex: ^err(e?|s)$`) in `.moai/config/astgrep-rules/go/error-handling.yml`; non-error returns are no longer matched, and the autofix fires under the SAME guard so it is automatically gated. False-positive count on the dogfood tree: 16,124 → 357 (97.8% reduction). The `fix:` field is preserved (AP-GAR-002). **Dogfood-tree only** (B1 decision): the distributed template baseline `internal/template/templates/.moai/config/astgrep-rules/` carries no error-wrapping rule and is NOT touched — the refinement propagates to dogfood users only. **D2 — whole-repo scan including worktrees / vendor / testdata / `*_test.go`**: ast-grep 0.40.5 does NOT support a `globs:` exclusion field in `sgconfig.yml` config-mode (empirically verified — adding any `globs` entry silently breaks the scan, returning 0 matches for every rule). The exclusion lives at the gate layer instead — new `filterExcludedPaths` in `internal/hook/quality/astgrep_gate.go` drops findings whose paths fall under `.claude/worktrees/`, `/vendor/`, `/testdata/`, or end in `_test.go`. The filter is authoritative regardless of `.gitignore` state (`.claude/worktrees/` was already gitignored, so the scan from the main checkout reported 0 there; the filter makes the exclusion explicit and covers `vendor/` + testdata + test files, which `.gitignore` does not cover). Applied at the gate entry point so both the PreToolUse path and the standalone `moai gate` CLI share one filter. **D3 — `moai gate` ignored `gate.yaml` and hardcoded blocking defaults**: `internal/cli/gate.go` `runGate` constructed `quality.DefaultGateConfig()` directly and never consulted `.moai/config/sections/gate.yaml`, so a project setting `ast_grep_gate: { warn_only_mode: true, block_on_error: false }` (advisory intent) still got blocking behavior — a config/behavior inconsistency where the PreToolUse path read the config but the standalone CLI did not. Fixed by routing `runGate` through a new `loadGateCfgForCLI(projectDir)` helper that calls the shared `config.NewLoader().Load()` chain (the same loader that includes `loadGateSection` from SPEC-CONFIG-AUDIT-REPAIR-001 M2), mapping `config.GateConfig → quality.GateConfig` verbatim after the `pre_tool.go loadGateConfig` pattern (including the `disabled_steps` verbatim copy for issue #1265 and the `RulesDir` default). Fall-back (AP-GAR-004): on missing/unparseable `gate.yaml`, the helper returns `quality.DefaultGateConfig()` with `ProjectDir` + `GoBuildTags` applied — the gate still runs and does NOT silently hard-block. **Smoke verified**: `./bin/moai gate` against an isolated temp project with a genuine `return err` violation + `gate.yaml warn_only_mode: true` exits 0 (advisory honored end-to-end). The run-phase code + tests landed across 4 commits (`df21e689e` M0+M1, `6122b0e31` M2, `e7f020763` M3, `65d06e5e8` M4 progress); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry + `@MX:NOTE` annotations on the two new code surfaces — no additional code changes. §25 template neutrality N/A (dogfood-tree config + `internal/` Go sources only; distributed baseline untouched per B1 — `git diff origin/main..HEAD -- internal/template/templates/` shows no new changes from this SPEC). Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-*` placeholder in this commit (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3) and backfilled in a follow-up commit after the sync PR merges. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-UPDATE-TEMPLATE-BASE-SNAPSHOT-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-TEMPLATE-BASE-SNAPSHOT-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). `moai update`'s 3-way YAML config merge now derives its BASE from a rendered install-time template snapshot (`.moai/cache/template-snapshot/`), instead of the raw NEW embedded template — closing the **D5 provenance defect** deferred by `SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001` (#1243, Decision D5). Tier L, P1 high, plan-audit iter-2 PASS 0.90, 23/23 ACs PASS, sync-auditor PASS (harmonic mean 0.952), AC-TBS-010 non-vacuity independently reproduced. **Root cause**: the prior `SaveTemplateDefaults` / `BackupMoaiConfig` base-derivation treated the raw embedded template (with `{{.Version}}` placeholders) as the BASE for the 3-way merge, so a template-blessed value change between installs (a version bump like `3.0.1 → 3.0.2`, or a default change) was computed as user-vs-new `diff` rather than base-vs-new `diff` — the merge then "preserved" the stale install-time value as if it were a user edit, silently blocking blessed updates from reaching the user's config. The prior SPEC hardened the merge *representation* (comments / order / quoting via the `*yaml.Node` tree) and explicitly deferred this provenance defect because (a) it is a different defect class (provenance, not representation), (b) it requires a new persisted artifact, and (c) it carries its own backward-compatibility surface. **Mechanism (`internal/cli/update/backup`)**: `snapshot.go` (new) renders the template section files to their install-time byte form via the existing `template.Renderer` (resolving `{{.Version}}` etc. to concrete values) and persists them under `.moai/cache/template-snapshot/` — a rendered snapshot, NOT a raw template copy. `base_loader.go` (new) resolves the BASE for `BackupMoaiConfig`: prefer the rendered snapshot when present (REQ-TBS-006), fall back to the prior raw-template behavior when absent (REQ-TBS-007). The snapshot is written at the end of `moai init` (REQ-TBS-001) and at the end of every `moai update` restore phase (REQ-TBS-002), wired via the new `update_snapshot_hook.go` thin hook called from the three restore-completion sites (`update_template_sync.go`, `update_clean_install.go`, `update_restore.go`) plus `init.go`. The snapshot lives under `.moai/cache/` (already gitignored at `.gitignore:273`) and survives the update clean step (`update_cleanup.go` deletes `.moai/config/` but not `.moai/cache/`). **Backward-compatible**: installs without a snapshot (pre-existing installs on first run after upgrade) fall back to the prior raw-template BASE on the first update (REQ-TBS-007), then create one; no migration step, no config rewrite, no user action required. **No representation regression**: the `*yaml.Node` merge from `SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001` is untouched — this SPEC swaps only the BASE source, not the merge engine. The run-phase code + tests landed in commit `0852eacf9` (`fix(SPEC-UPDATE-TEMPLATE-BASE-SNAPSHOT-001): use rendered template snapshot as 3-way merge BASE`, 23/23 ACs PASS, run_commit_sha backfilled in `b66f28b93`); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-after-merge` placeholder in this commit (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3; Route B squash merge means the final SHA is the merged-to-main SHA, known only after PR merge) and backfilled in a follow-up commit after the sync PR merges. README / docs-site unchanged — this is an internal merge-mechanism correctness fix, not a user-facing feature (no doc was actively wrong; docs-site documents no `moai update` merge/preservation behavior). §25 template neutrality N/A (`internal/cli/update/backup` Go sources + SPEC artifacts only — `git show --name-only 0852eacf9 | grep -c '^internal/template/templates/'` → 0, no template mirror touched). Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-WORKTREE-SQUASH-MERGE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-WORKTREE-SQUASH-MERGE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). `moai worktree clean --stale` / `--merged-only` now reclaim worktrees whose branches were squash-merged into the base branch. The merge predicate previously ran only `git branch --merged <base>` — a reachability test that lists a branch solely when its tip is an ancestor of `base`. A squash merge collapses N branch commits into one new commit on `base`, so the original commits never become ancestors; every squash-merged branch read as unmerged forever and its worktree was silently kept (no error was raised, so the failure was invisible). Because this repository auto-merges every PR with squash, the blind spot covered essentially every normally-merged branch. **Fix**: `internal/core/git/worktree.go` `IsBranchMerged` is now a composed predicate — reachability (preserved verbatim) OR empty-diff OR (rebase-merge history signal AND state reproduction) OR (squash-merge history signal AND state reproduction). The state-reproduction conjunct enumerates every path the branch touched since its merge-base and asks whether base HEAD holds identical content, guarded against the rename-fold, output-encoding, pathspec-interpretation, textconv, and submodule-ignore axes discovered during plan-phase. **No interface change**: the `WorktreeProvider.IsBranchMerged` signature is unchanged, both call sites in `internal/cli/worktree/clean.go` are untouched, and the `clean` command's help text is unchanged (the fix makes behaviour converge to the existing "merged into the base branch" wording rather than diverge from it).

- **Docs follow-up to `#1280`** — worktree redesign retired-flag sweep (4-locale). The `/moai plan --worktree` flag was retired by the worktree redesign (`#1278`); `#1280` swept three user-visible surfaces that still advertised it (the `quickstart.md` and `moai-plan.md` pages in all four locales, and the `moai init` / `moai update` `--help` text) but missed a fourth: the **subcommand table on the core-concepts overview page**. `docs-site/content/{ko,en,ja,zh}/core-concepts/what-is-moai-adk.md` listed `--worktree` as a live `plan` flag, so a reader copying the table into `/moai plan "..." --worktree` would hit an unknown-flag error. Removed `--worktree` from the flag column in all four locales, leaving `--branch`, `--resume SPEC-XXX` — matching the `moai-plan.md` reference table exactly, where worktree isolation moved to the enter-first pattern (`moai cc -w <name>` then `/moai plan`) that is now the only entry path. Recorded here so the retired flag is not re-introduced by a future 4-locale sync. Documentation-only; no code, config, or behavior change. 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-OBS-ENABLED-GATE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-OBS-ENABLED-GATE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Closed the observability `enabled`-key contract gap exposed by SPEC-HOOK-TRACE-FLUSH-001 (Tier S, P2/Medium, plan-audit iter-2 PASS 0.81, 7/7 ACs GREEN). `internal/cli/deps.go:259` `enableObservabilityIfConfigured(reg, cwd)` gated `TraceWriter` creation on **file existence only** (`os.Stat`), never reading the `enabled` key documented in `.moai/config/sections/observability.yaml` — so any user setting `enabled: false` (the documented opt-out) still had a `TraceWriter` created and trace files written. The contract violation was silent while the `TraceWriter` buffered in memory; SPEC-HOOK-TRACE-FLUSH-001 made the writer actually flush, which is what made the violation observable. **Fix**: the gate now runs a two-step check — `os.Stat` (preserved, REQ-OEG-005 skip-on-missing intact and the heavier parse skipped when the file is absent) THEN `hook.IsObservabilityEnabled()` (REQ-OEG-007 SSOT reuse, consulted only when the file exists). The SSOT returns false on absent key, false on `enabled: false`, and false on parse error (safe-defaults), so file-present + `enabled: false` and file-present + key-absent both short-circuit before `EnableObservability` is called and before the log directory is created (REQ-OEG-002 / REQ-OEG-004). **Cohabitation invariant preserved**: the 3 protected yaml-key read paths (`observabilityOptIn`, `hookOptInEnabled`, `IsObservabilityEnabled`) documented in `internal/hook/observability.go` / `observability_master.go` are untouched — deps.go becomes a consumer of the existing reader, NOT a new independent reader; `AC-HOI-007` 4-quadrant cohabitation test remains green. **Tests**: `internal/cli/deps_coverage_test.go` adds `TestEnableObservabilityIfConfigured_DisabledByEnabledKey` (AC-OEG-001) and `TestEnableObservabilityIfConfigured_AbsentKeyDefaultsDisabled` (AC-OEG-003), both using the `hook.SetObservabilityMasterForTesting(false)` + non-parallel + `t.Cleanup(hook.ResetObservabilityMasterForTesting)` seam pattern (per `internal/hook/notification_test.go:87` precedent — the only honest way to control the `IsObservabilityEnabled()` decision from a tempdir test, because the SSOT resolves cwd via `CLAUDE_PROJECT_DIR` → `os.Getwd()` and cannot read a tempdir's key). **AC-OEG-007 sabotage round-trip**: the RED leg is reliable precisely because the master-toggle seam bypasses the cwd/env resolution that would otherwise mask the sabotage — under the sabotaged `os.Stat`-only gate, `SetObservabilityMasterForTesting(false)` is ignored and `EnableObservability` is called anyway, failing the stub call-count assertion; the restored implementation passes the scoped run (`ok  github.com/modu-ai/moai-adk/internal/cli  0.850s`). **Static verification (AC-OEG-006)**: `grep -c 'IsObservabilityEnabled' internal/cli/deps.go` → `4`; companion grep for `hook.observability_events` / `hook.opt_in.enabled` in the same file excluding comments → `0` matches (REQ-OEG-006 — no system.yaml key reads introduced). **Coverage**: `enableObservabilityIfConfigured` 100% function-level (all four branches exercised); `./internal/cli/` package 76.4%. The run-phase code + tests landed in commit `468eb584a` (single Tier S M1 milestone); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. §25 template neutrality N/A (`internal/cli` Go sources + SPEC artifacts only — 0 template files touched). Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-HOOK-TRACE-FLUSH-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-HOOK-TRACE-FLUSH-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Stopped hook trace entries from being lost at process exit, so trace counts become usable as evidence of hook invocation (Tier M, priority High, 16/16 ACs PASS, 0 uncovered REQ). **Root cause**: `internal/hook/trace.TraceWriter` is asynchronous — `Write` enqueues to a 100-slot buffered channel and returns while a background goroutine writes to disk — and `Close()` was its only flush barrier, with **zero production callers** (the only `.Close()` matches in `internal/`/`cmd/`/`pkg/` were a gzip writer in `internal/harness/retention.go`). Because `moai hook <event>` is a one-shot process that reads stdin, dispatches, writes stdout, and exits, the background goroutine lost the race against process teardown. **Measured, not inferred**: `moai hook session-start` invoked 10 times against a scratch project produced 8 of 30 expected entries, and the last handler of each dispatch contributed **0 of 20** — the loss is structurally biased toward the final handler, i.e. the entry carrying the blocking decision, because earlier handlers' entries get the following handlers' execution time to flush while the last one always races the exit. A positive control (`moai hook pre-tool` × 121) produced a **created but 0-byte** trace file, proving the pipeline reached `os.OpenFile` and excluding "observability was simply off" as the explanation. **Fix**: `TraceWriter.CloseWithTimeout(d)` is a bounded flush barrier returning `nil` on a completed drain and an error matching the new sentinel `ErrFlushTimeout` when the budget elapses first; the budget lives in `internal/config/defaults.go` as `DefaultTraceFlushTimeout = 200 * time.Millisecond` rather than as a call-site literal. `registry.Shutdown()` takes the writer reference under the mutex and **releases it before waiting** (the drain path and `writeTrace` share that mutex, so holding it across the wait would risk a deadlock), and both production dispatch sites — `runHookEvent` and `runAgentHook` in `internal/cli/hook.go` — now `defer` it, reached through an anonymous `interface{ Shutdown() }` assertion so `hook.Registry` stays exactly 3 methods wide. `Close()` keeps its unbounded semantics and its existing contract test. **The budget is a ceiling, not a cost**: `CloseWithTimeout` returns the instant the drain completes. Measured drain on a local SSD over 200 repetitions per bucket is p99 140µs for the 3-entry case a real dispatch produces and p99 3.39ms / max 5.30ms with the channel at its 100-entry capacity, so 200ms leaves roughly 40x headroom over the worst measured case while remaining ~4% of the tightest configured hook timeout. **Residual loss is measured, not silenced**: an unconditional "flush everything" guarantee would contradict the budget, so when the budget is exhausted the abandoned count is emitted as the structured field `undrained_entries` — loss cannot be driven to zero under an async design, but it must not be silent. **Three regression guards, all falsified before being trusted**: `TestRegistryShutdownFlushesLastHandlerEntry` (`internal/hook/shutdown_test.go`) asserts the **last** handler's entry is present in the file — asserting mere file existence would be vacuous, since the positive control above created an empty file; `TestHookCommandFlushesLastHandlerEntry` (`internal/cli/hook_flush_test.go`) builds the binary and crosses the process boundary, because `internal/hook` does not import `internal/cli` and therefore cannot execute the CLI wiring at all, leaving `REQ-HTF-002` with no behavioral judge otherwise; and `TestFlushBarrierHasProductionCaller` guards against the caller count returning to zero. The sabotage round-trip reproduced the production failure exactly — the E2E guard failed with a created-but-empty trace file, and only 1 of 5 runs won the race, which is why it runs under `-count`. **@MX correction**: the `@MX:ANCHOR` above `Close` claimed `fan_in=24` and "all hook handler teardown paths call Close"; measured production fan_in was 0, and that false assurance is part of why the missing barrier went unnoticed. Demoted to `@MX:NOTE` per the protocol's below-3 rule; the goroutine-lifetime `@MX:WARN` on `NewTraceWriter` is preserved because that hazard is unchanged. The `goleak.IgnoreTopFunction` for `TraceWriter.run` in `internal/hook/main_test.go` was **removed rather than re-justified** — its sole cause was a test detaching a writer without closing it, that test now calls `Shutdown`, and the package runs with zero exemptions. Verified: `go test ./internal/hook/ -count=3` ok with no goleak exemptions, `go build ./...` and `go vet ./internal/hook/... ./internal/cli/...` exit 0, `moai spec lint` no findings. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry — no code changes. No user-facing surface changed: this is internal runtime behavior, so no README or docs-site update applies. §25 template neutrality N/A (`git status --porcelain internal/template/` empty across the run phase — no template mirror touched). Known scope exclusions recorded as follow-ups, not fixed here: `enableObservabilityIfConfigured` in `internal/cli/deps.go` gates on the **existence** of `.moai/config/sections/observability.yaml` and never reads its `enabled` key, so `enabled: false` does not disable tracing; the 4 unverified hook families (`security-*`, sync-gate, the 5 workflow hooks, ci-watch) are only now worth re-investigating; the flush-budget measurement covers local SSD on macOS only, with no network-filesystem, Windows, or loaded-disk figure — `undrained_entries` is the production surface for that residual; and `TestFlushBarrierHasProductionCaller` asserts a floor of 3 distinct handlers, not an exact count derived from production wiring. Also observed but **not this SPEC's regression**: `internal/cli/launcher.go` now reports one `errcheck` finding (`Error return value of fmt.Fprintf is not checked`), introduced by a parallel session's `os.Stderr` → `launcherStderr` rename, which took the call out of errcheck's default exclusion — 0 occurrences of `launcherStderr` at the pre-run baseline `c907db541` versus 5 now, and none of this SPEC's 8 commits touched that file. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-CI-LOOP-DEVONLY-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CI-LOOP-DEVONLY-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Stopped distributing a CI watch / auto-fix workflow whose executable never shipped (Tier M, P1/HIGH, plan-audit PASS 0.84 after 3 iterations, 19/19 ACs GREEN, 0 uncovered REQ). **Root cause**: the template tree referenced `scripts/ci-watch/` and `scripts/ci-autofix/` shell scripts that exist only in the development repository, so every downstream user received documentation, a `moai-workflow-ci-loop` skill, and Frozen constitutional clauses mandating a workflow they had no executable for. **Load-bearing refutation** — `moai pr watch` does NOT perform the watch loop: its default mode prints an instruction to run a shell script and exits 0, `os.Exit(2)` does not exist in the CLI, and the polling loop, 30-second interval, 30-minute timeout, and `exit 2` all live in `scripts/ci-watch/run.sh`; the `--help` `Long` text described a contract `RunE` never implemented. An earlier revision of this SPEC concluded the opposite by treating help prose as evidence of implementation and was corrected by executing the command — **help text is not evidence of implementation**. That refutation is why the two protocol files are treated differently. **Measured outcomes**: template-tree script-path references 27 → 0; files carrying the skill identifier (template + `catalog.yaml`) 9 → 0; `moai pr watch` mentions in the distributed rule tree 5 → 0; constitution findings 77 / 18 ci-attributed → 59 / 0 with `validate-exit=1`; `canary_gate: true` 73 → 65 in each of the two zone registries; `.github/required-checks.yml` prerequisites in the template 4 → 0; dev-repo mirror references 9 → 0; user-visible binary strings instructing an absent script 3 → 0; and the 9 dev-only scripts + the skill directory + both mirror protocol files preserved unchanged. **Intentional template/mirror asymmetry**: `ci-autofix-protocol.md` was rewritten in the template to drop its script dependency but preserved verbatim in the dev-repo mirror, while `ci-watch-protocol.md` stopped shipping entirely — one principle covers both, *a rule belongs only where the thing it governs exists*. **Two new guards, both mutation-verified**: `TestEmbeddedTemplatesExcludeCILoopSkill` (`internal/template/embed_ci_loop_guard_test.go`) walks the compiled `//go:embed` FS — the deployment artifact, not the source tree — and correctly reported 3 files when retargeted at a still-shipped skill; `TestArchiveSkill_PreservesUserContent` (`internal/cli/update_archive_test.go`) byte-compares archived content and reported "archive 0 files, source 2" when the copy was disabled, with a copy→rename regression caught by its source-still-present assertion. **Deferred-decision outcome**: the `.github/required-checks.yml` prerequisite was deferred rather than fixed, on the reasoning that every reference to it sat inside this SPEC's removal scope and would vanish on completion — measured 4 → 0, so the deferral was correct and no follow-up SPEC is needed. Two judgments were promoted from deferred debt at run-phase entry by user decision (AC-CLD-018 `moai pr watch` behavior / flag / exit-code invariance under the string-only edit, AC-CLD-019 archive-not-delete), taking uncovered REQ from 1 to 0. docs-site updated across all 4 locales (ko/en/ja/zh): `guides/ci-autonomy.md`, `advanced/skill-guide.md`, `workflow-commands/moai-sync.md`. **Known gap**: `internal/cli` package coverage is 75.9%, below the 85% target — a pre-existing baseline not caused by this SPEC (which only added tests), but not met. §25 template neutrality verified (`TestTemplateNoInternalContentLeak` + `TestSplitHarnessNamespaceNoLeak` PASS; 0 SPEC-ID occurrences in the template tree). Route B (PR-mandatory). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-UPDATE-LEGACY-SKILL-LIST-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-LEGACY-SKILL-LIST-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Stopped `[ARCHIVE_DRIFT]` from re-firing on every `moai update`, on every project, with no path to convergence (Tier M, P1, plan-audit PASS 0.89 after 3 iterations, 0 MUST-FIX). **Root cause**: `legacySkillIDs` in `internal/cli/update_archive.go` listed 16 skills as "removed in BC-V3R3-007", but three of them — `moai-domain-backend`, `moai-domain-frontend`, `moai-domain-database` — are live, currently-shipped template skills. Every `moai update` wipes `.claude/skills/moai*` (`update/deploy/deploy.go:51-55`, glob target) and redeploys from the embedded template, then runs the archive step (`update.go:449-457`, post-sync); `.moai/archive/` is not a clean target, so the archive survives. Because those three sources are recreated on every run, `archiveSkill` never reached its source-absent short-circuit and `checkArchiveDrift` compared a fresh redeploy against a frozen snapshot — a warning that could never converge. Reproduced on two projects. Provenance: `74bae50f4` deleted all 16 from both trees and `ec0e9e257` authored the list the same day; `697a6e2c7` (PR #709, "Wave 1 Bundle — Skill Consolidation Stage 1") revived three as consolidation targets the next day, and `git log -S"moai-domain-backend" -- internal/cli/update_archive.go` returns exactly one commit — the list was never edited after the day it was written. The template package DID notice (`internal/template/skills_removal_test.go` was narrowed 16 → 9 with an in-source note naming these three as still present in the template tree); the parallel correction to the CLI package never happened, and `SPEC-V3R6-UPDATE-ARCHIVE-CONTRACT-001` met the identical symptom, diagnosed it as unwired `--force`, and declared the list explicitly out of scope. **M1**: `legacySkillIDs` 16 → 13, with a doc comment explaining why the three are absent and pointing at the guard. **M2**: new `TestLegacySkillIDsNotEmbedded` (`internal/cli/update_archive_guard_test.go`) asserts the list stays disjoint from `template.EmbeddedMoaiSkillNames()` — the first check comparing the list against the real embedded manifest rather than against itself (all six pre-existing tests touching `legacySkillIDs` seed synthetic fixtures FROM the list and assert against it, so they pass whatever it contains). Falsified before being trusted (REQ-LSL-008): under a `go test -overlay` re-adding one live ID the guard produced exactly 1 anchored `--- FAIL` naming the injected ID, with the real source verifiably untouched; a pure `classifyManifest` function factors the comparison so the degradation branches stay reachable, and manifest read-error / empty-set both `t.Skip` rather than pass, because a silent pass on an unavailable manifest is a vacuous green. **M3**: untracked the three wrongly-committed `v2.16` archive `SKILL.md` files — snapshots of the post-revival consolidated skills, NOT the deleted legacy bodies (the real legacy content had `references/` and `modules/`, recoverable only from `74bae50f4^`); the four genuine entries (`moai-framework-electron`, `moai-platform-auth`, `moai-platform-chrome-extension`, `moai-platform-deployment`) are preserved byte-unchanged, and no `.gitignore` rule was added (deliberately out of scope). **M4**: `archiveLegacySkills` now accumulates per-entry failures and returns `errors.Join(...)` instead of returning at the first error — all three former in-loop `return archived, fmt.Errorf(...)` sites append and `continue`, where the `continue` is load-bearing rather than incidental, because the two drift-backup sites sit ABOVE `archiveSkill` in the loop body and a bare append would fall through into `os.Rename` for an entry whose backup parent could not be created; the `total:` summary is now unconditional, `archived` still counts successes only, and the `archive: ` / `total: %d skills archived` output literals are unchanged. **Also fixed**: `TestDryRunArchive` (`internal/cli/update_dry_run_test.go`), a 7th consumer the plan-phase inventory missed because it hard-coded the three IDs as string literals instead of referencing `legacySkillIDs` — invisible to an identifier grep, and therefore also missed by `AC-LSL-006`'s `-run` selector; it broke on the first implementation commit and was fixed by deriving the fixture from `legacySkillIDs[:3]`, removing the duplication that hid it. Six further literal uses in `migrate_restore_skill_test.go` / `update_archive_test.go` are NOT defects (`archiveSkill` / `restoreSkill` take the ID as a parameter and never consult the list) and were left untouched per scope discipline. **AC corrected in-flight against measurement**: `AC-LSL-006(a-i)` expected 19 parent PASS lines; the measured value is 20, because M4 adds `TestArchiveLegacySkills_ContinuesAfterFailure`, whose name matches the AC's own `TestArchiveLegacySkills_` selector prefix — verified as an addition, not a substitution, by enumerating all 19 pre-existing parent tests by name in the run. The run-phase code + tests landed in PR #1260 (merge commit `005d800af`, 16/16 ACs PASS, CI 26 checks = 20 SUCCESS / 6 SKIPPED / 0 FAILURE, `go test ./internal/... ./pkg/... ./cmd/...` exit 0 with 104 ok and 0 build-failed, `golangci-lint run ./internal/cli/...` 0 findings, `gofmt -l` clean, `go build` exit 0 on darwin + `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64` + `GOOS=linux GOARCH=amd64`); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. §25 template neutrality N/A (`internal/cli` Go sources, SPEC artifacts, and archive deletions only — `git show --name-only 005d800af | grep -c '^internal/template/templates/'` → 0, no template mirror touched). Known follow-ups, out of scope here: (a) `moai migrate restore-skill --force` would overwrite a live skill with the stale archive (`migrate_restore_skill.go:68-83` removes the target then copies the archive over it); (b) wrong archives already created in downstream user projects are not cleaned up; (c) `moai-meta-harness` retirement — its `SKILL.md` self-declares DEPRECATED while rules and tests still reference it (unrelated, surfaced during the same investigation). - **[SPEC-CLIFIX-LINTER-STALE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-CLIFIX-LINTER-STALE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). - **[SPEC-UPDATE-REINSTALL-LOOP-002](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-REINSTALL-LOOP-002/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Ended the release-user `moai update` clean-reinstall loop reported in issue #1243, where a v3 project re-entered the destructive v2→v3 reinstall on every run and never converged (Tier M, P1 high, plan-audit iter3 PASS 0.88, 20/20 ACs PASS). Five distinct defects were fixed as one behaviour-coherent chain. **Defect 1 — literal version test (M1, `internal/cli/v2_detection.go`)**: `probeVersionSignal` classified the version signal with `strings.HasPrefix(v, "v3.")`, so a released `3.0.1` / `3.0.1-rc13` / `v3.0.0+build.5` string never matched the v3 override and the project read as v2. A new `normalizeVersionMajor` helper now trims whitespace, strips at most one leading `v`/`V`, and parses the leading digit run as the major — `major >= 3` is the v3 negative-override, `major == 2` **with a lowercase `v` prefix** is Signal 1 positive, and everything else is Signal 1 negative with no override. Prerelease and build suffixes classify by major alone. The lowercase-only prefix scope is deliberate: bare `2.x` and uppercase `V2.x` did not match the pre-change literal either, and making either positive would flip a residue-free project from `IsV2=false` to `IsV2=true` — the widening NFR-RIL2-001 forbids without exception. A file-level parse error stays Signal 1 positive (a malformed `system.yaml` is more likely a partial v2 migration), but a well-formed file carrying an unrecognized version string is the separate negative case, so a digit-parse failure never widens the destructive path. **Defect 2 — PRESERVE ∩ deprecated net-zero removal (M2, `internal/cli/update_preserve_inventory.go`)**: `preserveInventoryRoots` (`.moai/specs`, `.moai/project`, `.claude/commands`) intersects `defs.DeprecatedPaths` in 9 entries (8 `.claude/commands/agency/*.md` + `.moai/project/brand`). `runCleanReinstall` snapshotted those paths into the PRESERVE inventory (Step 2), deleted them (Step 4), then restored them (Step 6) — a net-zero removal that left the "deprecated path present" v2 signal armed and re-armed the next `moai update`, while the log still reported a positive removal count because the post-REMOVE re-scan runs before Step 6 undoes it. `buildPreserveInventory` now drops every entry equal to or nested under a `DeprecatedPaths` entry via a new `isUnderDeprecatedPath` predicate; matching is slash-normalized (windows parity) and requires an explicit separator boundary, so `.moai/project/brandX` is NOT excluded by `.moai/project/brand` — the bug a bare `strings.HasPrefix` would introduce. The filter is applied AFTER both inventory sources are merged, so it covers the user-owned scan as well as the preserve-root walk. **Defect 5 — deletion without backup (M2, `internal/cli/update_clean_install.go`, same commit as Defect 2 by necessity)**: Step 4 called `os.RemoveAll` with no backup, contradicting the file header. Removing the Step 6 resurrection without adding a backup would have converted a net-zero no-op into unbacked-up deletion of user-authored `.claude/commands/agency/*.md`, so both halves land together. Step 4 now backs up every path it is about to delete, all-or-nothing, aborting before ANY removal on failure with a grep-able `DEPRECATED_BACKUP_FAILED` sentinel naming the unprotected path; a new `expandDeprecatedBackupTargets` expands directory entries (`.agency`, `.moai/project/brand`) into contained files, because `scanDeprecatedPaths` returns directories while `backupDeprecatedPaths` copies file contents and errors on a directory. The manifest manager is hoisted from Step 5 to Step 4 (the backup classifies against the deployed hash), so a manifest load failure now aborts while the tree is still intact. Removal count remains derived from the filesystem diff, never from the planned-list length. **Defect 3 — residue never cleaned on v3-confirmed projects (M3, new `internal/cli/update_residue_cleanup.go`)**: the v3 override short-circuits Signals 2/3 unconditionally, so a tree carrying full v2 residue but a v3 version string resolves `IsV2=false` and is never cleaned — and M1's normalization made `V3VersionConfirmed` true for the *entire released population*, turning that override from a rare edge into the default path and converting #1243's "loops forever" into "residue is never cleaned". The override's **consequence** is narrowed rather than the override itself: a v3-confirmed project still must not enter the destructive full reinstall, but one carrying residue is routed to `runV3ResidueCleanup` — back up, remove, and let the existing `.agency/` migration pre-step run. It takes no `template.Deployer` at all: no PRESERVE snapshot, no tree wipe, no forced redeploy. The sweep operates on the **pre-migration** residue snapshot, because the migration copies `.agency/` to `.agency.archived/` and leaves both in place while `.agency.archived` is itself a `DeprecatedPaths` entry — a sweep scanning afterwards would delete the archive the migration had just written. The `isMoAIProject` marker gate is checked inside the helper rather than only at the call site, so it holds for every caller, and removal is confirmed by post-`RemoveAll` `Lstat` absence rather than by the planned list. The `update.go` block is EXTENDED, not replaced — `runAgencyMigrationAdapter` keeps firing under its existing conditions and is not gated behind the sweep's outcome. **Defect 4 — `--dry-run` unreachable (M4, `internal/cli/update.go`)**: `runUpdate` returned from its `--dry-run` branch before the v2-detection block, so `runCleanReinstall`'s dry-run renderer and `runV3ResidueCleanup`'s `dryRun` branch were both dead from the CLI — `moai update --dry-run` on a v2 tree showed only the legacy-skill archive summary and no plan at all. A new `emitDryRunReinstallPlan` computes the v2 fingerprint inside the dry-run branch and dispatches to the renderers that already existed (reaching them rather than writing parallel ones); a detection failure degrades to a warning and no plan, matching the non-dry-run block, because a dry run must never fail the command. The early return itself does **not** move — it stays above the deny-rule migration whose `stripRetiredV2DenyEntries` call rewrites `.claude/settings.json`, since relocating it past that block would make a dry run write to disk. **Known behaviour, accepted rather than silently fixed**: for a tree carrying `.agency/`, the sweep converges in more than one run (`.agency.archived` is itself a deprecated entry and the migration copies rather than moves) — bounded and terminating, unlike the #1243 net-zero loop, and recorded in the REQ text with its cause rather than left as a surprise. **In-flight AC correction**: `AC-RIL2-014`'s first command was satisfiable by a *comment alone* — the literal grep matched anywhere in the file, so a fixture whose `permissions.deny` was empty and whose only occurrence sat in a comment passed; a comment-stripping filter was added as the floor and a runtime assertion on the parsed `settings.json` as the ceiling, with the residual trailing-comment gap documented rather than hidden. The run-phase code + tests landed in PR #1261 (squash-merge commit `beeb0ebc2`, 20/20 ACs PASS, `go test ./...` 106 ok / 0 FAIL, `golangci-lint run` `0 issues.` identical to baseline, darwin + `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64` builds exit 0, `internal/cli` coverage 75.8%); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. §25 template neutrality N/A (`internal/cli` Go sources + SPEC artifacts only — `git show --name-only beeb0ebc2 | grep -c '^internal/template/templates/'` → 0). Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Preserved YAML comments, key order, scalar style, and indent across `moai update`'s 3-way config merge — the **third independent defect** isolated by issue **#1243** (distinct from `SPEC-UPDATE-REINSTALL-LOOP-002` above, which closed the clean-reinstall-loop defect and also cites #1243; this entry closes the separate comment/order/quoting-preservation defect, not the loop). Tier M, P1 high, plan-audit iter-2 PASS 1.00 (skip-eligible), 23/23 ACs PASS, coverage 89.6% (≥ 88.9% baseline), cross-platform build clean (darwin + `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64` exit 0), `golangci-lint run` 0 issues. **Mechanism (`internal/cli/update/backup`)**: the prior `MergeYAML3Way` / `DeepMerge3Way` decoded YAML into `map[string]any`, merged the maps, and re-emitted via `yaml.Marshal` — a round-trip that destroyed everything but values (comments dropped, keys alphabetized, scalar style reflowed `"1h"` → `1h`, indent 2 → 4). The merge was rebuilt on the `*yaml.Node` tree (`node_merge.go`, new): `DeepMerge3Way` now returns `*yaml.Node`, `deepMerge3WayTo` walks the node tree and preserves `HeadComment` / `LineComment` / `FootComment`, key insertion order, scalar `Style` (quoted vs plain), and the encoder pins `enc.SetIndent(2)` (REQ-UYP-004) so emitted files keep the project's 2-space indent. **Old-only-key policy reversal**: a user-added key absent from the new template was previously dropped silently; it is now preserved AND reported on stderr as a retained-key advisory (REQ-UYP-007), so users see what was kept instead of losing customizations without trace. **Indent reflow is intentional**: 22 of 30 section templates were already 2-space so they re-emit byte-identical; the remaining 8 (previously 4-space in the template) are reflowed to the pinned 2-space on next `moai update` — this is the REQ-UYP-004 contract, not a regression. **Decision D5 deferred**: `SaveTemplateDefaults` base-derivation provenance is NOT fixed inline and is the next session's plan-phase target under `SPEC-UPDATE-TEMPLATE-BASE-SNAPSHOT-001` (directory not yet filed — recorded as a known gap in `progress.md` §E.4, not a failure; `acceptance.md` §D.4 forward-looking check marks it NOT-yet-filed). The run-phase code + tests landed in commit `ddd86602b` (`fix(SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE-001): preserve YAML comments/order/quoting via node-tree merge (#1243)`, 23/23 ACs PASS, TDD RED-then-GREEN falsifiability captured, subagent-boundary grep clean, §25 template neutrality verified — `grep -rn 'SPEC-UPDATE-YAML-PRESERVE\|REQ-UYP-' internal/template/templates/` → 0 matches); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-*` placeholder in this commit (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3; Route B squash merge means the final SHA is the merged-to-main SHA, known only after PR merge) and backfilled in a follow-up commit after the single run+sync+close PR merges. README / docs-site unchanged — this is an internal merge-mechanism correctness fix, not a user-facing feature (no doc was actively wrong). Route B (PR-mandatory, single-PR strategy: run+sync+close carried by one PR, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-PIPELINE-FANOUT-ACTIVATION-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-PIPELINE-FANOUT-ACTIVATION-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Activated the ten plan/run/sync pipeline fan-out sites that were written but never reachable in practice, and resolved a re-audit-scope contradiction inside `plan-auditor` (Tier M, 10 REQ / 15 AC, all 15 PASS). Documentation-only: zero Go source files modified. **The load-bearing finding** is that a fan-out site is unreachable when BOTH (a) it is phrased as a discretionary `MAY`, and (b) it lives in a sub-skill the orchestrator only `Read`s *after* it has already entered the phase serially — by the time the site is visible, the serial path is the one already running. Fixing only (a) leaves seven of the ten still invisible; fixing only (b) leaves them visible but optional. Both halves therefore land together. **M1.1 — Fan-Out Index** (`.claude/skills/moai/workflows/plan.md`, `run.md`, `sync.md` + their template mirrors): each of the three phase routers gains a `## Fan-Out Index` table listing every fan-out in its phase with a stable ID (`FO-PLAN-1..2`, `FO-RUN-1..4`, `FO-SYNC-1..4`), its trigger condition, the sub-skill file that owns it, and what is parallelised — so the orchestrator learns a fan-out exists from the always-read router rather than from a file it has not opened yet. **M1.2 — discretionary → conditional** (the six sub-skill / router sites listed below): all ten sites move from `the orchestrator MAY …` to `the orchestrator shall …` under a bolded GEARS modifier (`**Where**` / `**While**` / `**When**`) on the same line, so the obligation is never condition-free — every promoted site remains gated on its own trigger, and each site's fail-open fallback ("the scan runs as one pass with identical output — no error, no warning") is preserved. Sites: `plan.md` FO-PLAN-1 (research fan-out), `plan/spec-assembly.md` FO-PLAN-2 (Phase 11 review lenses), `run/phase-execution.md` FO-RUN-1 (Phase 1.8 MX context-map shards), `run/task-decomposition.md` FO-RUN-2 / FO-RUN-3 / FO-RUN-4 (RED-stage drafter pool, Phase 13/16/17 quality band, Phase 18 MX scan shards), `sync.md` FO-SYNC-1 (Phase 7 quality check), `sync/quality-gates-quality.md` FO-SYNC-2 / FO-SYNC-3 (Phase 9 MX shards, Phase 10 test drafting), `sync/doc-execution.md` FO-SYNC-4 (Phase 12 five-drafter / one-applier structure). **Scope addition, disclosed rather than absorbed silently**: FO-SYNC-2 in `quality-gates-quality.md` had *no* fallback clause at all before this change — promoting it to `shall` without one would have created the first unconditional obligation in the set, so a fallback was ADDED there. That is one clause beyond the original M1.2 statement, and it is recorded here for the same reason it is recorded in the SPEC: an addition that makes a promotion safe is still an addition. **The read/write boundary is unchanged by every promotion** — each fan-out remains read-only (drafters and shards return text and write nothing), tag and document *application* stays single-writer, blocking P1/P2 determinations are still made on the merged result so no shard can clear a gate alone, and the binding verdicts stay with `plan-auditor` / `sync-auditor` rather than with any fanned-out judge. **M1.3 — re-audit-scope contradiction** (`.claude/agents/moai/plan-auditor.md`): the retry-loop section said iteration 2+ is a "full audit PLUS regression check", while the same file's earlier clause and `run/phase-execution.md` both describe a defect-delta re-audit. Iteration 2+ is now stated as a defect-delta re-audit plus a regression check over the prior iteration's defects, agreeing with both. **Verdict authority is explicitly unchanged** — the new text states that the delta scope reduces re-audit cost only and never permits an orchestrator self-assessment to substitute for an auditor verdict. **M1.4 — per-tier REQ/AC budget** (`.claude/rules/moai/workflow/spec-workflow.md` § SPEC Complexity Tier): the tier table gains requirement and acceptance-criterion ceilings — S 8, M 16, L 25 — applying **independently** to each count rather than to their sum, so a Tier M SPEC may carry up to 16 requirements AND up to 16 acceptance criteria. Exceeding a ceiling is stated as a signal to tier up or split, not to relax the budget. **M1.5 — cascade**: the `plan-auditor` catalog hash was regenerated (`internal/template/catalog.yaml`), and `run.md` hit its hard 200-line entry-router ceiling (`TestEntryRouterLOCCeiling`) at 208 once its four-row Fan-Out Index landed — a constraint the plan's risk table did not anticipate. Resolved by relocating the two Phase 4 operational-entry paragraphs (Mode 4 research fan-out, Mode 6 launch procedure) **verbatim** into `run/phase-execution.md`, the sub-skill that already owns Phase 4; both files were inside the SPEC's declared envelope. **The relocation was not loss-free, and the earlier claim that it was is corrected here**: the two Mode paragraphs did move verbatim, but the index table's one-sentence rationale — that the run-phase fan-out sites live in sub-skills read on demand, which is why they are enumerated in the always-read router — did not survive the trim (`grep -c "on demand"` now returns 1 in `plan.md`, 1 in `sync.md`, 0 in `run.md`). The table itself, which is what REQ-PFA-001 mandates and what actually resolves the reachability problem, is present in all three routers, and no acceptance criterion covers the rationale sentence; the residue is that `run.md` alone states the index without stating why. Restoring it requires first freeing a line, since `run.md` now sits at exactly 200 of its 200-line ceiling — recorded as a follow-up rather than done here, because trimming an unrelated line is outside this SPEC's envelope. **§25 template neutrality**: all ten local edits are mirrored under `internal/template/templates/`, and the four pre-existing intentional local-vs-template divergences were verified unchanged (`1/0` on each) — a blind copy would have flattened them. Two adjacent defects were deliberately left alone and named rather than fixed opportunistically: the stale `exit 2` hook description in the local `sync.md`, and a pre-existing neutrality leak in the mirrored `plan-auditor.md`. The run-phase edits landed in seven commits (`bf46ff163..a95369b63`, 23 files, +217/−42, 15/15 ACs PASS, `go build ./...` and `go vet ./...` exit 0, `go test ./...` zero FAIL, `moai spec lint` clean, `TestEntryRouterLOCCeiling` PASS); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional doctrine or template edits. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's `enforce_admins: true` branch-protection override). 🗿 MoAI

- **[SPEC-ENVKEY-ANTHROPIC-SSOT-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-ENVKEY-ANTHROPIC-SSOT-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). Single-sourced the `ANTHROPIC_*` environment-variable **name** family through `internal/config/envkeys.go`, closing the follow-up named in SPEC-CLIFIX-HYGIENE-001's own CHANGELOG entry (Tier M, P2, plan-audit iteration 2 after an initial FAIL 0.72). No runtime behaviour changes: this is a mechanical name-indirection refactor, and every constant's value is byte-identical to the literal it replaces. **The load-bearing finding (§A.2)** is that the pre-existing gate `TestNoBareGLMEnvVarLiteralsInCLIProduction` (`internal/cli/glm_env_parity_test.go:66`) carried two independent defects making it structurally incapable of covering this family: its `banned` slice held only three `CLAUDE_CODE_*` names, and its walk root was `os.Getwd()`, which under `go test` resolves to the **package directory** `internal/cli` rather than the repository root — so of the 83 bare literals measured at base `76d9a8f3b`, only 46 were reachable and **37 (45%) were structurally invisible**. Naively reusing that pattern would have shipped a guard that silently passes on nearly half the surface, so guard repair was treated as a first-class deliverable rather than a by-product. **M1 — SSOT constants**: `envkeys.go` goes 6 → 9 ANTHROPIC constants, adding `EnvAnthropicAPIKey` and `EnvAnthropicDefaultFableModel` (two names in active production use that had **no constant at all**) plus `EnvAnthropicPrefix` for the bare namespace prefix `ANTHROPIC_`, doc-commented as a prefix rather than a variable name; the guard matches on exact literal equality (`bannedSet[val]`), not substring containment, so banning `"ANTHROPIC_"` does not collide with `"ANTHROPIC_API_KEY"`. **M2 — new repo-root-scoped guard**: `internal/config/anthropic_env_ssot_test.go` / `TestNoBareAnthropicEnvVarLiteralsInProduction`, hosted beside the SSOT it enforces, resolving its walk root via `findProjectRoot(t)` and scanning `internal/` + `pkg/` + `cmd/` while excluding `_test.go` files and `internal/config/envkeys.go` itself (a new hazard created by the widening — a repo-root walk otherwise flags the very constant definitions it exists to enforce). Per OQ-4 the existing CLI-scoped test is left intact rather than widened: its identifier asserts CLI scope, it is owned by an already-closed SPEC, and a separate test gives this family an independent RED/GREEN signal. **M3–M5 — transition of all 83 literals to 0**, in a measured RED-window trajectory (83 → 37 after `internal/cli` → 8 after `internal/hook` → 0): `internal/cli/glm.go` 32, `internal/hook/session_end.go` 12, `internal/hook/glm_tmux.go` 9, `internal/hook/session_start.go` 8, `internal/cli/launcher.go` 7, `internal/cli/settings.go` 6, `internal/tmux/cg_detect.go` 4, `internal/statusline/metrics.go` 3, `internal/sandbox/env.go` 1, `internal/cli/worktree/tmux_integration.go` 1 (the prefix site). Behaviour preservation rests on a **forward attestation** rather than an assertion: applying the same value-preserving mapping to the base source of all 10 files and gofmt-comparing against the working tree reports `IDENTICAL` for all 10, so no wrong-but-compiling constant was substituted. **M6 — falsifiability proof**, because a guard that has never been observed to fail is not evidence: all **9/9** banned names were planted in turn and each produced a non-zero guard exit, including the three with zero natural offenders (`ANTHROPIC_`, `ANTHROPIC_DEFAULT_FABLE_MODEL`, `ANTHROPIC_REASONING_EFFORT`) that no other AC exercises; one plant per scan root (`internal/sandbox/env.go`, `cmd/moai/main.go`, `pkg/version/version.go`) proves each root independently, since `pkg/` and `cmd/` hold zero natural literals and their coverage would otherwise be asserted and never proven; and a sibling plant in `internal/config/log.go` was detected, proving the `envkeys.go` exclusion is an exact-path match rather than a package-wide exemption. Total residual diff after every plant-and-revert loop: 0 lines. **Out of scope, recorded so a later reader does not "fix" it by mistake**: the 291 `ANTHROPIC_*` occurrences in `_test.go` files (a declared hardcoding-allowed zone per `CLAUDE.local.md §14`, verified unchanged at 291) and the 12 references under `internal/template/templates/` (governed by template neutrality; the SPEC's merge commit changed 0 template files). **Known residual gap (follow-up candidate):** the three `CLAUDE_CODE_*` names guarded by the CLI-scoped test remain unguarded outside `internal/cli/` — deliberately excluded here to keep this SPEC's blast radius to one env-var family. The run-phase code + guard + SPEC artifacts landed in PR #1254 (merge commit `a89e875e2`, 15/15 ACs PASS, 26 CI rollup entries: 20 SUCCESS / 6 SKIPPED / 0 FAILURE, linux+windows cross-build exit 0, `go vet` exit 0, `golangci-lint` `0 issues.` identical to the pre-M3 baseline); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. §25 template neutrality N/A (Go sources under `internal/` only; the SPEC merge commit changed 0 template files). - **[SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-OPTIN-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-OPTIN-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **Behavior change for existing users (AC-REQ-7):** the Main-Checkout Branch-State Guard introduced by SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-001 now ships **default-OFF (opt-in)**. Users who today receive the guard ON (every distributed install) will see it go INERT after this lands — routine git commands (`git switch`, `git stash`, `git reset --hard`, `git merge`, etc.) in a single-developer primary checkout are no longer denied. Maintainers of shared multi-session checkouts (the guard's actual intent) opt back in by setting `workflow.branch_guard.enabled: true` in local-only config (see `CLAUDE.local.md §22.9`); the template default stays `enabled: false` (§25 template neutrality). Independently of the default flip, the `branchStatePatterns` regex set is refined to exclude read-only forms that were false positives — `git stash list`, `git stash show`, and `git merge-base` no longer match even when the guard is enabled, while the genuinely dangerous forms (`git switch`, `git checkout <branch>`/`-b`, `git branch`, `git reset --hard`, `git rebase`, bare/mutating `git stash` push/pop/apply/drop, actual `git merge <branch>`) remain matched. **M1** (`internal/config/types.go` + `internal/config/defaults.go`, `### Changed`): new `BranchGuardConfig{ Enabled bool }` schema gate, default `false`, wired additively BELOW the existing exemption logic. **M2** (`internal/hook/branch_guard.go`, `### Fixed` semantically): `git merge` pattern anchors on trailing whitespace (`\bgit\s+merge\s`) so `git merge-base` no longer matches; `git stash` pattern requires bare end-of-input OR a mutating subcommand (push/pop/apply/drop), excluding `stash list`/`stash show`. **M3** (`internal/hook/pre_tool.go`, `### Changed`): `checkBranchState` call-site is gated by `preToolHandler.branchGuardEnabled()` reading `Workflow.BranchGuard.Enabled` via the existing `ConfigProvider` — when disabled, NO `git rev-parse` subprocess runs (the entire branch-state path is skipped). `checkBranchState` signature is unchanged (no cascade into the parent SPEC deny-origin test). **M4** (`internal/hook/pre_tool_test.go` + `pre_tool_branch_guard_integration_test.go`, `### Tests`): parent SPEC deny-origin and fail-open tests updated to opt in via a `cfgWithBranchGuard(true)` helper; config-default-false is explicitly asserted. **M5** (`.claude/rules/moai/workflow/main-checkout-branch-guard.md` v1.1.0 → v1.2.0 on BOTH source and template sides, `### Changed`): rule mirror documents the opt-in default + the pattern refinement; source retains SPEC/REQ tokens, template side is §25-sanitized (no SPEC-ID/REQ/AC tokens). **M6** (`CLAUDE.local.md §22.9`, `### Docs`): maintainer opt-in intent + local-config procedure documented. **M7** (verification): full `go test ./...` + `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` + `golangci-lint run` + C-HRA-008 subagent-boundary grep + template-neutrality grep all clean. REQ-6 backward compatibility preserved: `MOAI_BRANCH_GUARD_EXEMPT=1` env var and `AgentType == "manager-git"` identity exemption continue to bypass the guard regardless of the config flag; fail-open norm (uncertainty → allow) is unchanged. The run-phase code + doctrine + SPEC artifacts landed in commit `214b6f6fb` (M1-M7 single commit, Tier M behavior-coherent unit); this sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition + `progress.md` §E.4 `sync_commit_sha` population + this CHANGELOG entry — no additional code changes. **Known follow-up (not in scope):** `.moai/config/local/` is not yet gitignored — the gitignore addition is a separate chore (tracked in run-phase §E.3 blocker note + `CLAUDE.local.md §22.9`); the maintainer opt-in file is NOT created in the committed tree (would violate §25 template neutrality). - **[SPEC-KANBAN-BOARD-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-KANBAN-BOARD-001/spec.md)** — sync-phase close (3-phase plan→run→sync). **The six-column kanban board now has a single-origin board state store at `.moai/state/kanban-board/` — column assignments live in exactly one file that only the `lead` role may write, replacing the rejected `column:`-in-SPEC-frontmatter design of the superseded `SPEC-KANBAN-MULTISESSION-001` (59 REQ / 61 AC, plan-audit FAIL at 0.87 for structural reasons; this SPEC carries the board model of the three-way split).** Single-origin means the store is the only writer surface: the board NEVER writes a `column`, `status`, or any other frontmatter field into a SPEC file (REQ-KB-007), so SPEC bodies stay owned by their phase agents and there is no second datum for the frontmatter-vs-board drift that killed the predecessor's design.
  - **Store + resolution extraction (M1).** The board state directory resolves as the parent of the absolute git common dir through a newly extracted `internal/core/git` checkout discriminant (ported per `SPEC-KANBAN-WORKTREE-001` REQ-KW-018's extraction disposition), and `internal/hook`'s branch_guard now consumes the extracted function instead of its private copy. The path deliberately does NOT collide with the session-record dir (`SPEC-KANBAN-RENAME-001` REQ-KR-009 — one directory name, two occupants, two resolution rules, so the board got its own `.moai/state/kanban-board/`). An absent state file IS the empty board (created on demand, REQ-KB-021 — neither "partially written" nor "unreadable" owned the absent case before); every write is atomic through `internal/atomicfile` (same-directory temp + rename), and `LoadBoard` reads through `atomicfile.ReadFile` so a racing reader never misreports `ErrBoardUnknown` from two valid states (the M3-fix4 reader half).
  - **Sole-writer enforcement through the role carrier (REQ-KB-017 / REQ-KB-025).** Only the `lead` role may write board state; a refusing reader reads the caller's DECLARED role through the REQ-KB-025 carrier — adopted ahead of `SPEC-KANBAN-BOOTSTRAP-001` so exactly one declaration contract exists in the system, consumed by name rather than re-derived (the carrier carries the key `REQ-KS-019` dispatch routing and `REQ-KS-012` quorum accounting read, while defining no part of those mechanisms).
  - **Board-wide cross-process advisory lock with stale-lock clearing (REQ-KB-013 / REQ-KB-023).** The lock refuses to clear a lock whose owner is live, and an indeterminate liveness result is treated as LIVE — the fail-safe direction, because guessing "dead" is what unlinks a live holder's lock. The Windows liveness probe uses `OpenProcess(PROCESS_QUERY_LIMITED_INFORMATION)` + `GetExitCodeProcess` with STILL_ACTIVE (259) = live (the old `proc.Signal(0)` shape is EWINDOWS on everything but Kill — constant-false, found by sync-audit); an artifact EMPTY at the first read and still empty after a 100ms grace is clearable as interrupted acquisition, closing the killed-between-create-and-write brick.
  - **Bounded recovery (REQ-KB-022).** Recovery from a partial write is one invocation, one verdict, no retry — the sanctioned recovery can never itself produce the empty board it exists to repair, and can never wedge the board on a retry loop.
  - **Card model, branch-side status read, compatibility table, admission (M2).** The six columns are a closed enumeration (`backlog → plan → run → review → sync → done`). A card's `status` is read from its worktree-observed branch by blob read — never a checkout — with the primary checkout as fallback where no branch exists and `unresolved` when no single tree resolves (REQ-KB-020, closing the every-card-inconsistent gap where the primary's copy still read `draft` while the card sat in `run`). The (column,status) compatibility table governs dispatch refusals, and WIP-2 admission runs beneath the board-wide lock.
  - **Four fix passes: three review rounds + one sync-audit round.** Review rounds: the SpecID traversal sweep moved to the anchor so a rename-in-place stays covered (M3-fix); `LC_ALL=C` pinned the containment boundary (M3-fix2); the empty-id wedge was removed by the minimal-fix operator decision — shape-conditional only, traversal sweep unchanged (M3-fix3). The sync-audit round first FAILed 0.786 on four Windows-substrate defects (constant-false liveness probe; a Windows test layer `go build` could never see was uncompilable; the interrupted-acquisition empty-lock brick; the reader half of the atomicity guarantee) and re-audited PASS 0.882 after M3-fix4 (`431ce5071`). The process change adopted from that arc: **`GOOS=windows go vet ./...` is the cross-compile gate for the test layer** — `go build` never compiles `_test.go` files, so a bare cross-build is not compile verification of tests.
  - 25 requirements / 25 acceptance criteria (both at the Tier L ceiling), all PASS, verified against the run-phase implementation at HEAD `81e9fd41f` of worktree `~/.moai/worktrees/kanban-board` (per-milestone commits M1 `dc03459bf` → `5c2a942d1` → `f0786d16f` → M2 `4ae37e617` → `dba5af978` → M3 `b12e891ad` → fix passes `92065a1e4` → `f0ca483d0` → `640321877` → `431ce5071` + SHA backfill `81e9fd41f`; progress evidence §E.2/§E.3). Verification (kanban env unset): full suite `go test ./... -count=1` rc=0 / 0 fails; `golangci-lint run` 0 issues; host `go vet` 0; `GOOS=windows go vet ./...` rc=0; `GOOS=windows GOARCH=amd64 go build ./...` rc=0; coverage `internal/kanban` 88.0% / `internal/core/git` 86.7% (≥85% threshold). Sync-audit arc: FAIL 0.786 → re-audit PASS 0.882 (Functionality 88 / Security 88 / Craft 87 / Consistency 90, harmonic mean, Tier L threshold 0.85; report `.moai/reports/sync-audit-SPEC-KANBAN-BOARD-001.md`, local-only). Known recorded gap G1: the Windows test layer compiles under the vet gate and asserts its intended shapes but has NOT executed on a real Windows host — the first runtime execution is the release multi-os gate. README / docs-site / project-docs unchanged — measured: no user-facing surface changed (0 files under `internal/template/templates/`, no CLI, no rule/skill). The four inherited debts (kanban-dispatch.md disagreement, backlog column-vs-queue, binary lag, BOOTSTRAP adoption) and the BOOTSTRAP-bounded sole-writer seam stay open, recorded in §E.3/§E.4. This sync commit carries only the frontmatter `in-progress → implemented → completed` transition on all four SPEC artifacts + the `progress.md` §E.4 audit-ready signal + this CHANGELOG entry — zero code changes (sync is markdown-only). `sync_commit_sha` populated as `pending-backfill-sync` placeholder (self-referential-hazard workaround per `spec-frontmatter-schema.md` D3) and backfilled in a follow-up commit. - **[SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-WORKTREE-BRANCH-GUARD-001/spec.md)** — added a PreToolUse conditional-deny hook that mechanically enforces the main-checkout branch-state isolation doctrine, closing the gap between the v1.0.0 doctrine-only `main-checkout-branch-guard.md` rule and actual runtime enforcement (Tier M, 13 ACs: 11 PASS + AC-WBG-003 branch-state-layer PASS with documented Gap-G1 + AC-WBG-007 sanitized-pair PASS-WITH-DEBT). **(1) Conditional-deny hook** (`internal/hook/branch_guard.go`, new, + `internal/hook/pre_tool.go` Handle integration) — denies branch-state-changing git commands (`git switch`, `git checkout <branch>`/`-b`, `git branch -d`, `git stash`, `git rebase`, `git merge` onto the checked-out branch) when ALL three hold: primary checkout (absolute git-dir equals absolute git-common-dir), command matches the `branchStatePatterns` regex set, and invoking agent is not exempt. Sentinel deny reason prefix `BRANCH_GUARD_VIOLATION:`. The deny rides the new fast `checkBranchState` regex path (microseconds, structurally distinct from `checkBashCommand`'s `DangerousBashPatterns`), NOT the 10.58s census-P1-B quality-gate path — measured wall-time ≤ 500ms per invocation against the 5s PreToolUse budget. **(2) Discriminant** (`isPrimaryCheckout`) — `git rev-parse --path-format=absolute --git-dir` vs `--git-common-dir` on git 2.31+, falling back to `--absolute-git-dir` + cwd-normalized `--git-common-dir` on older / Apple Git. **(3) manager-git exemption** (`isExemptAgent`, identity-based NOT phase-scoped) — `HookInput.AgentType == "manager-git"` (main-thread `claude --agent manager-git`) OR `MOAI_BRANCH_GUARD_EXEMPT=1` env var (closes the sub-agent-spawn gap where Claude Code does not reliably populate `agent_type`). **(4) Fail-open with advisory** (REQ-WBG-012) — uncertainty (not a git repo, git binary missing, non-zero `git rev-parse`, absent agent identity) falls through to `allow` + stderr advisory + `.moai/logs/branch-guard-audit.log` entry, aligning with the Bash Risk-Amplifier FAIL-OPEN doctrine. **(5) Worktree allow** — git-dir ≠ git-common-dir short-circuits to `allow` regardless of command pattern (legitimate worktree branch operations never denied). **(6) CLI worktree advisory** (`internal/cli/{init,update,web}.go`) — `moai init`/`update`/`web` surface advisory wording recommending worktree isolation for branch-changing work (`moai cc -w` / `moai cg -w`). **(7) `.worktreeinclude` reconciliation** — repo-root and template copies reconciled to byte-identity; redundant `teammateMode` mention dropped from the local copy. **(8) Doctrine rule bump** (`.claude/rules/moai/workflow/main-checkout-branch-guard.md`, v1.0.0 → v1.1.0) — documents the mechanical enforcer that supersedes the doctrine-only text. **(9) Template mirror §25 sanitized-pair** — `internal/template/templates/.claude/rules/moai/workflow/main-checkout-branch-guard.md` is intentionally NOT byte-identical to the source rule: §25 forbids a SPEC-ID in the template mirror, so the mirror is §25-sanitized (SPEC-ID → generic prose) and enrolled in the `sanitizedPairPaths` registry (`internal/template/sanitized_pair_parity_test.go`) so `TestSanitizedPairParity` enforces doctrine parity while `TestTemplateNoInternalContentLeak` enforces mirror §25 cleanliness. The source retains the SPEC-ID + REQ tokens for traceability; the rule is intentionally excluded from the `workflowOptMirroredPaths` byte-parity allowlist (NOTE comment in `internal/template/rule_template_mirror_test.go`). `branch_guard.go` coverage 94.1% floor / 91.7% statement (≥85% AC §C gate); full `go test ./internal/hook/... ./internal/template/...` exit 0; `GOOS=windows` cross-build exit 0; `golangci-lint run` 0 issues. **Known debt (transparent, both documented in acceptance.md F-1 + spec.md §E)**: (a) AC-WBG-003 Gap-G1 — end-to-end `git reset --hard` allow is unsatisfiable through Handle because `git reset --hard` is pre-gated by the pre-existing `AskBashPatterns` (returns DecisionAsk before the branch-state check); the exemption is proven at the branch-state layer + via the non-ask command `git switch main`, and this is the documented Phase-D interim break (acceptance.md F-1, pending a follow-up SPEC for the orchestrator-side `MOAI_BRANCH_GUARD_EXEMPT=1` spawn contract for `Agent(manager-git, ...)` Phase D invocations); (b) AC-WBG-007 sanitized-pair (REQ-WBG-007 amended from byte-parity in commit `4764e2579` after the orchestrator's M3 premise was corrected — §25 forbids a SPEC-ID in the template mirror, making byte-parity impossible without violating `TestTemplateNoInternalContentLeak`). - **[SPEC-ASTGREP-EDIT-001](.moai/specs/SPEC-ASTGREP-EDIT-001/spec.md)** — ast-grep wiring repair and the new `moai ast-edit` write-side command (Tier L, tdd; AC matrix AC-001/002/003, 010/011/012, 020/021, 030/031, 040/041, 050/051, 060/061/062, 070). The first independent sync audit returned **FAIL (0.770 against the Tier L 0.85 threshold)** — against the certification layer, not the implementation. Five acceptance criteria named `go test -run` selectors matching **zero** tests; because `go test -run` exits 0 on a zero-match selector and prints `[no tests to run]`, those five would have certified a fully reverted implementation as passing. They were repaired (real test names, `-count=1`, PASS restated as the presence of a `--- PASS:` line) together with two further must-fix findings, each carrying falsification evidence. A re-audit then scored **0.843**, still 0.007 short, and surfaced a sixth criterion (AC-012) carrying the same unfilled-placeholder defect plus a stale "safe default" heading on REQ-AGE-002 — both repaired in a third amendment. `progress.md` § Sync-Audit Remediation records the findings with their falsification evidence, and § G.1 names every residual item the close deliberately carries rather than dropping it from the record: the `--dry` default, a pre-existing template-mirror divergence that cannot be closed without violating template neutrality, the unparsed nested `rule:` block form, and a `FilesModified` double-count. **(0) The rewrite guards never ran in CI** — `.github/workflows/` provisioned no ast-grep, so every fixture-rewrite guard, including the falsification guards added here, was skipped by `requireSG`. Investigating it found the reported "absent `sg` → silent skip" was not Ubuntu's behaviour: Ubuntu and Debian ship `/usr/bin/sg` as a newgrp alternative, which satisfies `exec.LookPath` while being an unrelated program, so the guards do not skip — they run and **fail** (reproduced by putting a non-ast-grep `sg` first on `PATH`: three FAILs). `requireSG` now confirms `sg --version` reports ast-grep, reusing the check `internal/astgrep/rule_seed_test.go` already carried for the same reason, and `ci.yml` installs `@ast-grep/cli` (pinned, because the rewrite path parses sg output) on all three runners and then asserts the resolved binary is ast-grep, failing the job rather than skipping silently. **(1) `moai ast-edit`** (new command, `internal/cli/astedit.go`) — a distinct write-side entry point registered separately from the read-only `moai ast-grep`, so a `Bash(moai ast-grep:*)` permission grant never widens into write capability; `--dry` computes and prints the `ReplaceResult` without touching any file. **`--dry` is opt-in, not the default** — a run without it rewrites files in place, which the help text states explicitly (`This command MODIFIES FILES IN PLACE. Run it with --dry first to preview the changes`). Inverting that default, or gating the write path behind an explicit `--write`, is deliberately left to a follow-up SPEC rather than decided here. Pattern mode (`--pattern`/`--rewrite`) applies via `SGAnalyzer.PatternReplace`; rule mode (no `--pattern`) applies every loaded rule's `fix:` field, with `--rule <id>` narrowing to one rule. Skipped rules are counted under two distinct notices: a rule with no `fix:` is detection-only by its author's choice, whereas a rule whose matcher the loader cannot read (a nested `rule:` block) is a loader limitation the author neither chose nor can see — reporting both as "no fix: field" would misattribute the second to the first. **(2) `sg run --rewrite` no-op fix** (`internal/astgrep/analyzer.go` `PatternReplace`) — the non-dry rewrite path was missing `--update-all`, so `sg run --rewrite` printed a diff and exited 0 without ever touching disk; the existing unit test had pinned the buggy argument string, so the gap was undetected. Fixed by adding `--update-all` and re-pinning the mock expectation to the write flag, with a fixture-rewrite assertion (not mock-arg-only) as the falsification guard. **(3) `FindRulesConfig` search-path repair** (`internal/hook/security/rules.go`) — added `.moai/config/astgrep-rules/sgconfig.{yml,yaml}` to the resolver's search list (the shipped ruleset location every default MoAI project actually has) and removed the two retired `moai-tool-ast-grep` skill paths, which could never resolve since that skill no longer exists; previously the resolver returned the empty string for every default project and the security hook's rule load silently found nothing. **(4) Shipped rule `fix:` assessment** — all 4 shipped rule files (`go-no-hardcoded-api-url`, `go-no-duplicate-coverage-threshold`, `sec-hardcoded-credential`, `sec-weak-hash-md5`, `sec-command-injection-{shell,exec}`) remain deliberately detection-only; the disposition table in `acceptance.md` § REQ-AGE-006 records an evidence-backed reason per rule (constant-name invention, compile-breaking import mismatch, secret-exposure masking, etc.) — zero `fix:` fields were added, which is a verdict, not a deferral. A known gap is recorded (not fixed): the shipped rules use ast-grep's nested `rule:` block form, which `astgrep.Rule` does not parse (only the flat `pattern:` field), so rule mode conservatively skips them rather than passing an empty pattern to `sg`. **(5) Dead skill-reference cleanup** — replaced `moai-tool-ast-grep` mentions with the surviving `moai ast-grep` / `moai ast-edit` surface across `moai-foundation-cc/SKILL.md`, `moai-workflow-ddd/SKILL.md` (including its `related-skills` frontmatter), `moai-workflow-loop/SKILL.md`, and their template mirrors. **(6) Docs** — `moai ast-grep` / `moai ast-edit` documented across all 4 docs-site locales (`docs-site/content/{en,ja,ko,zh}/cli-reference/ast-grep.md` + `_meta.yaml`); the README set does not enumerate individual CLI commands, so no README change applies. Full `go test ./...` (105 packages, 0 FAIL) + `golangci-lint run` (0 issues) + `go test ./internal/template/ -run 'Neutrality|InternalContent|Leak|Namespace'` all clean. Route B (PR-mandatory, this repo's branch-protection override); single sync commit carries the merged `in-progress → implemented → completed` 3-phase close. 🗿 MoAI
